# Architecture Deep-Dive: Supporting Modules & Key Design Decisions

Moved out of `.claude/rules/architecture.md` to keep the always-loaded instruction surface small (issue #1131). Nothing below is deleted or rewritten, only relocated and indexed: this doc is on-demand reference material, in the same category as `docs/*-guidelines.md` per `.claude/rules/architecture.md`'s own Cross-References section, not part of the always-loaded Tier 1 rules. `architecture.md` keeps a topic index for both sections below; grep this file for a topic's distinctive phrase to jump straight to its entry.

## Supporting Modules

- **`sova/adapters/`**: TaskAdapter ABC + GitHub and Jira Cloud implementations (state via `agent:` labels), factory, per-project auth via `sova/utils/gh.py` (GitHub) and httpx (Jira). `ldap_client.py` is a standalone, opt-in LDAP directory client (not a task adapter): `LdapClient` (people search, org chart traversal, manager chain, group membership, TTL cache) plus `create_ldap_client()`, which returns `None` (never raises) when `[ldap] enabled = false` or the optional `ldap3` dependency (`pip install sova[ldap]`) is missing. `TriageRole` and `CreatePRStep` are the only current consumers: both resolve `create_ldap_client(ctx.config.ldap)` and treat `None` plus every downstream failure (no VPN, no match) as a silent no-op, since the directory is a best-effort enrichment source, never a hard dependency for the pipeline. `LdapConfig.server` requires the `ldaps://` scheme unless `allow_insecure` is explicitly set (validated at config construction), and `_search_sync()` passes `ldap3.Tls(validate=ssl.CERT_REQUIRED)` so an on-path attacker cannot serve an unverified certificate. `LdapConfig.uid_attribute` (default `uid`) lets non-Red-Hat directories, e.g. Active Directory's `sAMAccountName`, use their own person-naming attribute for filters, requested attributes, and DN parsing. `TriageRole._suggest_assignee()` only auto-assigns when a component/area label resolves through `LdapConfig.team_mapping` to exactly one group member: a fuzzy `search_people()` match is always a suggestion only, since result ordering does not establish component or cost-center ownership. `suggest_ldap_reviewers()` (shared by `CreatePRStep` and the CodeRabbit throttle queue in `pr_throttle.py`, so queued PRs get the same suggestion as directly-created ones) resolves the PR author's LDAP uid via `LdapConfig.uid_mapping`, since a GitHub username and an LDAP uid (e.g. a Kerberos id) are frequently different identifiers; an unmapped user falls back to using `github_user` as the assumed LDAP uid, logging a distinguishable reason on lookup miss. `JiraAdapter._resolve_account_id()` requests several `/user/search` candidates and keeps only an exact identity match on email, email local-part, or display name, since Jira's search is prefix-based and could otherwise assign an unrelated colliding user.
- **`sova/llm/`** - Provider-agnostic LLM interface. `provider.py` defines the `LLMProvider` ABC (invoke, invoke_streaming, invoke_batch, invoke_command, normalize_model_name, check_available, list_available_models) and `create_provider` factory. `invoke()` accepts optional `system_prompt` and `max_tokens` params (both default to `None` for backward compatibility), enabling structured LLM calls through the provider abstraction. `invoke_batch()` is a concrete default (sequential fallback via `invoke()` N times) so only batch-capable providers need to override. `providers/claude_code.py` is the default implementation wrapping the Claude Code CLI. `litellm_provider.py` adds LiteLLM multi-provider integration with automatic fallback. `providers/anthropic_batch.py` implements `BatchProvider` with dual backends: Vertex AI (GCS + batchPredictionJobs via httpx + google-auth ADC) and Anthropic direct (Message Batches API via httpx). Auto-detected by `create_batch_provider()` (Vertex when `ANTHROPIC_VERTEX_PROJECT_ID` + `batch_gcs_bucket` set, Anthropic when `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` set, None otherwise). The batch provider is special-purpose (instantiated on-demand, never registered as global provider). `client.py` is a thin delegation layer with module-level `get_provider()`/`set_provider()` so existing callers (`from sova.llm import invoke`) work unchanged; `invoke_batch()` auto-detects the batch provider before falling back to the global provider's sequential default. `client.py` also wires compression into all four entry points (`invoke`, `invoke_command`, `invoke_batch`, `invoke_streaming`) immediately after `guard_prompt()`: `maybe_compress()` gates on `compression.enabled` (via `_try_load_config`, treating a `None` config as disabled), lazily imports `sova.llm.compression`, classifies a fast content-type hint via `classify_content_type()` (inspects only the first 100 chars: diff/json/code/text), and returns the prompt unchanged on any error. `invoke_command` compresses only `args` (never `command`); `invoke_batch` rebuilds each `BatchRequest` via `dataclasses.replace` so caller objects are not mutated. `system_prompt` is never compressed and the default stays OFF. `gcp_auth.py` provides `VertexTokenProvider`, the one shared Google Cloud ADC bearer-token helper for the Vertex AI REST callers (batch submission in `providers/anthropic_batch.py`, model enumeration in `litellm_provider.py`): credentials are resolved once via `google.auth.default()` and refreshed in place behind an `asyncio.Lock`, so concurrent callers on one instance share a single refresh. `google-auth` is an optional extra, so every caller must handle the `ImportError` it raises; `dashboard/services/llm_suggestion_service.py` used to hand-roll its own `_get_vertex_token()`, but #924 removed that direct Vertex REST call entirely, and a later pass moved the service onto `sova.llm.client.invoke()`, whose completion path (`LiteLLMProvider._call()`) resolves ADC itself via litellm without ever touching this module. **Model enumeration** (`LLMProvider.list_available_models(allow_probe=True)`) is a concrete ABC default returning `CURATED_MODELS`, so existing and third-party subclasses keep working with no network or subprocess work; three providers override it. `providers/anthropic_api.py` reads the account's real catalog via the SDK's `models.list()`. `litellm_provider.py` fans out across every configured backend (Vertex publisher catalog for `anthropic`/`google`/`openai`, Ollama `/api/tags`, an OpenAI-compatible `/v1/models`) through one `_fetch_json_entries()` helper that shape-checks every layer of the payload (`api_base` is operator-supplied, so a scalar body, a non-list key, or a non-dict entry must not reach a caller's `entry.get()` as an AttributeError) and absorbs any failure into an empty list. `providers/claude_code.py` has no listing endpoint at all, so it probes the curated candidates with a minimal `claude -p` turn each, bounded to one probe per `(identity, model)` per process and gated on the account being authenticated; `_probe_model()` resolves "worked" through the same `_partial_success_payload()` guard `invoke()` uses (the CLI exits nonzero on a fallback warning while still emitting a clean JSON result), so the probe can never be stricter than the call path it predicts. Unlike `invoke()`, the probe's argv adds `--safe-mode` (disables CLAUDE.md auto-discovery, hooks, MCP servers, and custom commands/agents for that one invocation; auth, model selection, and permissions still work normally, unlike `--bare`, which would additionally force API-key-only auth and break subscription/OAuth accounts) and `--tools ""` (disables every built-in tool): `bypassPermissions` mode otherwise loads whatever project hooks and MCP config sit in the inherited cwd, so enumerating models against a contributor-controlled repository (a fork PR's worktree) could execute commands with the authenticated user's privileges merely by being probed for availability, with no invoke-time guardrail to catch it since the probe never runs project code by design. `list_available_models()` also re-probes `claude auth status --json` directly (`_probe_auth()`) rather than calling `self.get_auth_details()`: that method reuses whatever `check_available()` last cached on the instance, which goes stale the moment the CLI account is switched on the same long-lived instance, and enumeration must key its cache (and any probe results) off the account active right now, not a possibly-stale prior identity. Issue #1032, PR #1105 (CodeRabbit review). All three go through `client.py:cached_enumeration()`, which single-flights per identity behind a per-identity lock and caches for its own `_ENUMERATION_TTL_SECONDS` (1800s), deliberately separate from the reactive negative cache's `_UNAVAILABLE_TTL_SECONDS` (300s) since a deployment's catalog is a stable positive fact. The load-bearing distinction is between **no enumeration source configured** (curated is this deployment's stable answer, so it is cached) and **a configured source that produced nothing** (an outage, so the compute returns `None`, curated is served without being cached, and the next call retries): pinning the fallback for the full TTL on a transient ADC expiry, a stopped Ollama daemon, or a rate-limited CLI would report a stale catalog for half an hour. `_probe_curated_models()` returns `None` rather than `[]` for exactly this reason, and `AnthropicAPIProvider._fetch_sdk_models()` reports an empty catalog the same way so all three providers agree. That retry is only real if the evidence behind it also expires, which is why `set_probe_outcome()` splits its lifetime by outcome: a working model keeps the 1800s enumeration TTL (a stable positive fact), while a failing probe falls back to the reactive 300s `_UNAVAILABLE_TTL_SECONDS`, since negative probe answers pinned for the full enumeration TTL would leave the retry with nothing left to re-probe and silently defeat it. `get_enumeration()` hands back a copy so a caller sorting or filtering the list in place cannot corrupt the cache. `LiteLLMProvider._enumeration_identity()` keys on model, `api_base`, a SHA-256 fingerprint of `OPENAI_API_KEY` (never the raw key, matching `_anthropic_api_enumeration_identity()`'s contract; folded in because `_enumerate_openai_compatible()` resolves this same credential at call time, so two accounts hitting one `api_base` must not share one cached catalog), and the Vertex project/region (those select which publisher catalog is read), not on the config section name, because every vendor-alias provider type constructs that same class. `cost.py` records cost data. `compression.py` wraps the optional `headroom-ai` package (0.3.0+) behind an import guard, providing a standalone `compress()` function that returns input unchanged when the package is missing, disabled, below `min_chars`, or on any error. `models.py` defines `LLMResult`, `StreamEvent`, `BatchRequest`, `BatchResult`, `BatchTimeoutError`, and the enumeration vocabulary: `ModelInfo` (id/family/tier/display_name/source), the `ModelFamily` StrEnum (`OPENAI_OSS` deliberately distinct from `OPENAI`, since Vertex's `openai` publisher serves only open-weight `gpt-oss*` models and collapsing the two would imply access a credential does not grant), `classify_model_family()`, `model_tier_for_id()` (a reverse map derived from `_ALIAS_TO_CURRENT_MODEL` rather than restated, so a model-ID revision cannot leave a hand-maintained tier table stale), and `CURATED_MODELS`. `errors.py` is a leaf module (imports nothing from `sova`, so any layer can use it) defining the typed failure hierarchy: `LLMError` plus `ConfigurationError`, `BillingError`, `ModelUnavailableError`, `RateLimitError`, `ProviderUnavailableError`, `LLMTimeoutError`, and `LLMInvocationError`. Every class subclasses `RuntimeError` so the existing `except RuntimeError` catches in the step layer keep working. `ConfigurationError` is a static, operator-fixable configuration fact (e.g. `CodexRuntime.spawn()` refusing an unenforceable budget cap), distinct from the transient `BillingError`/rate-limit conditions a retry or fallback-model hop might clear; `sova/core/workflow.py`'s `is_configuration_failure()` resolves it straight to a non-retryable `"failed"` outcome rather than burning retries or walking `agent.fallback_models`. `classify_error(detail)` maps a failure string to a class by case-insensitive substring match, scanned terminal-first (configuration, billing, model availability, rate limit, provider availability, timeout) so a detail naming both an exhausted budget and a 429 classifies as terminal; missing, empty, or unmatched input falls back to `LLMInvocationError` (the contract is "name this failure's category", never "was there a failure"). `classify_exception(exc)` resolves an already-typed `LLMError` to its own class, then the exception class name walking the MRO (matched by name rather than `isinstance` because `anthropic` and `litellm` are optional extras and LiteLLM re-raises upstream provider exceptions that are not anthropic classes), then a `status_code` attribute (402/404/408/429, plus 5xx as `ProviderUnavailableError`), then `classify_error(str(exc))`. All three providers raise through it: `providers/claude_code.py` classifies from the already-redacted failure detail it was building anyway (message text and the exit-1-with-valid-JSON partial-success guard are unchanged) and types the empty-output and JSON-parse faults directly as `LLMInvocationError`; `providers/anthropic_api.py` maps SDK exceptions at both the invoke and streaming raise sites; `litellm_provider.py` wraps at both `acompletion` call sites via `_as_llm_error()` so no raw SDK exception escapes the provider boundary, preferring `ProviderUnavailableError` when `_is_connection_error()` matches and keeping the transport error one hop down as `__cause__` so that cause-chain walk still resolves the fallback log reason. `is_billing_failure()` is the legacy union (`BillingError`, `ModelUnavailableError`, `RateLimitError`) that `sova/core/workflow.py` aliases as `_is_billing_failure` to drive the `billing_exhausted` fallback; the string table stays the classifier that actually fires today because steps stringify exceptions into `StepResult.error` before the workflow layer ever sees them. `is_fallback_eligible()` (every category except `BillingError` and `LLMInvocationError`, since a different model cannot fix an exhausted budget and an unclassified failure gives no reason to expect a retry to differ) gates the client-owned fallback loop in `client.py`. `_invoke_with_fallback()` walks the chain built from the resolved primary plus `agent.fallback_models`, sharing one deadline of `timeout * _DEADLINE_SAFETY_MARGIN`. Every attempt receives what is **left** of that deadline and the caller's full `max_budget_usd`, not a per-candidate slice: slicing handed the model that almost always succeeds a fraction of the configured timeout (half of it with a single fallback entry), turning slow-but-healthy develop calls into spurious timeouts. Repeat spending is prevented by category instead: an exhausted budget raises `BillingError`, which is not fallback-eligible and re-raises before another candidate is tried. CLI output formats: `--output-format json` returns `{result, total_cost_usd, usage, duration_ms, session_id}`; `--output-format stream-json` emits JSONL (`type: "assistant"`, `type: "result"`). Dashboard's `_parse_stream_line()` extracts readable text from stream events. Compression savings are recorded on `LLMResult` by `_record_compression_savings()` in `client.py`, called after every `invoke()` with the pre- and post-compression prompt strings. It uses an identity-passthrough guard (`compressed is original`): `maybe_compress()` returns the exact same string object on every no-op path (disabled, unavailable, below `min_chars`, or a Headroom error), so an `is` check reliably distinguishes "compression did not run" (both columns stay `None`) from "ran but saved nothing" (`tokens_saved = 0`). When compression did run, `tokens_saved` is estimated from the character delta (~4 chars/token, clamped at 0 for a net-zero or expanded payload) and `pre_compression_input_tokens` is derived from `result.input_tokens + tokens_saved`. `CostRecord.tokens_saved` and `CostRecord.pre_compression_input_tokens` (both nullable `Integer`, migration `034_add_compression_savings_columns.py`, idempotent via a `_column_exists()` guard) persist these values so ROI is queryable without log parsing. `dashboard/services/cost_service.py:get_summary()` sums `tokens_saved` across all `CostRecord` rows and estimates a USD figure via `_compression_savings_usd()` (looks up the configured default model's input rate from `models.py:input_rate_per_mtok()`, falling back to `0` for unknown/unconfigured models); the `/costs` page renders both as a "Compression Savings" stat card.
- **`sova/git/`**: branch/commit/push (`branch.py`), PR operations (`pr.py`), LLM rebase (`rebase.py`), worktree lifecycle (`worktree.py`). `operations.py` is a thin re-export facade. Always wrap `json.loads(result.stdout)` in try/except when parsing `gh` CLI output: even successful commands can return empty stdout in edge cases.
- **`sova/ipc/`**: `_BaseAgentProcess` (shared process delegation), `AgentProcess` (pipe-based I/O) and `FileAgentProcess` (file-based I/O for dashboard restart survival), `AgentRuntime` ABC + `ClaudeCodeRuntime`/`AiderRuntime`/`CodexRuntime` (pluggable agent backends via `create_runtime()` factory, `output_dir`/`run_label` params for file-based output, global state via `get_runtime()`/`set_runtime()`), AgentHandoff + DashboardHandoff models, notifications (desktop + Slack). Dashboard-spawned agents use file-based output (`.claude/agent-output/{run_id}.stdout/.stderr`); CLI-spawned agents use pipes. `CodexRuntime` wraps OpenAI's Codex CLI (`codex exec --json --sandbox {codex.sandbox}`), added as the subprocess foundation for epic #940; the prompt is passed after a `--` separator since `codex exec` takes it as a positional argument that would otherwise be misread as a flag if it started with a dash. `agent.runtime = "codex"` is selectable from `sova.toml`/`SOVA_AGENT_RUNTIME`/the dashboard settings select (issue #945); model and sandbox policy come from the separate `[codex]` section (`CodexConfig`: `model` empty by default, meaning the Codex CLI's own configured default is used, and `sandbox` a `Literal["read-only", "workspace-write"]` with no `danger-full-access` option, so the default spawn surface can never grant unrestricted access), passed into `create_runtime(runtime_type, codex=cfg.codex)` rather than read by `CodexRuntime` itself. `spawn()` never forwards its caller-supplied `model` argument (a Claude model id resolved from `agent.model`) to `codex exec --model`, only `codex.model`. `fallback_model` is still unsupported and only logged as a warning (tracked under epic #940, not #945). `max_budget_usd` is a harder stop as of issue #1128: Codex CLI has no mechanism to enforce a dollar cap, so `spawn()` raises `ConfigurationError` (not `BillingError`: this is a static, operator-fixable configuration fact, not a transient billing/rate-limit condition, so it must not be classified as billing or walk the model fallback chain re-raising itself once per candidate) on any non-`None` `max_budget_usd` unless `codex.allow_unenforced_budget` is set, in which case it logs `codex.budget_not_enforced` and proceeds with cost unbounded. The reviewer's own repo-context sub-call (`ReviewerRole._gather_repo_context()`) bypasses `CodexRuntime.spawn()` entirely (a direct `run_shell()` call) and requests no budget cap at all, so by `CodexRuntime`'s own rule (which only rejects a *requested* `max_budget_usd`) it needs no opt-in; it is deliberately NOT gated on `codex.allow_unenforced_budget` (an earlier revision did gate it on that flag, which silently disabled an explicitly-enabled `review.repo_context_agent` feature for any project with no other reason to touch a pipeline-only budget setting). The `cost_source == UNKNOWN` warning (`reviewer.repo_context_cost_unbudgeted`, below) remains the traceability mechanism for this sub-call's otherwise-invisible spend. Because every pipeline step calls `dispatch_command()`/`dispatch_prompt()` with `max_budget_usd=agent.max_budget - ctx.cost_usd` (always non-`None`, `agent.max_budget` being `gt=0`-validated), a project running `agent.runtime = "codex"` for a pipeline role must set `codex.allow_unenforced_budget = true` or every such spawn fails closed; the `custom`/`command:*` role path through `agent_lifecycle.py` never passes `max_budget_usd` at all and is unaffected. `CostSource.SUBSCRIPTION` (distinct from `CostSource.UNKNOWN`) and `LLMResult.model` are also populated as of #1128: `CodexRuntime.create_stream_parser()` (and `__init__`'s own convenience parser, kept in agreement with it) resolve cost source once per spawn via `_resolve_codex_cost_source(billing_mode)`, a stateless, uncached function. A set `CODEX_API_KEY` in `os.environ` always wins and means metered/API-key billing (`CostSource.UNKNOWN`, since SOVA has no Codex pricing source); its absence is deliberately *not* read as evidence of a subscription (`codex login --api-key` can store a metered key in Codex's own keyring, invisible to this env-var check), so the result stays `CostSource.UNKNOWN` there too unless the operator explicitly declares `codex.billing_mode = "subscription"` (`CodexConfig.billing_mode`, default `"unknown"`), the only path to `CostSource.SUBSCRIPTION` (a known zero-incremental-cost fact, not a pricing gap, once declared). The resolved source is passed into a fresh `CodexStreamParser(cost_source=...)`. Only the `CODEX_API_KEY` environment check is read fresh on every call; `billing_mode` itself comes from `self._config`, a snapshot taken at `create_runtime()`/`reload_runtime()` time, so a `codex.billing_mode` edit becomes visible on the next spawn only after a `reload_runtime()` call, not immediately. `create_stream_parser()` resolves this per spawn, so every real Codex spawn sees that snapshot; `CodexRuntime.__init__`'s own convenience parser (`self._parser`, used only by the diagnostics-only `parse_output()`, unused on both live Codex paths today) resolves once at construction and keeps that answer for the runtime instance's lifetime. The reviewer's own repo-context sub-call (`ReviewerRole._gather_repo_context()`) builds its `CodexStreamParser` the same way, importing `_resolve_codex_cost_source()` directly rather than duplicating the logic, so its `reviewer.repo_context_cost_unbudgeted` warning fires on the same UNKNOWN-only condition. `LLMResult.model`, once resolved, is threaded past the parser too, onto a step-scoped field rather than the run-wide one: `sova.core.agent_dispatch._dispatch_to_runtime()` sets `ctx.runtime_model`/`ctx.runtime_model_reason` for any runtime whose `honours_model_override` is False (today only Codex), deliberately never touching `ctx.resolved_model` (set from the Claude alias `agent.model` back in `AssessStep` and read by every later text-only `LLMProvider` step such as `create_pr`/`spec`/`research` to pick the model THEY ask for; overwriting it would leak e.g. `"gpt-5-codex"` into a later Claude-CLI invocation from an unrelated step in the same run). `WorkflowEngine._run_step_with_timeout` resets both `runtime_model` fields, and the runtime usage accumulators below, before every step attempt. `_dispatch_to_runtime()` also tags each such result with `LLMResult.runtime_name`, so `ctx.add_usage()` accumulates it into `ctx.runtime_usage`/`runtime_cost_usd`/`runtime_cost_source` instead of the provider path's `cost_source`, combining each provenance label with `combine_cost_sources()` rather than letting the last call overwrite it. `_update_step_execution` writes that runtime share as its own `CostRecord` under `runtime_model`/`runtime_model_reason` and the remainder under `resolved_model`/`model_selection_reason`, so a Codex-executed step's `CostRecord.model` names the model Codex actually ran without contaminating any other step's model resolution, and a step that mixes Codex dispatches with a priced provider call (`DevelopStep`) gets one record per source whatever order it made the calls in. The runtime record is written whenever a runtime dispatch was accounted, even at zero cost and zero tokens: a Codex step's cost is always `Decimal("0")`, so gating on cost alone would mean no CostRecord is ever written for it. The dashboard spawn path mirrors both halves via `AgentState.runtime_model`/token fields (set in `_parse_runtime_stream_line()`, unconditionally, even at zero cost) and `agent_db.py:_record_cost()`, gated on `last_result_cost is not None or runtime_model is not None` rather than `last_result_cost and last_result_cost > 0` (the truthy-zero form silently skipped every confirmed-zero-cost Codex run). `CodexStreamParser` separately captures `model` from a top-level `model` field on `thread.started` (persisted across the thread's turns, like `thread_id`) with a per-turn override from the same field on `turn.completed`/`turn.failed`, defensively via `_str_field()`; today's CLI may emit neither, so `create_stream_parser()` also passes `codex.model` as a third, lowest-precedence fallback (`CodexStreamParser(model=...)`). Precedence is turn, then thread, then configured: observed beats configured, so a CLI that silently substituted a different model is reported as the model it actually ran, while a run whose model SOVA itself selected is no longer recorded as `""`. `LLMResult.model` stays `""` only when `codex.model` is unset too, meaning the CLI picked its own default and SOVA genuinely cannot name it. The `turn.failed`/`error` terminal path reads `usage` through the same coercion helpers as `turn.completed` rather than reporting zero across the board: a turn that failed or was cancelled part-way still spent the tokens it had already consumed, and a failure event carrying no usage still reports 0 exactly as before. The one cost figure deliberately *not* populated for Codex is `cost_usd` itself, which stays `Decimal("0")`: `CostRecord.cost_source` (nullable `String(20)`, migration 037) is what distinguishes that confirmed zero/unpriced figure from a priced one, carried onto `AgentState.last_result_cost_source`/`ExecutionContext.runtime_cost_source`/`cost_source` and persisted by both `agent_db.py:_record_cost()` and `WorkflowEngine._update_step_execution()`, so a Codex run still reads as `$0.00` in the dashboard cost views but is no longer indistinguishable there from a genuinely free run (#1128; by-model/by-source aggregation of the column itself is #922). `parse_output()` delegates to `CodexStreamParser` (`sova/ipc/codex.py`), which maps Codex's JSONL lifecycle events onto the same `StreamEvent`/`LLMResult` models `ClaudeCodeRuntime.parse_output()` targets: agent messages and concise command/file-change progress become `content` events, `turn.completed` becomes exactly one terminal `result` carrying the thread id and token usage, `turn.failed`/`error` become one terminal `result` with `stop_reason="error"`, and reasoning items plus unknown event/item types produce nothing (logged at debug) so a future Codex schema addition cannot terminate stream processing. That guarantee depends on treating every JSON-sourced field defensively before it is used as a dict/set key or rendered as text: `event_type` is validated `isinstance(..., str)` before the `_NO_OP_EVENT_TYPES` frozenset membership check (a non-string `type` such as `[]` or `{}` would otherwise raise `TypeError` on that hash lookup, propagating out of `parse_line()`), and every `text`/`command`/`aggregated_output`/error-`message` read goes through a `_str_field()` helper rather than `str(obj.get(key, ""))`, since the latter renders an explicit JSON `null` as the literal string `"None"`. Two properties are non-obvious. (1) Every rendered string goes through `_redact_and_truncate()`, which redacts before truncating and moves the scan window's own cut onto a whitespace boundary first: truncating first could slice a secret below its pattern's minimum match width and leave a mangled fragment that no longer redacts. It tries three safe cuts in order (whitespace inside the margin region, then redact-the-window and drop the margin, then whitespace anywhere before `max_chars`), because the first alone throws away the caller's whole budget on an unbroken blob and the second alone can erase a secret-dense line entirely. Only the whitespace cuts are provably safe; the fixed 256-char margin the second drops bounds a sliced value only for patterns whose value ends the match at a fixed minimum width (widest: `github_pat_\w{82,}`). A pattern needing trailing context after an unbounded value is not bounded by it: a JWT sliced below its 10-char third segment can surface its header and payload (never the signature, which sits in the dropped tail, so the token stays unusable), and a connection-string password longer than the margin can surface its head. That residual is accepted rather than fixed, since closing it means collapsing every unbroken blob back to a whitespace cut, and `TestBoundaryMarginResidual` in `tests/test_codex_parser.py` pins it so it cannot drift silently. (2) The parser is stateful (thread id, terminal-result latch, last agent message) and `CodexRuntime` is a module-level singleton (`get_runtime()`), so the instance it holds is a single-stream convenience: it is correct for one process's output and would interleave state across concurrent Codex agents. This is now wired live (issue #944): `AgentRuntime.create_stream_parser()` is the ABC hook (default `None`, so `ClaudeCodeRuntime`/`AiderRuntime` are byte-identical to before), and `CodexRuntime` overrides it to build a brand-new `CodexStreamParser` on every call, so each spawned process gets its own instance rather than sharing the runtime singleton's state. `start_agent()`/`start_command()` (`sova/dashboard/services/agent_lifecycle.py`) call it once, immediately after `runtime.spawn()` returns, storing the result on that run's own `AgentState.stream_parser`; `_parse_stream_line()` (`sova/dashboard/services/agent_output.py`) delegates to it per line when set, and reads only the per-line `StreamEvent.result.stop_reason` to set `AgentState.stream_failure` on a terminal failure (there is no separate `terminal_result` attribute anywhere in this path: duplicate-terminal-event suppression happens entirely inside the parser's own internal latch). `create_stream_parser()` is called in its own try/except *outside* the spawn try/except: by the time it runs the subprocess is already forked, so a construction failure there falls back to `stream_parser = None` and logs a warning rather than being treated as a spawn failure (which would finalize the run as orphaned while leaving the live process untracked, with no liveness-sweep entry to ever reap it). Because the reader tails the output file on a poll interval, the terminal JSONL event is normally still unread when `process.wait()` returns; `_drain_stream_reader()` (`sova/dashboard/services/agent_finalize.py`) awaits the reader task (gated on `stream_parser is not None` so Claude/Aider finalization timing is unchanged) before `_wait_and_finalize()` reads `stream_failure`, so a Codex turn reporting `turn.failed` while still exiting 0 cannot finalize as success. The drain bound comes from a second, equally optional ABC hook, `AgentRuntime.stream_reader_drain_timeout()` (default `None`), rather than `agent_finalize.py` reading `codex.reader_drain_timeout` straight out of config: a config lookup on every agent's exit would call the blocking `load_config()` (TOML + DB) on the event loop, stalling every other concurrent agent's async I/O for its duration, the same anti-pattern `agent_progress.py`'s cached pipeline resolver exists to avoid. `CodexRuntime` overrides the hook to return `codex.reader_drain_timeout`; `start_agent()`/`start_command()` call it once, at spawn time right alongside `create_stream_parser()`, and store the result on `AgentState.stream_reader_drain_timeout`, so `_resolve_reader_drain_timeout()` reads that already-resolved value (falling back to the module constant `_READER_DRAIN_TIMEOUT`, itself derived from `CodexConfig`'s own field default rather than a second hardcoded `10.0` literal, when the spawning runtime declared no tuning of its own) with no I/O on the finalize path at all. `_drain_stream_reader()`'s `task.done()` guard also checks a reader task that had *already* crashed before the drain call started, not only one that fails during the `asyncio.wait()` inside it, so a reader crash is surfaced regardless of which side of that timing window it lands on. `LLMResult.session_id` (Codex's thread id) is recorded on `AgentState.runtime_session_id` and merged into `TaskRun.assessment_json` under a `runtime` key at finalization: diagnostics only, since #944 explicitly defers `codex exec resume`. The merge is written *before* `_finalize_task_run()`'s `_TERMINAL_STATUSES` early return, not after: the inner `sova run` normally finalizes the TaskRun itself before the wrapper process exits (the `--run-id` passthrough above), so that return is the common path and anything placed after it never records the id at all. `LLMResult.reasoning_output_tokens` is a Codex-only breakdown detail deliberately excluded from `total_tokens`, since Codex reports it as a component of `output_tokens` (OpenAI Responses API convention) and adding it would double-count. Issue #942. As of #1128 it is also no longer discarded past `codex.py`: `accumulate_llm_result()` sums it across a thread's turns, `AgentState.last_result_reasoning_tokens`/`ExecutionContext.reasoning_tokens` carry it (both `None`/unset on every non-Codex source, which never populates the field), and `CostRecord.reasoning_tokens` (nullable, migration 038) persists it from both `agent_db.py:_record_cost()` and `WorkflowEngine._update_step_execution()`. A drift-guard test (`test_runtime_literal_matches_registry_and_settings_meta` in `tests/test_ipc.py`) pins `AgentConfig.runtime`'s `Literal`, the `_RUNTIMES` registry, and `settings_meta.py`'s `agent.runtime` options to the same set. `reload_runtime(cfg)` (used by both `sova/cli/app.py` and `sova/dashboard/app.py`'s config-reload paths) replaces the old `set_runtime(create_runtime(cfg.agent.runtime))` call so the `[codex]` section is threaded through on every reload; a running agent's `AgentProcess` holds no reference to the runtime object, so the swap only affects future spawns. `POST /settings/agent-runtime/test-connection` mirrors the existing `/settings/llm/test-connection` probe (shared body extracted into `_check_component_availability()`) for an on-demand dashboard readiness check.
- **Codex gets the neutral half of the headless guardrail preamble, and `read_only` is enforced by the sandbox, not the prompt**: `_HEADLESS_PREAMBLE` (Claude Code's guardrail text: headless-mode, pipeline-boundary, literal-command-execution, worktree-conflict-recovery, no-background-waiting, plus a Claude-only CONTEXT MANAGEMENT section that references `/compact`) is split into `_HEADLESS_PREAMBLE_CORE` (provider-neutral) and `_HEADLESS_PREAMBLE_CLAUDE_TAIL` (the `/compact` section); `_HEADLESS_PREAMBLE` itself is reassembled from the two pieces plus the trailing "Execute the following instruction..." suffix, byte-identical to before the split (`ClaudeCodeRuntime.spawn()` is unaffected). `CodexRuntime.transform_prompt()` (the same ABC hook `AiderRuntime` already uses to rewrite Claude-shaped prompts) prepends `_HEADLESS_PREAMBLE_CODEX` (core + suffix, no Claude tail) to every prompt, closing the parity gap the class docstring used to document against epic #940; `spawn()` calls `transform_prompt()` before placing the prompt on `codex exec`'s argv, so the known `ps`/`pkill -f` argv-visibility hazard (see the argv-builder entry above) now covers a longer string, a residual explicitly deferred to epic #940 rather than fixed here (moving Codex prompt delivery to stdin is a separate spawn-contract change). The core preamble's COMMAND INTERPRETATION clause deliberately does not spell out a literal `sova run N`-shaped example (an earlier draft did, e.g. `sova run 42`, which would have put every Codex agent's argv inside reach of a `pkill -f "sova run"` regardless of what that agent was doing, including a `command:*` run whose own prompt never mentions sova). That only closes the static-example half of the hazard, though: a non-pipeline role (custom) still wraps its command in a real `sova run ...` invocation (`agent_lifecycle.py`'s `cmd_parts`) before handing the prompt to `CodexRuntime.spawn()`, so that literal substring still reaches argv for that role; never reap SOVA processes by matching prompt text. (The reviewer role no longer launches through `CodexRuntime`, or any `AgentRuntime`, at all: see the #1126 entry below.) `AgentRuntime.spawn()` gained a keyword-only `read_only: bool = False` parameter (mirroring how `create_stream_parser()`/`stream_reader_drain_timeout()` were introduced as optional ABC hooks): `ClaudeCodeRuntime`/`AiderRuntime` accept and ignore it (no sandbox mechanism of their own, so the flag is a no-op there, same as before this issue), while `CodexRuntime.spawn()` computes `sandbox = "read-only" if read_only else self._config.sandbox` before building argv, so it can never append a second `--sandbox` pair regardless of the configured default. **Superseded by #1126**: `start_agent()` passing `read_only=(effective_role == "reviewer")` on a `runtime.spawn()` call, and the `effective_role == "reviewer" and runtime.name == "codex"` fail-fast guard this paragraph used to document (design decision 3 of issue #946), were both removed from `agent_lifecycle.py` once the reviewer role stopped calling `runtime.spawn()` at all; see the #1126 entry below for why routing it through an `AgentRuntime` sandbox was a redundant wrapper rather than a protection it depended on. `read_only` itself remains on the `AgentRuntime.spawn()` interface (unused today, available to a future non-pipeline role), and the old role launch-path classification comment above `_PIPELINE_ROLES` in `runtime.py` (and `test_pipeline_roles_set` in `tests/test_ipc.py`) was updated to match. An opt-in, network-backed smoke test suite (`tests/runtime/test_codex_smoke.py`, `make test-codex-smoke`) exercises a real `codex` CLI against a disposable `tmp_path` git repository (never the SOVA checkout, verified via a `git rev-parse --git-common-dir` comparison in the `disposable_repo` fixture): representative edit+verify, read-only sandbox enforcement, deterministic failure propagation (an unknown model id, not real model behavior), and mid-run cancellation. Gated by both the `codex_smoke` pytest marker (declared in `pyproject.toml`, for local ergonomics/`make` filtering) and a `skipif` on `SOVA_CODEX_SMOKE=1` plus `shutil.which("codex")`, since `make test-py`/CI collect `tests/` with no `-m` deselection for this marker and the `skipif` is what actually keeps it out of the default run. Every spawn in that suite passes `output_dir`/`run_label` (a `tmp_path` sibling of the repo, so it cannot perturb the read-only test's `git status` comparison) rather than taking the pipe-based default: nothing in the test module drains an `AgentProcess`'s stdout/stderr pipes, and `codex exec --json` emits a JSON line per reasoning step, so a long turn would fill the pipe buffer, block Codex on write, and hang `await process.wait()` until the 300s timeout instead of failing with a diagnosis. Files: `sova/ipc/runtime.py`, `sova/ipc/testing.py`, `sova/dashboard/services/agent_lifecycle.py`, `tests/runtime/test_codex_smoke.py`, `pyproject.toml`, `Makefile`. Issue #946.
- **`sova/agents/`**: `RuntimeAdapter` ABC, the installation-side counterpart to `sova.ipc.runtime.AgentRuntime`: that ABC decides how to *spawn* a coding-agent process for a given backend, this one decides where that backend's workflow commands and skills live *on disk*. `ClaudeCodeAdapter` (`commands_dir()` -> `.claude/commands`, `skills_dir()` -> `.claude/skills`) is the only adapter with a real `commands_dir`; `CodexAdapter.commands_dir()` returns `None` because Codex has no slash-command concept of its own, so its workflow commands instead reach it through the provider-neutral `AGENTS.md` instruction body via `_HEADLESS_PREAMBLE_CODEX` (see the `sova/ipc/` Codex entry below), while `CodexAdapter.skills_dir()` returns `.agents/skills`, Codex's own documented skill-discovery directory, which this repo's `.agents/skills/` predates and already holds hand-authored, independently-maintained content under plain names (`testing-patterns`, `database-patterns`, etc.); rather than avoiding that directory (a prior version of this adapter targeted `.codex/skills` instead for exactly that reason), every command-derived entry there is installed under a `sova-` prefix via `RuntimeAdapter.skill_name_prefix` (empty for `ClaudeCodeAdapter`, `"sova-"` for `CodexAdapter`), so it can never collide with a plain-named pre-existing directory no matter what name it uses. A standalone, distributed skill from the shared `sova/commands/data/skills/` directory (e.g. `sova/commands/data/skills/design-taste`, `sova/commands/data/skills/issue-template`) is a different artifact class and is never prefixed: it installs under its own bare name in `.agents/skills/` too, since `issue-template` and `sova-issue-template` naming the same command-derived-looking concept would be exactly the ambiguity the prefix exists to avoid in the other direction (issue #1136). Codex skills are mechanically rendered from `sova/commands/data/commands/*.md`, not hand-authored, and every command-derived `.agents/skills/` entry is name-prefixed rather than directory-separated (issue #1123): `RuntimeAdapter.extra_skill_sources(canonical_commands_dir)` (default: empty dict) is the mechanism, and `CodexAdapter` overrides it to return `sova.commands.skill_render.render_codex_skills()`, which derives one `SKILL.md` package per canonical command (reviewed per-target substitutions in `_CODEX_SUBSTITUTIONS` rewrite `$ARGUMENTS`, SOVA template variables, and `/foo` cross-references into Codex-appropriate prose, mirroring `marketplace_export.py`'s `_SUBSTITUTIONS`; `_assert_fully_rendered()` raises rather than shipping any idiom that substitution list doesn't cover). `sova.commands.skill_render.render_self_skills()` mechanically renders this repo's own `sova/commands/data/commands/` + `sova/commands/data/skills/` into the checked-in `.agents/skills/` tree (`make skills-render`, guarded by `tests/test_skill_render_drift.py`, mirroring `self_render.py`/`test_command_render_drift.py` for `.claude/commands/`): a command-derived entry lands at `sova-<name>/`, a standalone one (`design-taste`, `design-system`, `issue-template`) lands at its own bare `<name>/`, both tracked in the same `.sova-manifest.json`, so a drift check that wants "every entry this render produces" can't rely on the `sova-` prefix alone and must read the manifest's managed-name set instead (see `TestRenderedTreeShape`/`TestNoDrift` in `tests/test_skill_render_drift.py`). `materialize_combined_skill_sources()`'s existing-content check (`_is_unmanaged_at()`) distinguishes "this bare name is already SOVA's own prior install of this same standalone skill" (checked against that manifest; proceed normally) from "this bare name is pre-existing, unmanaged, hand-authored content" (no matching managed manifest entry; skip permanently, no `force=True` override): without that distinction, a bare-named standalone skill installed a second time would mistake its own previous output for hand-authored content and refuse to ever update again. When `existing_target_dir` has no manifest at all yet (a fresh target, or one whose manifest was lost to corruption or manual deletion), there is no recorded verdict to read either way, so the check instead compares the existing on-disk content's hash against *this call's* freshly rendered candidate: a match is read as "unrecorded but unchanged" (let through, so the ordinary installer can re-adopt it into a fresh manifest with no `--force` needed), and a mismatch is treated the same as confirmed hand-authored content (skip). Reading a missing manifest as unconditional "unmanaged, skip forever" (an earlier version of this check did) would have silently and permanently dropped a SOVA-managed standalone skill from every future sync the moment its manifest was lost, with no way for even `--force` to recover it, since the entry never reaches `_update_files()`'s own already-correct no-manifest-entry recovery logic at all (issue #1136 finding). `create_runtime_adapter()` (`registry.py`) never raises: an unrecognized `agent.runtime` value, an empty string, or `"aider"` (which has no adapter of its own yet) all fall back to `ClaudeCodeAdapter` so artifact installation keeps working exactly as it did before this abstraction existed. `registry.py` also exposes `artifact_exclusion_prefixes()`, which calls every registered adapter's `commands_dir()`/`skills_dir()` against a throwaway probe path and collects a whole-directory prefix (always suffixed `/`) for each, today `.claude/commands/`, `.claude/skills/`, and `.agents/skills/`; `sova/core/steps/rearrange_commits.py`'s `_IGNORABLE_UNTRACKED_RE`/`_EXCLUDED_PATHSPECS` and `sova/core/steps/develop.py`'s `_NON_SUBSTANTIVE_RE` union that set with the hand-listed `.claude/`/`.sova/` roots, so a future adapter's own artifact directory is automatically excluded from the "did the agent leave work uncommitted?" gate checks without a second hand-edit (the #1090 failure mode: agent infrastructure churn misread as the agent's own uncommitted work). The function deliberately does NOT narrow to an adapter's `skill_name_prefix` subtree (e.g. `.agents/skills/sova-`) even though `CodexAdapter` declares one: `_mirror_runtime_skills()` (`sova/git/worktree.py`) copies an adapter's entire `skills_dir()` into a worktree, hand-authored unprefixed content (`testing-patterns`, `database-patterns`) included, so a narrower exclusion left that mirrored, unmanaged content looking like the agent's own uncommitted work the moment a worktree was created, reopening #1090 for exactly the directory this mechanism exists to protect. `skill_name_prefix` still does its own separate job wherever `materialize_combined_skill_sources()` merges a command-derived skill into that shared directory: avoiding a destructive overwrite of the unmanaged content, not gate exclusion. `CommandEntry.runtimes` (`sova/commands/catalog.py`) and `RuntimeAdapter.supports_command()` parse and check a per-command runtime restriction list, and `install_commands()`/`update_commands()`/`diff_commands()`/`reverse_diff_commands()` (`sova/commands/distribution.py`) accept an optional `adapter: RuntimeAdapter | None` keyword that filters the discovered commands through `adapter.supports_command()` before installing or diffing; every call site that passes `adapter=ClaudeCodeAdapter()` (the `.claude/commands/` install/update/diff paths in `project.py`, `commands.py`, and the dashboard's `setup.py`/`settings.py`/`settings_service.py`) resolves its target directory through `adapter.commands_dir(project_dir)` too, rather than a hardcoded `.claude/commands` literal, so a runtime-restricted command can't be installed or reported as "new" through the dashboard while the CLI already excludes it. `adapter=None` (the default, kept for any caller that hasn't opted in) preserves the pre-existing unrestricted behavior exactly. No canonical `sova/commands/data/commands/*.md` file uses `runtimes:` yet, so this is a live, tested filter with no current effect until a command author restricts one. `_parse_yaml_simple()`'s frontmatter parser normalizes `runtimes`/`inputs`/`outputs` through a shared `_normalize_str_list()` helper that accepts block-list, inline-bracket (`["a", "b"]`), and bare-scalar (`runtimes: claude-code`) spellings alike, wrapping a non-empty scalar as a single-element list; this closes a prior fail-open hole where a bare scalar's own string parse failed an `isinstance(..., list)` guard and silently collapsed to `[]` ("unrestricted"), inverting the author's intent. `sync.py:sync_runtime_skills()` is additive, not a replacement: `sova install`/`sova setup` always render into `.claude/skills/` first regardless of `agent.runtime`, and this function only mirrors the same canonical skills into whatever non-Claude runtime's directory is configured (today, only Codex's `.agents/skills/`), merging in `adapter.extra_skill_sources()` (Codex's command-derived skills) via a scratch directory first so a single `update_skills()` call produces one coherent manifest, returning `None` as a no-op when the configured runtime's skills directory resolves to the same one Claude Code's always-on install already maintains, or when the runtime declares no skills directory of its own. It always goes through `update_skills()`, never the unconditional-overwrite `install_skills()`, because `update_skills()`'s underlying `_update_files()` is conflict-aware even on a target with no manifest yet, in case a destination ever does hold pre-existing content. `_update_files()` resolves that "no manifest entry" case in three ways rather than one blanket conflict: a missing target file installs cleanly; an existing file whose content already hashes to the current canonical rendering is silently *adopted* into the manifest (this is the common recovery path after a manifest is deleted or corrupted, not unmanaged content, and must not require `--force` to clear); and only a file that both lacks a manifest entry and genuinely diverges from canonical is reported as a conflict, with `--force` the explicit override for that case alone (so `--force` can never be tricked into overwriting unmanaged content merely because its manifest entry is absent). `sync_runtime_skills()`'s `update_skills()` call also passes `prune_stale=canonical_dir.is_dir()`, the one caller that opts in at all: `_update_files()` honors that flag by additionally deleting any manifest entry absent from the new source tree altogether, but only when it is still `managed` and its on-disk content still matches the manifest hash; with `force=True`, a locally-modified retired entry is deleted too (an explicit override is read as "delete the retired copy regardless"), while without it such an entry is left alone and reported via `result.conflicts` instead. The `canonical_dir.is_dir()` guard exists because `adapter.extra_skill_sources()` (the command-derived half of the scratch tree) fails open to `{}` when the canonical `sova/commands/data/commands/` directory is missing or unreadable (a broken/partial install: it ships as `sova/commands/data/commands/` package-data since issue #1206, resolved via `sova.commands.catalog.get_canonical_dir()`, but a corrupted or incomplete install can still be missing it), while the standalone skills alone still make the scratch tree non-empty: without the guard, `update_skills()`'s own `if not files: return UpdateResult()` safety valve never fires, and `prune_stale=True` against that silently-truncated source would delete every real `sova-<name>` entry and report it as a benign "naming scheme" migration (issue #1136 finding). This exists because removing the standalone-skill `sova-` prefix (issue #1136) otherwise left a prior install's `sova-<name>/SKILL.md` on disk forever alongside the new bare `<name>/SKILL.md`, both discoverable by the runtime at once; `update_commands()`/the plain `.claude/skills/` `update_skills()` calls in `commands.py` leave the flag at its default `False`, since their source lists are filtered (`include_autonomous`, per-runtime `supports_command()`) for reasons unrelated to a file being retired upstream, and pruning against a filtered view would delete entries that are still canonical. All manifest entries touched during one `_update_files()` call are accumulated in memory and written via `sova.commands.manifest.write_manifest()` once at the end, rather than once per file via `update_manifest()` (which previously re-read and rewrote the whole manifest file on every touched entry). `report_runtime_skills_sync()` prints the mirrored result's conflicts (with the same `! name -- locally modified, skipped` wording the `.claude/` paths use) and any orphan warnings in one place, shared by both `project.py`'s install flow and `commands.py`'s `skills-update`/`skills-sync` commands, which also now thread an explicit `--force` through to the mirror instead of silently ignoring it there. `warn_orphaned_runtime_artifacts()` never deletes: switching `agent.runtime` away from Codex leaves `.agents/skills/` on disk, and this function only emits an advisory string (surfaced by `sova install`/`sova setup` and by `sova commands skills-update`/`skills-sync`) naming the stale directory so a human can decide whether to remove it; a human may have hand-edited content there. The non-default-adapter set this scans is recomputed per call from the live `ADAPTERS` registry, compared by each adapter's resolved `skills_dir()` rather than by `isinstance(..., ClaudeCodeAdapter)`, so a future adapter registered after import, or one that subclasses `ClaudeCodeAdapter` while pointing at a different directory, is still covered. `sova/git/worktree.py`'s `ensure_claude_artifacts()`/`missing_claude_artifacts()` only ever mirrored `.claude/{commands,rules,agent-memory,skills}` into a worktree, so a project that gitignores generated artifacts (the standard SOVA-installed pattern) left every worktree-spawned Codex pipeline agent with no skills at all: `_mirror_runtime_skills()` (called from `ensure_claude_artifacts()`, and its gap detected by a parallel check added to `missing_claude_artifacts()`) resolves the active adapter via `load_config(project_dir)` and mirrors its `skills_dir()` into the worktree the same tracked-and-present-safe way `.claude/` is mirrored, reusing `_tracked_paths()` (now parameterized by pathspec instead of hardcoded to `.claude`) so a branch-tracked copy in the worktree is never overwritten. Issue #1131.
- **`sova/knowledge/`**: Memory CRUD + search + promote, tier loading, persona detection, review patterns, team sharing (export/import across installations)
- **`sova/utils/`**: Cross-cutting utilities: `json.py` (robust JSON extraction from LLM responses with fence/prose handling), `logging.py` (structlog wrapper), `shell.py` (subprocess safety), `gh.py` (GitHub CLI helpers), `markdown.py` (parsing), `rtk.py` (RTK token compression integration), `permissions.py` (settings.json manipulation), `mcp_config.py` (project-scope MCP servers in `<project>/.mcp.json` plus `enabledMcpjsonServers` approval in `.claude/settings.json`; the Claude CLI ignores an `mcpServers` key inside settings.json), `formatting.py` (time/cost display), `review_markers.py` (leaf module: the `sova-addressed` PR review marker regex, the shared `SHA_RE` anchor pattern, and the `## Address Review` summary formatter used by both the address-review pipeline and the dashboard verdict scan), `network.py` (leaf module: `looks_like_network_outage()`, the one predicate for whether a failure message names a transport failure, shared by `ShellResult.is_network_unreachable`, the reliability failure taxonomy, the address-review cycle count and network self-heal eligibility), `diagnostics.py` (the sixteen environment/config checks behind `sova doctor`, each returning `DiagnosticCheck` rows, run in one canonical order by `run_all_checks()`, which wraps every one in `_guarded()` so a check that raises costs a single failed row instead of a traceback on the CLI or a blank panel on the dashboard; shared verbatim with the dashboard's health panel via `dashboard/services/health_service.py`, so the CLI and the UI can never report different checks)
- **`sova/commands/`**: command and skill distribution: catalog (discover + classify + skills dir), templates (regex rendering), manifest (SHA-256 tracking), distribution (install/update/diff with conflict detection for commands, guidelines, and skills)
- **`sova/config/`**: Pydantic Settings v2, TOML loader, DB-backed config loader (`db_loader.py`), project registry, per-request project context (`context.py`, moved from dashboard/project_context.py)
- **`sova/db/`**: SQLAlchemy 2.0 async ORM models (TaskRun, OutputLine, StepExecution, FailureRecord, CostRecord, Memory, TaskAssessmentRecord, IssueLifecycle, LifecyclePhaseRecord, WorkflowDefinition, CommandContract, PREvent, MergeQueueEntry, ProjectSetting, FeedEventRecord, BudgetOverride), session factory (SQLite default, PostgreSQL optional). See `docs/database-guidelines.md` for session patterns, JSON column gotchas, and migration conventions.
- **`sova/supervisor/`**: Supervisor-level services. `progression.py`: `TaskProgressionEngine` evaluates all active tasks on each poll cycle and produces `ProgressionDecision` objects. Deterministic state machine with 14 gate checks extracted into standalone functions in `sova/supervisor/gates/` (one file per gate: rate_limit, connectivity, memory_pressure, dependency, human_involvement, quota, ci_budget, slots, budget, already_running, repeated_failure, ownership, file_conflict, review_completed; the address-review cap moved out of this gate list into `resolve_next_action()`'s `review_budget_exhausted` rule, see "Address-review budget is a resolver state" below, and the merge-conflict gate moved into its `conflicting` rule, see "`IntegrationGatesConfig` and its three independent evaluators: REMOVED" below). `BlockReason` dataclass defined in `gates/__init__.py`, re-exported from `progression.py` for backward compatibility. GitHub API rate limit gate runs first (global, precomputed once per cycle via identity-keyed `github_quota.py` trackers); fails open if tracker unavailable. Rate limit check runs before `build_dependency_graph()` to avoid wasting API calls during cooldown. Memory pressure gate runs second (global system condition, precomputed once per cycle); fails open on psutil errors; warn/block thresholds configured via `[memory_guard]` in `sova.toml` (`enabled`, `warn_threshold_gb`, `block_threshold_gb`). Repeated failure gate (`check_repeated_failures_gate` in `sova/supervisor/gates/repeated_failure.py`) blocks SPAWN_RESEARCHER when the count of failed researcher runs since the last successful run >= `supervisor.max_researcher_failures` (default 3, 0=unlimited); prevents infinite re-spawn loops on closed or mis-labelled issues. Config: `SupervisorConfig` in `sova.toml` under `[supervisor]` (enable flag, per-phase auto flags, `require_approval`, `respect_dependencies`, `poll_interval_seconds`, `max_researcher_failures`, `max_spawns_per_cycle`, `ci_warn_minutes`, `ci_block_minutes`). `require_approval=True` (default) stores actionable progression decisions in an in-memory plan (keyed by `project_slug` via `resolve_project_slug()` for multi-project isolation) instead of executing them; the supervisor dashboard shows a "Pending Actions" panel with per-item approve/skip controls. `auto_research` defaults to `False` (previously `True`) so new installs do not spawn researchers on all triaged issues on first start. `auto_address_review` enables supervisor-level address-review spawning when a SOVA review verdict is "revise" or "block" (complements the handoff-based intra-pipeline chaining with a supervisor fallback for command-path reviews). `github_quota.py`: identity-keyed in-memory rate limit state trackers for GitHub API; records hits from the shell layer per `github_user`, provides cooldown gate for the progression engine, PR monitor, and `api_health` status for the dashboard; emits feed events on hit/recovery transitions. `get_github_quota_tracker(identity)` returns a per-identity tracker; all callers pass `github_user` from config or adapter. `track_rate_limit(result, identity)` is the shared function called by `GitHubAdapter._track_rate_limit()`, `sova/git/pr.py:_track_gh_rate_limit()`, and `sova/supervisor/pr_monitor.py:_track_gh_rate_limit_pr_monitor()` to feed the tracker from all `gh` CLI call sites. `pr_monitor.py`: `PRMonitor._poll_cycle()` checks `should_skip()` both before API calls and after `asyncio.gather()` to catch rate limits triggered mid-cycle by `_is_coderabbit_rate_limited`. `coderabbit_quota.py`: quota tracking for CodeRabbit review rate limits. `ci_budget.py`: GitHub Actions CI minutes tracking via billing API with TTL caching; identity-keyed trackers matching `github_quota.py` pattern; fail-open on API errors (zero-state budget never blocks). `dependency_graph.py`: builds a `DependencyGraph` from issue bodies (parses `## Dependencies` sections). Issues labeled `type: epic` are tracking containers. They are: (1) excluded from `get_ready_tasks()`, (2) skipped as dependencies by `assess_suitability()` (in `sova/supervisor/suitability.py`, which absorbed the old `_are_dependencies_satisfied()`) and `check_dependency_gate()` (in `sova/supervisor/gates/dependency.py`, prevents deadlock: children would block on the non-DONE epic, but the epic can only close when all children are DONE), (3) auto-closed by `auto_close_epics()` when all child issues reach DONE state. `_STATE_ACTIONS` maps each `TaskState` to the actions surfaced in the graph drawer (e.g., BACKLOG exposes Triage, TRIAGED/NEEDS_SPEC expose Run Researcher, RESEARCHED exposes Run Developer, IN_REVIEW exposes Integrate PR and Address PR). `planner.py`: `SupervisorPlanner` assembles a resource snapshot (GitHub quota, CodeRabbit quota, CI budget, agent slot occupancy, open PRs, issue counts, recent failures) and supervisor persona (#600), calls the LLM provider abstraction (`sova.llm.client.invoke` with `system_prompt` and `max_tokens`) to produce a structured `PlanResult` with reasoning, approved actions, and deferred items. The slot line reports in-use/free from `get_alive_count` (the same source the deterministic slot gate uses), not just `max`: given only a ceiling the model infers occupancy from the `in_progress` issue-label count, which goes stale whenever an agent dies without rolling its issue back, and then defers every candidate on false capacity grounds. `_poll_progression()` evaluates before planning and passes the actionable `(action, issue)` candidates to `plan()`, which renders them as an `## Available Actions This Cycle` section, because approvals are matched against that set verbatim. `apply_plan(decisions, plan)` (split out of `evaluate_all`) filters actionable decisions against the plan and can only subtract, never add/unblock, except that a plan naming no issue the engine offered this cycle is treated as describing work that is not on the table, and the deterministic decisions stand instead (an empty `plan.actions` is a deliberate hold and is honoured; a different action for an offered issue is a real disagreement the plan wins). Gated behind `supervisor.llm_planning` config (default false); returns None on provider error, timeout, or unparseable response (falls back to deterministic mode). Persona braces are escaped before `str.format()` to prevent `KeyError` on user-authored `{text}` in persona files.
- **Supervisor API caching layer**: six caches reduce GitHub GraphQL API consumption. Nearly all `gh` CLI `--json` commands use GraphQL (5000/hr limit), not REST (separately limited). Caches: (1) `coderabbit_quota.py:_sync_cache` (300s TTL, per-repo single-flight lock, `None` return on API failure prevents caching errors); (2) `pr_monitor.py:_rate_check_cache` (300s TTL, keyed by `(repo, pr_number)` tuple, halved TTL for rate-limited results); (3) `pr_service.py:_pr_cache` (120s TTL, shared across supervisor/monitor/dashboard/planner); (4) `dependency_graph.py:_graph_cache` (180s TTL, per-repo, invalidated on agent spawn and epic close, milestone-filtered builds bypass); (5) `queue_service.py:_queue_cache` (120s TTL, per-project, paginated issue list); (6) `pr.py:_find_pr_cache` (90s TTL, keyed by `repo:issue_id`, does not cache rate-limited responses). The daemon enforces a 60s minimum poll interval (`_MIN_POLL_INTERVAL`) via runtime clamping. Cache key resolution uses `adapter.repo` (GitHub) or `adapter.project_key` (Jira) for cross-adapter compatibility. The planner's `_get_open_prs()` reuses `list_open_prs_with_state()` (cache #3) instead of making its own `gh pr list` call.
- **`.claude/benchmark/`**: Development velocity benchmark infrastructure. `resolve_root.sh`: shared bash function mirroring `get_primary_worktree_root()` that resolves the main checkout path from any worktree via `git rev-parse --git-common-dir`, sourced by all other scripts so JSONL logs always write to the primary checkout (surviving worktree cleanup). `log.sh`: JSONL event logger for issue-level benchmarks. `session_start_hook.sh`: Claude Code SessionStart hook that creates `issue-{N}.jsonl` and logs `session_start` when opening a session on a benchmark branch (`feat/issue-NNN`). `session_end_hook.sh`: Claude Code Stop hook that captures session metrics (model, duration, token counts, cost) from transcript files and appends to benchmark log; self-healing (creates the log file if `session_start` didn't run). Both hooks registered in `.claude/settings.json` (SessionStart + Stop); `_copy_claude_artifacts` copies `settings.json` to SOVA-created worktrees so hooks fire there. For full coverage in Claude Code-created worktrees (EnterWorktree), add the same hooks to `~/.claude/settings.json`. Logs stored at `.claude/benchmark/issue-{N}.jsonl`, gitignored. Used for SOVA vs interactive development velocity comparison (issue #6).
- **Benchmark logs are for interactive sessions only; must not double-count SOVA's own autonomous runs**: `.claude/commands/*.md` (develop, develop-full, spec, review, review-full, review-pr, pr, address-pr, integrate-pr, health-audit) are the same command files SOVA's own pipeline invokes headlessly via `start_command()` (handoff auto-execution, supervisor autonomy) as the ones a human runs interactively by typing `/develop`, `/pr`, etc. They previously called `bash .claude/benchmark/log.sh "<event>_start/_complete" ...` inline, which meant SOVA's own autonomous command executions wrote to the same interactive-only JSONL logs that `benchmark-report.md` treats as the "interactive" half of the comparison (autonomous metrics are meant to come exclusively from the DB: `task_runs`, `step_executions`, `cost_records`). A `SOVA_AGENT_RUN=1` env guard (set by `_inject_agent_marker()` on every spawned agent, `sova/ipc/runtime.py`) made `log.sh` and both session hooks no-op for agent-spawned processes, but relying on an env-var guard across every spawn path was fragile. The inline `log.sh` calls were removed from all `.claude/commands/*.md` files entirely rather than trusting the guard; only the SessionStart/Stop hooks (which fire once per Claude Code session and already gate on `SOVA_AGENT_RUN` + branch pattern) still write to the benchmark logs. Consequence: `pr_created` and `review_start` events (previously used by `benchmark-report.md` to derive PR number/LOC/Files and review-round counts for interactive issues) are no longer emitted; those fields degrade to "N/A" per the report's existing missing-data handling. The distributable `sova/commands/data/commands/` directory (installed into other projects via `sova install`) never had these calls. Files: `.claude/commands/*.md`, `.claude/commands/benchmark-report.md`, `sova/ipc/runtime.py`.


## Key Design Decisions

- **A network outage is a first-class condition, and "could not verify" is never "verified bad"**: a 20-minute home-internet drop cost one project four failed developer runs (one of them 14 minutes and $5.65 deep, failing at its final `git push` with `ssh: connect to host github.com port 22`), two 1-second respawns of the same issues (`error connecting to api.github.com` on the first adapter call), and a silent misdiagnosis, because four layers were all blind to connectivity at once. (1) **Classification**: `sova/utils/network.py` is a leaf module (no intra-`sova` imports) whose `looks_like_network_outage()` is the single predicate for "does this text name a transport failure", consumed by `ShellResult.is_network_unreachable` (`sova/utils/shell.py`, sibling of the pre-existing `is_rate_limited`), the new `network_unreachable` bucket in `classify_failure_cause()` (`reliability_service.py`), `count_address_review_runs()` and self-heal eligibility. Its table is two-tiered for the same reason `_LLM_CONTEXT_MARKERS` is: an unambiguous phrase (`could not resolve host`, `ssh: connect to host`) matches alone, while a generic one (`operation timed out`, `connection refused`, `unreachable`) needs a remote endpoint named in the same message, so a lint step reporting a local timeout is not an outage. Errno identifiers are matched with word boundaries because Python's own `FileNotFoundError` lowercases to `fil-enotfound-error`, which a plain substring test reports as a DNS failure, i.e. as an outage rather than the missing-CLI-binary misconfiguration it actually is. The rule is checked **before** `_PROCESS_EXIT_RE` in the taxonomy, since `_build_exit_failure_message()` wraps the real cause in the `Process exited with code N` shape that regex would otherwise claim, and before the `fix_llm_*` prefixes, since those buckets exist to measure how well SOVA's own fix loop converges (#977) and an outage-caused timeout left in them is a false data point. Messages SOVA writes itself are re-read through this predicate, so `tests/test_network_predicate.py` pins that round-trip: the first draft of the address-pr message said "GitHub was unreachable", which the table did not match, silently defeating both the cycle-count exclusion and self-heal. (2) **State**: `sova/supervisor/network_health.py` clones `github_quota.py`'s shape (in-memory singleton, feed events on transition) but is **not** identity-keyed, since reachability belongs to the machine rather than a GitHub account, and answers only "is the network up right now", never "was this run network-caused", because the failures that motivated it happened inside agent *subprocesses* whose own tracker dies with the child, so using it for eligibility would silently under-detect. Fed reactively by folding `track_connectivity()` into the existing `track_rate_limit()` (all five `gh` call sites already route through it, so coverage is free) plus `_fetch_pr_fields()` in `agent_db.py`, which bypasses both the adapter and `sova/git/pr.py`; and proactively by `probe_connectivity()`, a DNS-only lookup (no quota cost, no credentials) of `api.github.com` and `api.anthropic.com` riding the existing 5s liveness sweep behind a 30s interval gate, so it needs no lifespan task and therefore no seventh entry in the `_shutdown_tasks()` cancellation contract. Resolving *any* host counts as reachable so one vendor's edge problem is not reported as the operator's internet being down; failing *every* host is decisive and skips the two-failure threshold that otherwise keeps a single blip from flapping the banner. The tracker is process-wide mutable state that gates spawning, so `tests/conftest.py` resets it per test (sibling of `_reset_log_dedup`): without that, one test simulating a transport failure would block spawns for the rest of the session and the suite's behaviour would depend on the developer's own connection. (3) **Blocking**, at two layers because they cover different paths: `check_connectivity_gate()` in `evaluate_all()` stops the supervisor ever *deciding* to spawn (placed before `build_dependency_graph()`, whose every API call would fail anyway), and `check_network_connectivity()` in `agent_validation.py` (cloned from `check_memory_pressure`'s `(block, warn)` contract, fail-open, `force=True` bypasses) guards `start_agent()`/`start_command()`, the one choke point both manual dashboard clicks and supervisor-executed spawns pass through. That function reads the tracker *before* the config, because the tracker read is in-memory while `load_config()` is a blocking TOML + DB read and nothing can need blocking when the network is up: otherwise every spawn pays a config load to learn nothing. (4) **Visibility**: `api_health` (`work_item_service.py`) gained an `unreachable` state checked before `rate_limited` (an outage outranks a throttle), rendered by the existing `_updateApiHealthBanner()`, plus a non-dismissible global banner in `base.html` driven by `GET /api/health/connectivity`, registered on the app rather than a project-scoped router, since one process-wide tracker has nothing to prefix per project, and always 200 so the banner's own fetch failing (the dashboard being unreachable) is never conflated with the network being down. Spawn-triggering JS short-circuits with a toast via `blockedByConnectivity()`; the backend gate is the real enforcement. A banner plus disabled spawn actions was chosen over a blocking modal deliberately: the dashboard stays genuinely useful offline for logs, costs, reliability and run history. Config lives in `[network_guard]` (`enabled`, `block_spawns`, `auto_resume`, `recovery_grace_seconds`, `resume_window_minutes`, `max_auto_resumes_per_hour`) with the full quadruple registration; it needs no `_RELOAD_PREFIX_MAP` entry because the pre-spawn check loads config per spawn and the daemon reloads per poll, so a mapping would print a misleading restart notice. Files: `sova/utils/network.py`, `sova/utils/shell.py`, `sova/supervisor/network_health.py`, `sova/supervisor/gates/connectivity.py`, `sova/dashboard/services/agent_validation.py`, `sova/dashboard/app.py`, `sova/dashboard/templates/base.html`. Issue: reported 2026-10-01.
- **An unverifiable outcome is recorded as `interrupted`, and an outage must not spend the address-review budget**: `_validate_address_pr()` (`agent_db.py`) verifies a push through `gh pr view` and `git fetch`, so during an outage every tier returns inconclusive and its deliberately fail-closed fallback reported `address-pr completed without pushing changes`, a confident accusation about a run whose outcome was simply unknown, and the recurring failure on the reporting project (re-running reproduced it, since the cause was the connection). `_fetch_pr_fields()` and `_check_pr_branch_pushed()` discarded the `ShellResult` entirely and returned a bare `None`, making "could not verify" indistinguishable from "verified nothing happened"; both now return `(answer, network_blocked)`, the validators return an `OutcomeValidation` NamedTuple, and `_downgrade_to_failed()` takes a `status` so a network-blocked validation lands as `interrupted` with a truthful message instead of `failed`. Fail-closed is unchanged for every other inconclusive case: only a network-blocked check gets the benefit of the doubt. `_validate_merge_command()` needed the type change only, not a semantic one: `get_pr_status()` raises on a transport failure and that path already degraded to "no accusation". **Marking the run `interrupted` is not sufficient on its own**, because `interrupted` is itself a member of `TASK_RUN_TERMINAL`, which was what the address-cycle counter (`count_address_review_runs()`, `gates/utils.py`) filtered on at the time: it therefore also excluded outage-classified runs, or two outages would have permanently exhausted a PR's `pipeline.max_address_review_cycles` budget. That filter ran in Python rather than SQL because the predicate is a substring table with a corroboration rule, not something a portable `LIKE` expresses across SQLite and PostgreSQL. (`count_address_review_runs()` and the whole DB-based counter were later deleted in favor of counting `sova-addressed` GitHub review markers directly, issue #1112; see "GitHub review markers are the sole SOVA verdict source" below, where outage exclusion became structurally unnecessary since a failed run never posts a marker.) Files: `sova/dashboard/services/agent_db.py`, `sova/dashboard/services/agent_finalize.py`.
- **Network self-heal is narrow by construction, and resumes rather than respawns**: `attempt_network_self_heal()` (`agent_recovery.py`) resumes runs that failed because of an outage once the connection has been healthy for `recovery_grace_seconds`. This is deliberately not a revival of the general auto-retry dropped in migration `020_drop_task_run_retry_columns`: eligibility requires the persisted `error_message` to **positively** identify a transport failure via `looks_like_network_outage()`, so overlapping the outage window is a filter and never evidence, and an unrelated bug that happened to fail during an outage is never resumed. It resumes through `start_agent(resume_run_id=...)`, reusing the `--resume` checkpoint machinery that already existed end to end, so the developer run that died at its final `git push` after 14 minutes keeps its completed steps; a run that completed nothing degenerates to a fresh spawn on its own, which is why there is one code path and no classifier choosing between them (`_load_checkpoint()` already drops a `worktree_path` that no longer exists, so GC during the outage needs no special handling). Bounds, each closing a specific failure mode: `termination_reason IS NULL` excludes a deliberate stop, a watchdog kill and an external signal (stronger than status alone, and the reason an automatic resume can never undo a human's stop); a newer running-or-done run for the same issue skips the candidate entirely, which is what prevents duplicating work the supervisor already redid on its own and opening a second PR; `EXISTS(resumed_from_id = id)` is the already-handled marker and a run that is *itself* a resume is never resumed again, capping every chain at one automatic follow-up without needing a column to tell automatic from human resumes (that column shape is exactly what migration 020 deleted); one resume per 5-minute tick plus an hourly per-project cap counting every resume, human ones included, since a human already retrying repeatedly is precisely when an automatic resume should hold off; and `force=False`, so the memory, connectivity, slot and issue-conflict checks all still apply to an automatic resume. Candidates are ordered by completed steps then cost, so the nearly-finished run goes first when the slot limit allows only one. Lives in the existing `_periodic_recovery_loop` (`app.py`) immediately **after** `recover_stale_runs()`, which is load-bearing: that call is what moves a zombie row into the terminal status this evaluates on. Riding that loop also inherits multi-project iteration and shutdown cancellation and works for dashboard-only users who run no supervisor daemon. `_resume_one()` resolves `slug = find_slug_for_path(project_dir)` before calling `start_agent()`, mirroring `progression.py:execute_decision()`: `attempt_network_self_heal()` runs from the background loop, not a request context, so the per-request `get_project_slug()` contextvar `start_agent()` would otherwise fall back to is never set there, and in multi-project mode every resume would land on the unpopulated `_DEFAULT_SLUG` pool regardless of which project the candidate came from. A `None` result (single-project mode, where the swept directory is never registered in the project registry) falls through to `start_agent()`'s own default resolution, which is correct there since `_DEFAULT_SLUG`'s pool is pre-seeded by `set_project_dir()`. Command roles (`command:address-pr`, `command:integrate-pr`, `reviewer`) are excluded: they have no checkpoint, and the supervisor's next poll re-proposes the action anyway. Files: `sova/dashboard/services/agent_recovery.py`, `sova/dashboard/app.py`, `sova/config/models.py`.
