- **Cancellation must never abandon a DB transaction**: SQLite holds the write lock of an abandoned connection until the owning process exits, so a task cancelled between `INSERT` and `COMMIT` makes the entire project database unwritable: runs cannot be finalized, the liveness sweep cannot reclaim dead runs, and no new agent can be registered, until the server is restarted. This is not theoretical: four agents exiting at once stranded a connection and idled the project for hours. Every writer on a cancellable path wraps its transaction in `asyncio.shield` (`OutputWriter.flush`, `ResourceWriter.flush`, `ResourceWriter.write_summary`), since `cancel_background_tasks()` cancels the agent-exit tasks with a 3 s timeout. When re-queuing buffered rows after a cancel, `asyncio.current_task().cancelling()` distinguishes "our task was cancelled" (the shielded write still completes, so re-queuing would duplicate) from "the write itself was cancelled" (nothing landed, keep the rows). Related: `synchronous=NORMAL` under WAL (see `docs/database-guidelines.md`) keeps commit windows short enough that writers do not exhaust the 30 s busy timeout in the first place. Files: `sova/core/output.py`, `sova/monitoring/writer.py`, `sova/db/session.py`.
- **A model column with no migration is invisible until every query on that table fails**: `alembic_version` records which revisions ran, never what the schema contains, so a column added to a model without a migration leaves the database stamped at head and permanently broken. `_run_migrations()` case 3 returns on string equality with the head revision, and neither the upgrade (a no-op at head) nor the self-healing fallback (`create_all` cannot alter an existing table) can repair it. Tests never catch it because they build their schema with `create_all` from the same models, so the ORM and the migration chain diverge invisibly. Five `Memory` columns (`embedding`, `retrieval_count`, `last_retrieved_at`, `archived`, `health_score`) landed this way across two commits in mid-2026 and took down the entire memory subsystem (dashboard `/api/memory`, `sova memory search`, `sova memory health`) on every existing install *and* every new one, since production replays the chain rather than calling `create_all`. Repaired by `035_repair_schema_drift`; `TestSchemaDriftGuard` in `tests/test_db.py` replays migrations onto an empty DB and asserts the ORM's tables, columns and indexes are all present, and `_find_missing_columns()` logs the offending `table.column` at startup instead of letting it surface later as an unrelated `OperationalError`. The index half of the guard matches on column set, not index name, because `001_initial_schema` named two `task_assessments` indexes differently from the model, which is harmless. Files: `sova/db/session.py`, `sova/db/migrations/versions/035_repair_schema_drift.py`, `docs/database-guidelines.md`.
- **A log line that parses as JSON is not necessarily a JSON object**: `.claude/sova.log` interleaves structlog JSON objects with raw multi-line tracebacks, and `json.loads` accepts a bare scalar, so a traceback frame consisting of a quoted string (`"no active connection"`, the argument of a `raise` spanning several source lines) parses cleanly into a `str`. `_parse_log_file()` assumed success implied a dict, called `.get()` on it, and the resulting `AttributeError` failed the whole `/api/logs` request, leaving the Logs page stuck on "Loading..." with no entries at all. Any parser reading a mixed-content log must check `isinstance(entry, dict)` after a successful parse and fall back to the plain-text path, not just catch `JSONDecodeError`. File: `sova/dashboard/services/log_service.py`.
- **An unbounded dependency upper bound (`<1.0`) lets CI silently install a release that was never tested against**: second confirmation of the pattern first seen with SQLAlchemy (PR #1081, `>=2.0,<2.1`). `fastapi>=0.115,<1.0` let a fresh `pip install -e ".[dev]"` resolve to whatever the newest 0.x release was on the day CI ran, with no lock file pinning it. FastAPI >=0.137 reworked `APIRouter.include_router()` to wrap every included sub-router in an opaque, lazily-flattened `_IncludedRouter` object instead of exposing flat `APIRoute` instances on `app.routes`; a route reached only through `include_router()` (every router in `sova/dashboard/app.py`'s `_register_api_routers()`) no longer appears as an `APIRoute` to code that walks `app.routes` directly. `tests/test_dashboard.py::TestRouterLevelCsrfCoverage` (added by #1204 to verify every mutating dashboard route carries `require_same_origin_csrf`) does exactly that walk with `isinstance(route, APIRoute)`, so under the newer FastAPI it silently matched nothing: the two tests asserting `== []` passed vacuously, and only the one asserting a specific non-empty exemption list caught the regression. A security-coverage test that can pass by finding nothing to check is the dangerous failure mode here, not the CI red itself. Root-caused by bisecting `fastapi==0.136.3` (passes) against `0.137.0` (fails) in a throwaway `python3.12 -m venv` matching CI's interpreter, since the installed version in an already-provisioned dev venv does not reflect what a fresh CI install resolves to. Fixed by pinning `fastapi>=0.115,<0.137` in `pyproject.toml`; the underlying route-walking test helper was left as-is since the pin keeps it exercising real routes. Files: `pyproject.toml`, `tests/test_dashboard.py`.
- **Python for SOVA**: unified stack for CLI, agent, dashboard. Zero-config SQLite for single dev.
- **Role-based agents**: triage, researcher, developer, reviewer with dispatcher routing
- **Gate checks between steps**: every step validates output before the next starts
- **Complexity-based timeout multipliers**: all pipeline step timeouts are multiplied by complexity tier (1.5x for COMPLEX, 2.0x for EPIC, capped at 3.0x). Applied uniformly across develop, validate, monitor_ci, and all other steps via `WorkflowEngine._step_timeout()`. See `docs/performance-guidelines.md` for details and config keys (`validation.fix_timeout`, `validation.hook_timeout`, `develop.step_timeout`).
- **Partial work preservation on timeout**: when a step exceeds its timeout, `WorkflowEngine._preserve_partial_work_on_timeout()` commits staged changes (via `git add -u`) with message `"wip: partial work from {step_name} (timeout)"`. Only tracked files are preserved; new untracked files are not committed. Sets `StepResult.partial_work=True` so the dashboard can surface this to the user. Prevents data loss when complex issues hit time limits. No-ops before `ctx.worktree_dir` is set (e.g. a timeout during `sync`/`assess`, or a role that never creates a worktree): `ctx.working_dir` falls back to `project_dir` in that window, and committing there could capture the operator's own uncommitted edits on whatever branch they have checked out in the primary checkout, so the guard commits from `ctx.worktree_dir` explicitly rather than the fallback property. Same guard applies to the SIGTERM shutdown path below, which shares this helper's underlying `commit_partial_work()`. Issue #978, PR #1100 (CodeRabbit review).
- **Ephemeral agents**: spawn, work, write handoff, die. No persistent sessions.
- **Worktree isolation**: each task gets its own git worktree (parallel-safe)
- **Adapter pattern for task sources**: swap GitHub/JIRA/Linear without touching core
- **Mandatory pipeline**: Triage -> Researcher -> Developer (enforced by Gate 3); `--force` bypasses
- **Split pipelines at role boundaries**: each role has its own step pipeline (developer: 17 steps, address-review: 10 steps, researcher: 4 steps, planner: 4 steps). A single monolithic pipeline that crosses role boundaries (develop + review + address in one sequence) breaks agent isolation and prevents independent lifecycles. The role detects which pipeline variant to run from `ctx.pr_number` alone (set by handoff). Issue state (`task.state`) is NOT used for variant detection because the dashboard may race it to `in_progress` before the role reads it.
- **Issue state ownership is human**: agents never auto-move issues to DONE. Issues stay IN_REVIEW until the human merges via `/integrate-pr`. The agent prepares the PR; the human approves and merges.
- **Handoff protocol**: JSON-based inter-agent state passing via file + DB
- **Short-lived agent model**: agents run, write handoff, exit; dashboard provides the interactive bridge
- **Markdown commands**: Claude Code loads them as slash commands, 30 commands with category frontmatter
- **Persona auto-detection**: detects project tech stack and loads relevant guidance
- **DB persistence**: TaskRun, CostRecord, StepExecution tracked in SQLite/PostgreSQL
- **Per-step token attribution**: steps accumulate usage by calling `ctx.add_usage(result)` rather than `ctx.add_cost(result.cost_usd)`, `WorkflowEngine._run_step_with_timeout()` snapshots the `ExecutionContext` counters around `step.execute()`, and the resulting `TokenUsage` delta rides on `StepResult.usage` into the `CostRecord` token columns written by `_update_step_execution()`. Recording cost alone left every engine-written `CostRecord` with empty token columns: `SUM(input_tokens)` across 3156 records was 9, because `sova/roles/triage.py` was the only caller of `record_cost()`, the sole writer that persisted usage. Consequences: no cost question could be answered with token data, and the compression columns from #897 stayed permanently NULL for the pipeline. The delta is attached on the timeout and exception paths too, since tokens burned before a failure are still billed. Both compression columns are populated: `tokens_saved` rides the `TokenUsage` delta directly, and `pre_compression_input_tokens` is derived in `_update_step_execution()` as `usage.input_tokens + usage.tokens_saved` (left `None` when `tokens_saved is None`, since compression never ran that window) rather than accumulated as its own counter: the sum is exact because `add_usage()` only ever adds a positive `result.tokens_saved` to the running total on the same call that adds `result.input_tokens`. `TokenUsage.tokens_saved` is `int | None` and `_delta_saved()` preserves the null rather than collapsing it: `None` means compression never ran, `0` means it ran and saved nothing, matching the `CostRecord` column semantics. That distinction cannot be derived from cumulative totals alone, because a step where compression never ran leaves the running total untouched and subtracts to `0`, which would persist as a real saving of nothing; `TokenUsage.compressed_calls` therefore counts compressed invocations and gates the delta. The synthetic `StepResult`s built on the timeout and exception paths carry `cost_usd` explicitly for the same reason: `_update_step_execution()` writes a `CostRecord` only when `cost_usd > 0`, so a step that spent money and then failed would otherwise drop out of cost records entirely. Files: `sova/core/context.py`, `sova/core/workflow.py`, `sova/core/steps/base.py`. Issue #971.
- **Unified TaskRun via `--run-id` passthrough**: the dashboard creates a TaskRun (with PID for process management), passes `--run-id {id}` to the subprocess, and WorkflowEngine adopts it via `_adopt_task_run()` instead of creating a second record. This eliminates the "wrapper agent" confusion on the Work page. `_adopt_task_run()` clears the `current_step="agent"` sentinel and preserves the dashboard's PID. The dashboard's `_finalize_task_run()` only updates status if not already terminal (the engine may finalize first), but always writes stream cost (more accurate). CLI runs without `--run-id` still create their own TaskRun.
- **Combined server**: `sova server start` runs dashboard + scheduler in one async process
- **Idempotent finalization**: when multiple codepaths can finalize state (e.g., WorkflowEngine completion + `_wait_and_finalize`, background sweep + manual mark-failed), guard with a status check before writing. `_finalize_task_run()` in `agent_db.py` uses `_TERMINAL_STATUSES = {"done", "failed", "rejected", "interrupted", "paused"}`: if the run is already in one of these states, status is not overwritten but cost is still updated (stream cost is more accurate). Any endpoint that sets a run's status to terminal (e.g., `mark-failed`) must also call `stop_agent(run_id=...)` to kill the subprocess, otherwise `_wait_and_finalize()` blocks forever on `await process.wait()`.
- **Stale run recovery + dismiss**: `recover_stale_runs()` queries all non-terminal TaskRuns (`status.notin_(_TERMINAL)`) on dashboard startup and marks dead-PID ones as "interrupted": not just `status == "running"`, so runs stuck in "pending", "assessing", etc. are also caught. `POST /api/agents/interrupted/dismiss` changes interrupted runs to "failed" so users can clear the banner. The "interrupted" status must be in `_TERMINAL` sets in `work_service.py`.
- **Lifespan shutdown must cancel ALL background tasks**: `_shutdown_tasks()` in `app.py` must cancel six categories of tasks: (1) per-agent I/O tasks via `cancel_background_tasks()` (reader, stderr, resource_flush, wait_and_finalize, ResourceCollector.stop()), (2) WebSocket producer tasks via `_ws_manager.cancel_all()`, (3) batch job tasks via `cancel_all_batches()`, (4) PR throttle/monitor tasks, (5) liveness sweep task, (6) merge queue monitor tasks (one per registered project, stopped via `monitor.stop()` + task cancel). The `cancel_background_tasks()` gather has a 3-second internal timeout; the outer `_shutdown_tasks()` call has a 5-second timeout via `asyncio.wait_for`. WebSocket `_broadcast` uses `asyncio.wait_for(send_json, timeout=2.0)` to prevent blocking on half-closed sockets. `_wait_and_finalize` handles `CancelledError` by calling `process.stop()` before re-raising. Files: `sova/dashboard/app.py`, `sova/dashboard/services/agent_lifecycle.py`, `sova/dashboard/routers/agents.py`, `sova/dashboard/services/batch_service.py`.
- **Subprocess isolation via `start_new_session=True`**: all agent subprocess spawns in `sova/ipc/runtime.py` (`ClaudeCodeRuntime.spawn()`, `AiderRuntime.spawn()`, `_spawn_with_file_output()`) use `start_new_session=True` to place agents in their own process group. This prevents uvicorn reload (SIGHUP/SIGTERM to the worker process group) from killing running agents. Complemented by narrowed `reload_dirs` in `sova/cli/app.py` which limits file watching to `sova/dashboard/` and `sova/cli/` only, so changes to core modules (`ipc/`, `core/`, `roles/`) do not trigger reloads at all. `AgentProcess.stop()` still works because `terminate()` targets the PID directly, not the process group. The `_check_cli_available()` version check subprocess intentionally omits `start_new_session` (short-lived diagnostic, not an agent). After a dashboard reload, surviving agents are tracked by the liveness sweep (PID alive = skip); when they finish, the sweep marks them "interrupted" since the `_wait_and_finalize` task was lost during reload. Files: `sova/ipc/runtime.py`, `sova/cli/app.py`. PR #592 (issue #592).
- **Environment scrubbing at the spawn boundary is the only correct fix for inherited provider-routing variables**: every agent spawn built its child environment with `dict(os.environ)`, so a `CLAUDE_CODE_USE_VERTEX` or `ANTHROPIC_VERTEX_PROJECT_ID` left in the server's environment silently redirected agents away from the configured `llm.provider`, surfacing as a cryptic "model not found" with no indication of the cause. Editing shell profiles cannot fix this: the server inherits from whatever shell launched it, and agents inherit from the server, so the fix must live where the child process is actually created. `scrub_agent_env()` in `sova/utils/env.py` removes two variable classes: third-party provider routing/model pinning (`CLAUDE_CODE_USE_VERTEX`, `CLAUDE_CODE_USE_BEDROCK`, `ANTHROPIC_VERTEX_PROJECT_ID`, `ANTHROPIC_MODEL`, etc.) and the identity of an enclosing interactive Claude Code session (`CLAUDECODE`, `CLAUDE_CODE_SESSION_ID`, `CLAUDE_CODE_MESSAGING_TOKEN`, etc.), which leaks in whenever the server itself is started from inside a Claude Code session. Credential variables (`ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`) are deliberately preserved. Applied at every subprocess boundary that talks to the Claude CLI: `_inject_agent_marker()` in `sova/ipc/runtime.py` (the single choke point for all agent spawn paths) and all three CLI invocation sites in `sova/llm/providers/claude_code.py` (`invoke()`, `check_available()`, `_start_streaming_process()`). A deployment that intentionally routes through Vertex or Bedrock opts variables back in via `agent.env_passthrough` (triple registered: `AgentConfig`, `settings_meta.py`), read defensively so a broken or missing project config degrades to scrubbing everything rather than crashing the spawn. Files: `sova/utils/env.py`, `sova/ipc/runtime.py`, `sova/llm/providers/claude_code.py`. Issues #929, #930, PR #928.
- **`check_available()` must report authentication, not just installation**: `ClaudeCodeProvider.check_available()` ran `claude --version` and treated a successful version check as readiness. An installed but logged-out CLI passes that check and reports healthy, then every agent run fails at invocation time with the real cause visible only in server logs. It now probes `claude auth status --json` after a successful `--version` and surfaces the account email and subscription tier on success, or points at `claude auth login` on failure. The probe fails open (reports available with an unknown auth state) when the CLI build has no `auth` subcommand or returns output this does not recognize, so an older CLI is never blocked by a version mismatch it can't help. File: `sova/llm/providers/claude_code.py`. Issue #930, PR #928.

  `LiteLLMProvider.check_available()` had the same defect one layer down: it reported `litellm <version>` as available, which only proves the package imports and says nothing about the vendor that will actually serve the request. `create_provider()` now forwards `cfg.provider` as a `vendor=` argument (one of `litellm`, `hybrid`, `openai`, `ollama`, `vertex`), and the check runs the credential probe that matches it: `OPENAI_API_KEY` present for `openai`, a reachable `GET {api_base}/api/tags` for `ollama`, and `ANTHROPIC_VERTEX_PROJECT_ID` plus a resolvable ADC token for `vertex`. `litellm`/`hybrid` are generic routing with no fixed vendor and therefore no single credential shape to probe, so they deliberately keep the package-only answer. The `vendor` argument matters beyond this check: without it a vendor-specific provider silently inherits the generic behavior and the Connections page would show a green dot for a provider with no credentials at all. File: `sova/llm/litellm_provider.py`, `sova/llm/provider.py`. Issue #1120.

- **The Connections page builds a candidate provider without an `LLMConfig`, and only persists one that re-validates**: `create_provider(cfg.llm)` takes the project's single already-validated `LLMConfig`, which by construction describes only the *active* provider, so it cannot answer "is `ollama` ready?" while `claude-code` is active: `LLMConfig`'s cross-field validator raises for `openai`/`ollama`/`vertex` with an empty `llm.model`, which an inactive candidate may well have. `_build_llm_candidate_provider()` in `sova/dashboard/services/setup_service.py` therefore instantiates the provider class directly, bypassing `LLMConfig` entirely, and the catalog passes a `_CATALOG_PLACEHOLDER_MODEL` for the model-required vendors (safe only because their `check_available()` probes credentials and never reads `self.model`). Three operations sit on top, deliberately separated by cost: `validate` is a free `check_available()` probe that persists nothing, `activate` re-runs that probe and persists only on success, and `test` is the one action that spends money (a real `invoke()`), never triggered implicitly by the other two. Both `validate` and `test` reject an id outside `_LLM_PROVIDER_LABELS` before building anything: an unknown id otherwise falls through `_build_llm_candidate_provider()`'s LiteLLM branch and, in `test`'s case, reaches a real billable `invoke()` against the submitted `api_base`. `activate` writes every key with exactly what the card submitted, including an empty model or api_base, so switching away from a vendor cannot leave that vendor's pin behind (an `anthropic` provider built with a leftover `gpt-5` fails on every call; both readers of `llm.model` fall back when it is empty, `cost_service` to `agent.model` and `reviewer` to its own default). All three keys are written through `settings_service.update_config_many()` as a single, all-or-nothing DB transaction: the combined resulting config is validated once (so there is no write-order dependency between a model-required target needing its model set first and a clear of `llm.model` needing the provider switched first), and a failure partway through rolls back every key rather than leaving a partial mix of the old provider plus the new model persisted. Two things are deliberately *not* gates. `list_available_models()` is best-effort discovery that falls back to the four curated Anthropic ids when no vendor source is configured at all (plain OpenAI with no `api_base`), so a model missing from the enumerated catalog is folded into `validate`'s detail as a typo hint by `_unknown_model_hint()` rather than rejected, and `_model_id_variants()` first reconciles the dialects the config and the catalog spell differently (litellm's `vertex_ai/` prefix, Ollama's implicit `:latest` tag); treating that answer as authoritative rejected every documented openai/ollama/vertex config outright, so no operator could activate the three providers that require a model. `activate_llm_candidate()`/`activate_runtime_candidate()` write straight through `settings_service.update_config_many()`/`update_config()` rather than `POST /settings/config` (whose handler dispatches to `_reload_all_configs()` on an `llm.`/`agent.runtime`/`codex.` key), so each calls `sova.llm.client.reload_provider_async()`/`sova.ipc.runtime.reload_runtime()` itself after a successful persist: without it, the running server kept serving calls through the old provider/runtime until an unrelated settings write happened to touch that prefix (issue #1148). And `routing_backend` is detected from the *scrubbed* environment a spawned Claude CLI child would actually see (`_claude_cli_backend()`, mirroring `sova/llm/client.py:resolve_alias`), never the server's raw `os.environ`: a routing var that is set but absent from `agent.env_passthrough` would otherwise make the page hide the `claude auth login` button and send the operator to refresh Google ADC on a deployment whose CLI is really firstParty. `_LLM_PROVIDER_LABELS`, `_RUNTIME_LABELS` and `_LLM_MODEL_REQUIRED` re-list ids owned by `LLMConfig.provider`, `AgentConfig.runtime` and `sova/config/models.py:_VENDOR_MODEL_EXAMPLES`; all three are pinned by `tests/test_dashboard.py::TestConnectionsAPI::test_catalog_tables_match_config` so adding a provider or runtime there cannot leave this page silently missing it. The `claude auth login` reconnect is CLI-owned: SOVA spawns the subprocess (single-flight per project, hard `_RECONNECT_TIMEOUT`, strong reference to the watcher task held on `_ReconnectSession.watcher` so the watcher cannot be garbage-collected mid-wait and pin `status` at `running` forever), observes only its exit code, and never reads the token; the real post-login state is re-derived by `get_auth_status()`'s own `claude auth status --json` probe, whose TTL cache `_invalidate_auth_status_cache()` drops so the page that caused the change does not show stale state. `llm.api_key` is shared by both the Anthropic and OpenAI cards (`create_provider()`/`_build_llm_candidate_provider()`): the resolved value is threaded explicitly as the `api_key=` argument to the underlying LiteLLM/Anthropic SDK call (never mutated into `os.environ`, so it cannot leak into every other subprocess SOVA spawns), taking precedence over any `OPENAI_API_KEY`/`ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` exported in the server's own environment rather than only applying when that var is unset. The keyring entry is scoped per `(project_dir, provider)` via `keyring_store.scoped_secret_name()`, not per-project-only: saving a key on the Connections page's OpenAI card writes `llm.api_key:openai:<path>`, and the same key on the Anthropic card writes a separate `llm.api_key:anthropic:<path>` entry, so switching providers (or probing an inactive candidate) never reads or overwrites a different provider's credential. A pre-upgrade, unscoped legacy `llm.api_key` keyring entry is read as a fallback only when the provider being resolved is the project's currently-active provider (never for an unrelated inactive candidate), and only ever read, never auto-migrated or deleted except by an explicit clear. Files: `sova/dashboard/services/setup_service.py`, `sova/dashboard/services/settings_service.py`, `sova/dashboard/routers/auth.py`, `sova/dashboard/routers/settings.py`, `sova/dashboard/templates/connections.html`, `sova/llm/provider.py`, `sova/llm/litellm_provider.py`, `sova/llm/keyring_store.py`. Issue #1120, #1148.
- **Codex credential ownership: keyring-first, `CODEX_API_KEY` opt-in scoped to the Codex child only**: `CodexRuntime` mirrors the Claude Code pattern above rather than inventing a second credential model. Local setups should rely on Codex's own keyring storage (`codex login`, `cli_auth_credentials_store = "keyring"`); SOVA never reads, copies, masks, or persists that credential; only a presence boolean is ever reported (`", CODEX_API_KEY set"` appended to the `check_available()` detail string, never the value). `CODEX_API_KEY` is added to `SCRUBBED_VARS` (`sova/utils/env.py`) so it is stripped from every spawned child by default, exactly like a provider-routing variable, rather than reused via the global `agent.env_passthrough` (which would re-leak it into `ClaudeCodeRuntime`/`AiderRuntime` children too). `CodexRuntime.spawn()` is the only call site that re-admits it: `scrub_agent_env()` gained an `extra_scrub` parameter (applied after the `passthrough` keep-set, so it wins unconditionally) and `_inject_agent_marker()`/`_spawn_agent_process()` gained a matching `extra_env` parameter (merged in after scrubbing). `CodexRuntime` uses both together: `extra_scrub=ANTHROPIC_CREDENTIAL_VARS` (`ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`, `ANTHROPIC_AUTH_TOKEN`, deliberately not in the shared `SCRUBBED_VARS` since the Anthropic provider still needs them) strips a Claude credential that happened to be set in the server's own environment, and `extra_env={"CODEX_API_KEY": ...}` re-injects the Codex key, both scoped to that one spawn. Readiness is a three-state probe (`check_available()`: missing CLI / logged-out / authenticated), composing the existing `_check_cli_available()` version check with `_probe_codex_auth()` (`codex login status`, a local credential-store read, never a model request, bounded by the same `_VERSION_CHECK_TIMEOUT` and run through the shared `sova/utils/shell.py:run()` helper, as `_probe_auth()` in the Claude Code provider already does, so timeout kill-and-wait and cancellation cleanup are not hand-rolled a second time) and a pure `_interpret_codex_auth_probe()`. Unlike Claude's `--json` contract, `codex login status` has no documented machine-readable output, so the interpreter attempts JSON first (guarding against a bare scalar the same way `_parse_log_file()` must, since valid JSON is not necessarily an object) and falls back to a plain-text phrase scan, matching `_extract_auth_detail()`'s approach to `gh auth status` in `doctor.py`. Two narrow rules keep that fallback honest: a recognized flag is read by strict identity (`is True`/`is False`), never truthiness, so a `null` or string value is unknown rather than a definite logout; and a JSON object whose flags were all unreadable stops at unknown instead of phrase-scanning its own source, where the key name `authenticated` would match regardless of its value. `_resolve_codex_api_key()` reads the caller-supplied `spawn(env=...)` mapping when there is one and only falls back to `os.environ`, so a curated per-project key is never replaced by the server's. Unparseable or unrecognized output fails open (`None`, "auth state unknown") exactly like the Claude probe: a CLI build variation must never block agent spawning. The probe reuses the same scrubbed-plus-re-injected environment a real spawn would see, so its answer matches what agents actually experience. Files: `sova/utils/env.py`, `sova/ipc/runtime.py`. Issue #943.
- **Reviewer spawns as a trusted subprocess, not through a coding-agent runtime sandbox**: the reviewer never needed interactive coding-agent tool-use: it only makes a model call over a diff plus GitHub/DB writes. `agent_lifecycle.py` now routes it through `spawn_direct()` (the same plain-trusted-subprocess path pipeline roles use) via a local `_DIRECT_SPAWN_ROLES = _PIPELINE_ROLES | {"reviewer"}` set, scoped to this one spawn-path decision; `"reviewer"` is deliberately NOT added to `_PIPELINE_ROLES` itself, since that constant names the `WorkflowEngine`-driven step-pipeline roles, a shape `ReviewerRole` bypasses entirely (it clears its own `current_step` sentinel instead, rather than running steps). Note that `agent_db.py` and `work_service.py` do NOT read this constant for that reasoning: each independently defines its own local `_PIPELINE_ROLES` frozenset (`agent_db.py`'s gates `_validate_pipeline_outcome()`, pipeline-bypass detection at run exit; `work_service.py`'s gates step-count display; `work_service.py`'s copy doesn't even include `"planner"`), so adding `"reviewer"` to `runtime.py`'s copy would not have propagated to either of them. `tests/test_ipc.py`'s `test_pipeline_roles_set` pins `_DIRECT_SPAWN_ROLES == _PIPELINE_ROLES | {"reviewer"}` so this does not drift silently again. This removes the `effective_role == "reviewer" and runtime.name == "codex"` fail-fast guard and the `read_only=(effective_role == "reviewer")` kwarg that issue #946 added (see the superseded-content note in that entry above): Claude Code's `read_only` flag was already advisory-only (no real sandbox), so the reviewer never actually depended on runtime-level sandbox enforcement, and under Codex the sandbox blocked the reviewer's own writes (DB, handoff file, GitHub API) outright. Routing around the runtime wrapper entirely removes a redundant layer rather than a protection the role depended on, and makes `agent.runtime` and `llm.provider` properly orthogonal for the reviewer: a project can set `agent.runtime = "codex"` (for the developer/pipeline roles) while the reviewer still runs successfully regardless, since it never touches `AgentRuntime` at all. A second, independent change makes `ReviewerRole._resolve_review_model()` provider-aware: `roles.reviewer_model` defaults to the bare Claude family alias `"sonnet"`, which only the Claude Code CLI and the Anthropic API provider's own alias table (`resolve_model_alias()`) can expand; any other `llm.provider` (openai, ollama, vertex, litellm, hybrid) routes through `LiteLLMProvider`, which sends the model string straight to the backend (`target_model = model or self.model`), so a bare alias there is not necessarily a real model ID. When resolution lands on one of `sova.llm.backends.TIER_NAMES` under a non-claude-code, non-anthropic provider, the resolver first runs it through `sova.llm.client.resolve_alias()`, the one choke point that already honours `llm.model_aliases` and, for VERTEX/BEDROCK, expands the tier to its own servable candidate ID; only when that leaves the alias unresolved does it fall back to `ctx.config.llm.model` (guaranteed non-empty for vendor provider types by `LLMConfig._default_model_for_litellm`'s validation). Resolving through `resolve_alias()` first matters because a Vertex deployment's own tier-candidate table can already expand `"opus"` to a pinned snapshot ID: falling back to `llm.model` unconditionally would silently discard that explicit, correctly-mapped `roles.reviewer_model` in favour of an unrelated general-purpose model (issue #1126 review). Third, an optional, narrowly-scoped sub-call (`ReviewerRole._gather_repo_context()`, gated by `review.repo_context_agent`, default off) lets the reviewer ask a read-only Codex sandbox (`codex exec --json --sandbox read-only`, plus `--model` when `codex.model` is configured) for extra repository context beyond the diff, parsed via a fresh `CodexStreamParser` and folded into the existing single-review prompt as a new, clearly-labeled "Additional Repository Context" block (untrusted background; never a finding or verdict source on its own; not wired into the panel-review path, which stays diff-only, so the sub-call itself is skipped entirely when `review.panel.enabled`). Because the sub-agent reads arbitrary, potentially attacker-authored (fork-PR) repository files, a static `<repo_context>` delimiter is not a safe fence on its own: content containing the literal `</repo_context>` could close the tag early and merge injected instructions into the prompt's own structure. `_build_review_prompt()` instead generates a per-call random nonce (`secrets.token_hex(8)`), delimits the block with `<repo_context id="{nonce}">...</repo_context id="{nonce}">`, and strips any occurrence of that nonce (plus backtick runs, as before) from the untrusted content first, so nothing the sub-agent produced can ever spell out a matching closing tag. The terminal event's text is only trusted when its `stop_reason` is not `"error"` and the process `returncode` is 0; a `turn.failed`/`error` event is treated as "no context" rather than injected into the prompt as if it were real repository background. Truncation detection does not infer from the rendered text's length against `codex.py`'s `_MAX_CONTENT_CHARS` (2000): that rendered text is already redacted and capped, so a secret-dense or whitespace-free message can shrink below the cap on the exact inputs truncation was meant to flag. Instead, `CodexStreamParser` tracks whether the *raw* `item.completed` agent-message text (before redaction/truncation) exceeded `_MAX_CONTENT_CHARS`, and threads that through as `LLMResult.truncated`; `_gather_repo_context()` appends the `"\n[context truncated]"` marker only when `llm_result.truncated` is set. The terminal event's token usage (not cost, which Codex's `LLMResult` always reports as `CostSource.UNKNOWN`) is attributed to the run via `ctx.add_usage()` so the spend is at least visible in token counts. The sub-call's own timeout is configurable via `review.repo_context_timeout` (default 120s) rather than hardcoded. That sub-call's environment is built from an explicit allowlist (`_REPO_CONTEXT_ENV_ALLOWLIST`: `PATH`, `HOME`, `USER`, `LANG`, `LC_ALL`, `TMPDIR`, `CODEX_HOME`, `XDG_CONFIG_HOME`, plus `CODEX_API_KEY` if set, resolved via the shared `sova.ipc.runtime._resolve_codex_api_key()` rather than a second inline lookup) rather than derived from the reviewer's own trusted `gh_env`, so it can never inherit a GitHub token or database connection string, even if a future `gh_env` field addition is never updated here. `CODEX_API_KEY` only ever reaches that allowlist lookup because it is in `SCRUBBED_VARS` and therefore stripped from the reviewer's own spawned environment by default: `spawn_direct()` (`sova/ipc/runtime.py`) gained an `extra_env` parameter, and `agent_lifecycle.py` passes `_codex_extra_env(gh_env)` at the reviewer's own spawn site, gated on `review.repo_context_agent` being enabled for the project (checked via a dedicated `_resolve_repo_context_agent_enabled()` helper, same fail-closed pattern as `_resolve_config_model()`), so the key reaches the reviewer process's `os.environ` only when the feature that reads it is actually turned on, rather than ambiently on every reviewer spawn. Files: `sova/dashboard/services/agent_lifecycle.py`, `sova/ipc/runtime.py`, `sova/roles/reviewer.py`, `sova/roles/_review_comments.py`, `sova/ipc/codex.py`, `sova/llm/models.py`, `sova/config/models.py`, `sova/dashboard/settings_meta.py`. Issue #1126.
- **`OPENAI_API_KEY` is scrubbed like `CODEX_API_KEY` and re-injected only for `spawn_direct()`'s trusted pipeline child, and Codex prompt delivery moves to stdin when the CLI documents support for it**: `OPENAI_CREDENTIAL_VARS` (`sova/utils/env.py`) folds `OPENAI_API_KEY` into `SCRUBBED_VARS` alongside `CODEX_CREDENTIAL_VARS`, so it is absent from every spawned child by default. Unlike `CODEX_API_KEY`, which `CodexRuntime` alone re-admits via `_codex_extra_env()`, `OPENAI_API_KEY`'s one trusted exception is `spawn_direct()` (`sova/ipc/runtime.py`), the launcher for the developer/researcher/planner pipeline roles: that child runs a full `WorkflowEngine` which may read `OPENAI_API_KEY` in-process (`sova/llm/litellm_provider.py`) when the project's `llm.provider` is an OpenAI-backed litellm model, so `spawn_direct()` passes `_openai_extra_env(env)` as `extra_env`, scoped to that one call site only; `ClaudeCodeRuntime`/`AiderRuntime`/`CodexRuntime` spawns never receive it (`agent.model` today only documents Claude models, so an OpenAI-backed Aider model is an explicit, documented non-goal rather than an oversight). The in-process litellm/OpenAI SDK read in the parent server itself reads `os.environ` directly and is unaffected by `scrub_agent_env()`, which only transforms the dict handed to a *spawned* child. `scrub_agent_env()` also gained `_PASSTHROUGH_INELIGIBLE` (`CODEX_CREDENTIAL_VARS | OPENAI_CREDENTIAL_VARS`), subtracted from the `passthrough` keep-set unconditionally before the scrub loop runs: previously a deployment naming `CODEX_API_KEY` in `agent.env_passthrough` could re-admit it to every runtime (the keep-set check had no carve-out for credentials, only `extra_scrub` did), which contradicted the documented intent that credentials are never reused via that global, provider-routing-oriented escape hatch; this closes that gap for both vars, not just the new one. Separately, `CodexRuntime.spawn()` gained a stdin delivery path mirroring `ClaudeCodeRuntime.spawn()`'s existing `stdin_payload` mechanism, resolving the argv-visibility residual the previous entry's class docstring used to document as deferred to epic #940: `_probe_codex_stdin_prompt_support()` runs `codex exec --help` once per runtime instance, in the same scrubbed environment `_probe_codex_auth()` uses (`_inject_agent_marker(None, extra_scrub=ANTHROPIC_CREDENTIAL_VARS)`, no credential needed for a `--help` read) via the same shared `run()` helper (a local, non-billing, non-model-invoking read, bounded by `_VERSION_CHECK_TIMEOUT`, so any `OSError`, timeout, or non-zero exit fails closed to `False`/argv), and looks for the CLI's own PROMPT argument documentation mentioning stdin: confirmation requires both the line to carry the argument's own `<PROMPT>`/`[PROMPT]` definition marker (`_PROMPT_ARG_MARKERS`, so an unrelated option's incidental stdin mention cannot false-positive, and the CLI's own synopsis/usage line, which carries the same marker but never describes stdin, is explicitly skipped so it cannot shadow the real Arguments entry) and, within the clause of that line actually containing "stdin" (split on `[.,]`, not the whole line), no negation marker (`_STDIN_NEGATION_MARKERS`) — the per-clause scoping exists because a line like "If not provided as an argument, read from stdin" contains "not" in a clause unrelated to the stdin confirmation, which a whole-line check misreads as negated. `_prompt_argument_blocks()` is a generator yielding every (non-usage) marker line's definition line joined with its wrapped continuation lines (clap wraps a long description onto further, more-indented lines with no blank line separating them), since the installed CLI's real PROMPT documentation might not be the first marker line found; the probe checks each yielded block in turn and confirms on the first one whose clause scan matches. If no marker line exists in the output at all, the probe raises `CodexStdinProbeAmbiguousError` rather than silently defaulting to argv delivery, since a future CLI restructuring its `--help` output would otherwise permanently and invisibly revert every spawn to the argv-visibility hazard with nothing in the logs to say why; this is distinct from a probe that could not run at all (CLI missing, timed out, non-zero exit), which still fails closed to argv. `CodexRuntime._resolve_stdin_capable()` caches the outcome (including a raised `CodexStdinProbeAmbiguousError`, re-raised on every subsequent call) on `self._stdin_capable` so the probe subprocess never re-runs on a later spawn from the same instance, and serializes the first probe behind a per-instance `asyncio.Lock` (`self._stdin_capable_lock`): without it, two spawns racing on the same still-uncached runtime instance would both run the probe concurrently, and whichever result landed last would silently overwrite an earlier ambiguous outcome with a plain `False` rather than the error that outcome is supposed to raise on every call. When the probe confirms support, `spawn()` omits the PROMPT positional entirely and delivers the (preamble-prepended) prompt via `stdin_payload` instead, so it never reaches argv; when it cannot confirm support, `spawn()` keeps today's behavior unchanged (`-- {prompt}` appended to argv). Failing closed is deliberate: delivering a prompt over stdin to a CLI that does not actually read it there risks the child blocking on an interactive terminal read, a hang strictly worse than the argv-visibility hazard the probe exists to close. Files: `sova/utils/env.py`, `sova/ipc/runtime.py`. Issue #1127, PR #1140 (CodeRabbit + self-review).
- **Reviewer post failure must not trigger address-review**: `ReviewerRole._post_review()` returns `bool` (True=posted, False=all attempts failed) and never raises. `_execute()` sets `review.post_failed = True` on failure. `_write_handoff()` is always called regardless. When `post_failed=True`, the handoff writes `next_action="review_post_failed"` with a single `HandoffAction(id="rerun_review", auto_execute=False)`. No `sova-review` marker is ever posted when posting failed, so `resolve_sova_verdict()` correctly reports `has_sova_review=False` rather than inventing a verdict, and `_process_auto_handoff()` does not spawn an address-review agent. File: `sova/roles/reviewer.py`. PR #513.
- **GitHub review markers are the sole SOVA verdict source**: `resolve_sova_verdict()` in `work_verdict.py` is the single assembly path: **both** the dashboard (`_fetch_sova_verdicts()`, a thin batching wrapper) and the supervisor (`_refine_in_review_action()` in `progression.py`) call it, so one PR cannot yield two different verdict dicts and therefore cannot resolve to two different next actions through `resolve_next_action()`. It is a thin adapter over `parse_review_history(reviews: list[dict]) -> dict`, which computes verdict, anchor commit, addressed state, and address-cycle count (`address_cycles`) from one PR's review history in a single pass: scans newest-first by `submitted_at` (the ordering key, not array position), skips DISMISSED reviews entirely (neither counted toward `address_cycles` nor eligible to win the verdict scan), counts every `sova-addressed` marker found as one completed address cycle, and reports `"addressed"` instead of a stale verdict whenever a `sova-addressed` marker is newer than the newest `sova-review` marker. A review body with neither marker (a human review, or a pre-marker SOVA review) is skipped without affecting the scan. The review history itself comes from `get_pr_review_data()` in `sova/git/pr.py`: the same batched GraphQL call that already fetches thread/bot-CR data for a PR also fetches its last 100 reviews' `state`/`body`/`submittedAt` (`PRReviewData.review_history`); the commit GitHub recorded each review against is deliberately not fetched, since the anchor `parse_review_history()` reports is the marker's own `sha=` value. A PR with more than 100 reviews (the GraphQL connection page-size maximum) hits `review_history_truncated`, and `resolve_sova_verdict()` fails closed (`history_unavailable`) rather than report an undercounted `address_cycles` or a lost verdict; backward pagination past this single page is a deliberately deferred follow-up (PR #1145 review discussion). `_prewarm_verdict_cache()` seeds every PR of one dashboard poll from a single batched `get_pr_review_data()` call, because `resolve_sova_verdict()` on its own can only ask for the one PR it was handed and would otherwise cost one GraphQL round trip per PR. `resolve_sova_verdict()`'s per-PR `_sova_verdict_cache` (positive TTL 5 min, negative TTL 30s, keyed by `(project_dir, pr_number)`) stores the whole computed verdict including `address_cycles`, since every field now comes from the same fetch; `invalidate_verdict()` (called by `_check_address_review_circuit_breaker()` right after an address cycle completes) is what keeps a served verdict from lagging a just-completed cycle, not a per-call recompute. **This replaces an earlier five-source assembly** (the local DB via a since-deleted `get_sova_review_verdict()`, the issue's `sova:*` label reconciled against the DB, a GitHub review marker scan, and a heuristic `## PR Summary`/`## Verdict` body-structure fallback for pre-marker reviews) that existed to paper over those five sources disagreeing with each other (#987, #988, #991, #993, #1019): the DB source needed a local TaskRun this machine's own DB might not have (a cross-instance review, or `/review-pr`'s output-parsed fallback); the label source carried no commit-SHA anchor and never cleared after an address cycle, so it could rubber-stamp a PR the other sources already knew had moved on; the DB-vs-label reconciliation (`_merge_label_verdict()`) and address-cycle supersession (`_supersede_if_addressed_locally()`/`has_address_cycle_since()`) existed purely to keep those sources from contradicting each other. Deleting the DB source also deleted its own address-review-round counter, `count_address_review_runs()` (`sova/supervisor/gates/utils.py`, a DB query over `TaskRun`/`StepExecution` with its own network-outage exclusion logic): outage exclusion is now structurally unnecessary, since a failed or interrupted run never posts a GitHub review and therefore can never produce a `sova-addressed` marker to begin with. What the move to a remote counter does introduce is an *unreadable* count, which the DB counter never had: `get_pr_review_data()` does not raise when GitHub is unreachable, it maps the PR to `None`, so collapsing that into an empty history would report "no review, zero cycles" during every outage and quietly uncap the review loop. `_fetch_review_history()` therefore returns `None` (not `[]`) for that case and `resolve_sova_verdict()` tags the result `history_unavailable` (`UNRESOLVED_KEY`) and declines to cache it, so the next poll retries instead of re-serving the non-answer. Display callers ignore the tag; `_check_address_review_circuit_breaker()` fails closed on it, blocking the next cycle with a distinct "budget unverifiable" reason, the same position `/address-pr` step 17 takes for the same decision. Because the marker is now the only record of a completed cycle, `ResolveExternalReviewsStep._post_address_summary()` also posts it for a cycle whose `addressed_review_findings` is empty (`AddressReviewStep`'s "drained the pending documentation queue" outcome), which it previously skipped: that cycle used to be counted from the DB and would otherwise be invisible to the budget. `sova/supervisor/gates/review_completed.py`'s `_has_reviewer_run()`, renamed `_has_review_marker()` (one of its three independent "was this PR reviewed" signals, alongside the `sova:approved` label and a human GitHub approval) and `_validate_review_pr()`'s fallback in `agent_db.py` (persists `TaskRun.handoff_json` for `/review-pr` outcome validation, still consumed by `address_review.py`'s findings lookup) were both updated to call `resolve_sova_verdict()`/drop the deleted heuristic respectively, rather than the deleted DB reader. A verdict is still anchored to the commit SHA the reviewer actually reviewed (`review_head_sha`, parsed straight from the `<!-- sova-review: {verdict} sha={full_sha} -->` marker); the stale-verdict check inline in `resolve_next_action()` (`work_state.py`) compares `PRFacts.sova_verdict_sha` against `PRFacts.head_sha`, and a verdict with an unknown SHA is treated as fresh, never stale. `parse_review_history()`'s `trusted_login` filter (both the verdict scan and the `address_cycles` count) only runs if there is actually a login to trust: `resolve_sova_verdict()`/`_prewarm_verdict_cache()` fall back to `get_active_gh_user()` (the ambient `gh auth status` account) when `adapter.github_user` is unconfigured, and fail closed (`history_unavailable`) rather than call `parse_review_history()` with an empty `trusted_login`, which would otherwise disable the forgery filter entirely. Files: `sova/dashboard/services/work_verdict.py`, `sova/utils/review_markers.py`, `sova/git/pr.py`, `sova/dashboard/services/agent_recovery.py`, `sova/dashboard/services/agent_handoff.py`, `sova/dashboard/services/pr_service.py`, `sova/dashboard/routers/prs.py`, `sova/supervisor/progression.py`, `sova/supervisor/gates/review_completed.py`, `sova/dashboard/services/agent_db.py`. Original canonical-path work: issue #987, PR #1019, #991. GitHub-markers-only rework: issue #1112.
- **An address cycle must be visible on GitHub and must supersede a verdict regardless of where that verdict was read from**: PR #1063 stayed at "SOVA Changes Requested" with the Integrate button gated after the autonomous address-review pipeline had fixed and pushed every finding, CI was green and CodeRabbit had approved. Three independent gaps combined. (1) The review had been posted by `/pr`'s inline self-review (the `/review-pr` workflow), which leaves no reviewer `TaskRun`, so `get_sova_review_verdict()` found no run and returned `no_review`; the address-cycle supersession check lived inside the branch that had just found a run, so it never ran, and `resolve_sova_verdict()`'s GitHub marker fallback then served the pre-fix `revise` forever. `_supersede_if_addressed_locally()` (`work_verdict.py`) now applies the same rule to a GitHub-sourced verdict through the new public `has_address_cycle_since()` (`agent_recovery.py`, fail-open `False`), keyed on the review's own `reviewed_at`. (2) The address run had `issue_number=NULL`: the PR was opened at 20:49, the address run started at 21:49, and the issue it now closes was only created at 21:58, so `_resolve_issue_from_pr()` correctly found nothing to link. Every query in `get_sova_review_verdict()` ANDed `issue_number == N` with `pr_number == P`, so this run was invisible even to the DB path. `_run_scope_filters()` now scopes on the PR number alone whenever one is known (a PR identifies the cycle exactly; the issue filter only ever added false negatives) and on the issue only when no PR is known. (3) The two address paths left different evidence on GitHub: `/address-pr` step 15 posted an `## Address Review: Round N` summary as a plain PR comment, and `AddressReviewStep` posted nothing at all, so nothing on the PR itself recorded that the findings had been handled. Both now post the summary as a COMMENT-state PR review whose first line is `<!-- sova-addressed: sha=<pushed head> -->` (`sova/utils/review_markers.py`, a leaf module so the steps and the dashboard share one regex without importing `sova.roles`): the pipeline does it in `ResolveExternalReviewsStep._post_address_summary()`, after `PushStep` and `MonitorCIStep` so the anchor is the commit that carries the fixes, from findings `AddressReviewStep` stores on `ExecutionContext.addressed_review_findings` (which `write_step_handoff()` also records as `addressed_findings`). A review, not an issue comment, because it arrives through the single `get_pr_reviews()` fetch with a server timestamp: `_parse_sova_review_from_github()` scans newest-first and returns `addressed` when it meets the marker before the verdict, so a cross-instance address cycle is detected with no extra API call, and `_load_findings_from_github_reviews()` skips marker bodies so a summary is never re-parsed as a finding. Separately, `format_from_json()` (`_review_format.py`) now accepts a `sha` field and the `/review-pr` command fetches `headRefOid` and passes it, so command-posted reviews are SHA-anchored like `ReviewerRole`'s (the anchoring shipped in #987 never reached this path). Deliberately unchanged: an "addressed" verdict still fails the `sova_reviewed` integration gate (#993 requires an approving verdict on the new head); the gate reason now says so explicitly, and the dashboard routes the PR to `PR_REVIEW_ADDRESSED` with "Review" as the primary action instead of "Address". Files: `sova/utils/review_markers.py`, `sova/core/context.py`, `sova/core/steps/address_review.py`, `sova/core/steps/resolve_external_reviews.py`, `sova/core/steps/_handoff_helpers.py`, `sova/dashboard/services/agent_recovery.py`, `sova/dashboard/services/work_verdict.py`, `sova/dashboard/services/pr_service.py`, `sova/roles/_review_format.py`, `sova/commands/data/commands/{address-pr,review-pr}.md` (mirrored in `.claude/commands/`).
- **`_post_review()` must never raise; `_write_handoff()` must always run**: when all three posting attempts fail (formal review API, body-only review API, PR comment fallback), the method returns `False` instead of raising. `ReviewResult.post_failed = True` is set before `_write_handoff()` is called. `_write_handoff()` branches on this flag: when `True`, it writes a `review_post_failed` `AgentHandoff` and a `DashboardHandoff` with a single `rerun_review` action (`auto_execute=False`). `get_sova_review_verdict()` returns `"post_failed"` for `next_action == "review_post_failed"`, which `resolve_next_action()` (see the next-action resolver entry below) falls through to `PR_AWAITING_REVIEW` since `"post_failed"` matches none of the revise/block/approve rules. This prevents null `handoff_json` (from an exception propagating before `_write_handoff()` ran) from being misclassified as "revise" by the output-line parser fallback. Files: `sova/roles/reviewer.py`, `sova/dashboard/services/agent_recovery.py`. PR #502.
- **Pipeline variant detection must gate on `current_step`**: `get_step_progress()` and `_detect_variant()` use `role+pr_number` to identify address-review runs, but ONLY when `current_step in (None, "agent")`. The `current_step="agent"` sentinel is set briefly at TaskRun creation and cleared by `_adopt_task_run()` when the WorkflowEngine takes over. WorkflowEngine progresses through real step names and acquires `pr_number` mid-pipeline via `_sync_task_run_context()` after `CreatePRStep`: without the gate, every developer run that creates a PR would be mislabeled as "address_review" for steps 10-12 (monitor_ci, extract_memory, handoff_to_reviewer). `_detect_variant_from_steps()` is authoritative when step history contains address-review-only steps; otherwise falls back to `get_step_progress()`.
- **Roles must self-discover missing context**: when a role is spawned fresh from the dashboard (not via `--resume`), `ExecutionContext` fields populated by earlier pipeline steps (e.g., `pr_number` from `CreatePRStep`) are `None`. Roles that depend on upstream context must look it up from GitHub rather than failing. Pattern: `ReviewerRole` calls `find_pr_for_issue()` when `ctx.pr_number` is missing.
- **PR deduplication in CreatePRStep**: `CreatePRStep.execute()` calls `find_pr_for_issue()` before `create_pr()`. If an open PR already exists for the issue, it adopts it (`ctx.pr_number = existing.number`) instead of creating a duplicate. `find_pr_for_issue()` verifies PRs by checking for issue-linking keywords (`Closes/Fixes/Resolves #N`) in the body or branch name pattern (`issue-N`), not free-text search, to avoid false positives.
- **State-adopting steps must replicate all side effects**: when a step has an early-return path for "already exists" (e.g., `CreatePRStep` adopting an existing PR), it must still perform all side effects the primary path does: especially tracker state transitions. Without this, issues get stuck (e.g., remaining `in_progress` when they should be `in_review`). Pattern: audit the primary path for side effects, then replicate them in every skip/adoption path.
- **macOS notifications via terminal-notifier**: `send_desktop_notification()` in `sova/ipc/notifications.py` uses `terminal-notifier` (Homebrew) as the primary path on macOS, supporting custom app icon (`-appIcon`), subtitle, sound, and notification grouping. Falls back to JXA (`osascript -l JavaScript`) if `terminal-notifier` is not installed. JXA fallback uses `json.dumps()` for injection-safe escaping and `soundName: "Glass"`. All call sites pass title="SOVA", subtitle="{Role} {status} #{issue}", message="{project} | {details}", group="sova-{issue}".
- **Dual handoff persistence (file + DB)**: agents write both DB-backed `AgentHandoff` (for orchestrator/scheduler history via `write_handoff()`) and file-based `DashboardHandoff` (for dashboard polling via `write_handoff_file()`). Dashboard reads the file for speed (no async DB in polling loop); scheduler queries the DB for cross-run state. `DashboardHandoff` has `next_actions` (UI buttons); `AgentHandoff` has `pending_findings` (agent context). Both written in every role's `_write_handoff()`.
- **Auto-handoff must clear handoff file before spawning next agent**: `_process_auto_handoff()` in `agent_handoff.py` calls `handoff_service.clear_handoff()` before spawning the next agent via `start_agent()` or `start_command()`. Without this, the newly spawned agent or a concurrent dashboard poll may re-process the same handoff, leading to duplicate agent spawns. The clear must happen synchronously before the spawn call, not after.
- **Stale review-only handoff bypass**: a timing race can leave a developer handoff saying "spawn reviewer" after the reviewer already ran: if the per-issue handoff file was overwritten by the developer's exit handoff after the reviewer wrote its results. Two guards prevent the stuck state: (1) `compute_work_item_state` in `work_item_service.py`: if all handoff actions are in `_REVIEW_ACTION_IDS = frozenset({"review", "review_pr"})` and `sova_verdict.has_sova_review=True`, the handoff is bypassed and PR state is used directly (so "Integrate PR" is shown instead of staying stuck at "Review PR"). (2) `_process_auto_handoff` in `agent_handoff.py`: before spawning a review-action agent, calls `get_sova_review_verdict`: if a review already exists, clears the stale handoff and returns without spawning. Manual recovery: `POST /api/handoff/clear {"issue": "N"}`. PR #514.
- **Seed cross-agent data before clearing the handoff file**: when spawning an agent that needs data from the previous agent's handoff (e.g., review findings for address-review), extract the data before `clear_handoff()` and write it to the new TaskRun's `handoff_json` via `start_agent(handoff_findings=...)`. Without this, the address-review step can't find findings when: (1) the handoff file was cleared, (2) `resume_run_id` points to a non-reviewer run, and (3) the issue-level DB fallback fails. File: `sova/dashboard/services/agent_db.py:_seed_handoff_findings()`.
- **Adapter ABC contract**: `TaskAdapter` in `sova/adapters/base.py` defines 21 abstract methods: `list_tasks`, `get_task`, `get_state`, `transition_state`, `assign`, `assign_to_user`, `add_reviewer`, `add_label`, `remove_label`, `_do_post_comment`, `_do_post_pr_comment`, `_do_post_pr_review`, `_do_edit_body`, `link_pr`, `get_pr_reviews`, `get_comments`, `_do_create_issue`, `get_available_transitions`, `list_milestones`, `create_milestone`, `set_milestone`, plus `github_user` field (constructor param). Public posting methods (`post_comment`, `post_pr_comment`, `post_pr_review`, `edit_body`, `create_issue`) are concrete Template Methods that run the egress filter before delegating to `_do_*` implementations. `assign` labels the task with an agent role (`role:{agent_role}`), distinct from `assign_to_user`, which assigns a specific person (used by LDAP-based routing, see `ldap_client.py` above); `add_reviewer` requests a specific person as PR reviewer (`JiraAdapter` no-ops both PR-specific methods since Jira has no GitHub PR concept). When adding new agent capabilities that interact with the tracker, add the method to the ABC first, then implement in `GitHubAdapter`. Factory: `create_adapter(config: ProjectConfig)` (`sova/adapters/__init__.py`), dispatching on `config.task_source.type` and reading `config.github_repo`/`config.github_user`/`config.task_source.*` itself; it does not take those as separate positional arguments.
- **LLM for user-facing outputs with structured fallback**: workflow steps producing user-facing content (PR descriptions, review comments) use a focused LLM call (Sonnet, ~$0.01) with structured context (commit log, diff stats, issue body). Fallback MUST preserve available data: build a structured body from already-fetched data rather than discarding to a bare stub. Pattern: `_generate_pr_body()` + `_build_fallback_body()` in `create_pr.py`, `_run_review()` in `reviewer.py`.
- **JIRA-aware pipeline outputs**: when `task_source.is_jira`, the pipeline adapts five output points: (1) `CommitStep` appends `JIRA: https://...` instead of `Closes #N` in commit trailers. (2) `WorktreeStep` produces `feat/PROJKEY-N-slug` branch names instead of `feat/issue-N`. (3) `CreatePRStep` uses a JIRA-aware LLM prompt template (no `Closes #N` for the LLM to generate wrong) and strips branch prefixes when falling back to branch name as PR title. (4) `CreatePRStep` title prepends `[PROJECT_KEY-NUMBER]` for JIRA's GitHub integration. (5) `parse_linked_issue()` in `pr_service.py` recognizes both JIRA markdown links (`[PROJ-42](https://...)`) and plain links (`JIRA: https://.../browse/PROJ-42`). The `/pr` command template (both distributable and SOVA-specific) is also task-source-aware. Files: `commit.py`, `create_worktree.py`, `create_pr.py`, `pr_service.py`, `sova/commands/data/commands/pr.md`.
- **Rebase with LLM conflict resolution**: `rebase_with_conflict_resolution()` in `sova/git/rebase.py` rebases onto the latest base branch and uses the LLM to resolve merge conflicts. Two-level loop: outer loop iterates over conflicting commits (capped by `max_commits`, default 5, uses `for...else` to detect exhaustion), inner loop retries LLM resolution per commit (capped by `max_attempts`, default 3). This prevents a multi-commit rebase from exhausting all retry attempts on the first commit. Stashes any uncommitted changes before rebasing and pops the stash on every exit path (success, no-conflict failure, conflict abort, loop exhaustion) via a `_pop_stash` closure (the function can be called on a dirty worktree). If rebase fails with no conflicted files (e.g., uncommitted changes still present, or other git error), the original error is surfaced rather than the misleading "no rebase in progress" message from a failed `--continue`. Aborts on LLM failure or loop exhaustion so the worktree is never left in a broken rebase state. `RebaseStep` in the address-review pipeline runs this before `AddressReviewStep`. `PushStep` decides whether to force-push via a real reachability check, not `ctx.pr_number`; see the divergence-check entry below.
- **A resolution attempt is never trusted on the LLM's own report**: the single-model path (`_resolve_conflicts_with_llm`) asks an autonomous agent to fix every conflicted file and stage it with `git add`, so both halves of that instruction must be verified independently. Git alone cannot do it: `git add` on a conflicted file clears its unmerged index entry whether or not the markers were removed, after which `git diff --diff-filter=U`, `git ls-files --unmerged` and `git status` all report clean and `git rebase --continue` commits the markers into history. `_unresolved_paths()` therefore pairs the git-level unmerged check with a content scan of each previously-conflicted file, and both resolution paths (single-model retry loop and consensus) run it before `--continue`. The content scan uses `_CONFLICT_FENCE_RE` (`<<<<<<<` / `>>>>>>>` only), not the broader `_CONFLICT_MARKER_RE` that `_is_valid_resolution()` applies to LLM output: a bare `=======` line is a valid markdown setext underline, and flagging one would abort a correctly resolved rebase. `_get_conflicted_files()` returns `None` (not `[]`) when the check itself fails, so a failed check is never read as "resolved"; the retry loop aborts on it rather than paying for another resolution pass, since a `git diff` that cannot run signals a structural fault (missing worktree, unreadable index) that an LLM retry cannot fix. Every failing `git rebase --continue` is logged with the paths still unresolved; previously the post-resolution one was silent, which is why issue #777's failure could not be diagnosed from its run log. Issue #965.
- **`sync_branch` stash guard**: `sync_branch()` in `sova/git/branch.py` stashes uncommitted changes before checkout and restores them after. The `_restore_stash()` helper must be called on every exit path (early returns for worktree conflicts, RuntimeError fallback, checkout failure raise, and the normal path). Uses `git reset --hard origin/{branch}` instead of pull because sync_branch only targets the base branch.
- **Command distribution never writes through a symlink**: `Path.write_text` follows symlinks, so the three render writes in `sova/commands/distribution.py` (`_install_files`, plus both branches of `_update_files`) sent their output to the link's target rather than into the target directory whenever an installed entry was a symlink. Projects do symlink commands to share them across checkouts, a pattern the entry below documents for the worktree copy path, and those shared files were silently destroyed by any `sova install` or `sova commands sync`: the symlink stayed in place pointing at content that was now a rendered copy of some other project's command, and the manifest recorded the write as successful, so nothing surfaced the loss. All three sites now go through `_write_rendered()`, which unlinks an existing symlink before writing. That guarantees the write lands inside the target directory; the entry becomes a regular file, so the sharing is undone rather than honoured, and a `commands.symlink_replaced` warning names both the entry and what it pointed at, since the alternative is destroying a file the caller never named. One helper rather than three copies of the guard, per this repo's three-occurrence extraction rule and because SonarCloud measures duplicated lines on new code regardless of occurrence count. Extracting it also made the write a single taint sink, which SonarCloud then flagged as a path-traversal BLOCKER (`pythonsecurity:S2083`), failing the quality gate; the answer was to enforce the containment the helper already claimed rather than suppress the rule, so it validates `destination.is_relative_to(target_dir)` after the unlink (checking the literal destination, not a symlink's target) and raises rather than skipping, since a filename here is always a single component from a directory listing and a violation therefore means a collector emitted a traversal: skipping would leave the manifest recording a hash for a file never written. Mirrors the `is_relative_to` guard in `_copy_worktree_files`. Distinct from `_copy2_skip_identical()` in `sova/git/worktree.py`, which handles the other symlink hazard (`shutil.copy2` raising `SameFileError` when source and destination already resolve to the same inode) and is unchanged. Files: `sova/commands/distribution.py`. Issue #1094.
- **Inherited `.claude/` state is never the agent's own work, at two layers**: `ensure_claude_artifacts()` exists to supply `.claude/` content a worktree would otherwise lack (its docstring: projects that gitignore `.claude/` "end up with worktrees missing slash commands, rules, skills, and config"), but it implemented that with `shutil.copytree(..., dirs_exist_ok=True)`, which overwrites unconditionally. Where `.claude/commands/` and `.claude/rules/` are tracked, the worktree already holds them from its own branch checkout, so every call replaced the branch's versions with the primary checkout's. That one behaviour broke two different ways. (1) A dirty primary checkout propagated its dirt into every worktree, where `RearrangeCommitsStep.validate_output()` counted it as the agent's uncommitted work and paused the run: 17 gate failures and $32.65 of unshipped address-review work, with PR #1086 stuck four rounds behind its own fixes (#1088). (2) Worse, a primary checkout sitting on another branch silently reverted the worktree's committed `.claude/rules/*.md` to the older text, and the agent then committed that revert as its own change; this is not hypothetical, it reverted PR #1085's documentation for issue #944 and was caught only by manually diffing the fold. The fix is at both layers, deliberately: `_tracked_paths()` asks `git ls-files -- .claude` in the worktree and `ensure_claude_artifacts()` passes a `copytree(ignore=...)` callback that skips a tracked destination **only when it still exists on disk**. Tracking means the branch owns that content, which is why an existing file must not be overwritten, but a tracked path missing from disk has nothing to preserve and copying it is the repair the caller asked for. Skipping it unconditionally is actively harmful once #976's `ensure_worktree_usable()` lands: that function calls `ensure_claude_artifacts()` to repopulate, treats a still-missing `.claude/commands` as unrepairable, and falls back to `return project_dir`, running the agent in the primary checkout, which is the exact failure mode both functions exist to prevent (see the `address-pr` wrong-directory entry below). `test_tracked_but_deleted_file_is_restored` pins it (a failed or impossible `ls-files`, e.g. a non-repo target, returns the empty set so the historical copy-everything behaviour is preserved rather than silently skipping a worktree's setup), which matches the function's documented intent exactly and leaves the gitignored case untouched; and `_EXCLUDED_PATHSPECS` in `rearrange_commits.py` excludes `.claude/` and `.sova/` from both the unstaged and staged diff checks, which is what `_IGNORABLE_UNTRACKED_RE` four lines below already did for untracked files and what `DevelopStep._NON_SUBSTANTIVE_RE` already did for change detection. Keeping the gate fix as well as the sync fix is defense in depth: any other mechanism that leaves `.claude/` churn in a worktree can no longer strand a pipeline. Files: `sova/git/worktree.py`, `sova/core/steps/rearrange_commits.py`. Issue #1090.

- **Worktree `.claude/` sync tolerates symlinked shared commands**: `ensure_claude_artifacts()` mirrors `.claude/{commands,rules,agent-memory,skills}` into each worktree via `shutil.copytree(..., dirs_exist_ok=True)`. A command file that is itself a symlink to a file shared across projects (e.g. `.claude/commands/x.md -> ~/.claude/commands/x.md`) is fine on the first sync (the symlink is dereferenced into a real file), but if the worktree's own copy later becomes a symlink to that same target, `shutil.copy2` raises `SameFileError` on every re-sync (worktree reuse, post-rebase restore, the `_assert_command_exists` fallback in `llm/provider.py`) because source and destination resolve to the same inode. `copytree` is passed `copy_function=_copy2_skip_identical`, a thin wrapper that treats `SameFileError` as a no-op instead of a failure; other entries in the same directory are unaffected either way since `copytree` already isolates per-entry errors. Files: `sova/git/worktree.py`.
- **A reused worktree must prove it is usable before an agent is spawned into it, not after the agent discovers a missing command**: `_resolve_issue_worktree()`'s two lookup paths (issue-number directory, and `find_worktree_by_branch()`) used to hand back an existing directory as-is, so a worktree left over from an earlier run (created before a command existed, or with its git linkage pruned while the directory survived) was returned unchanged; the agent then spawned into it and failed deep in the pipeline with `Command /develop not found` or a bare `[Errno 2] No such file or directory`, after budget was already spent. `ensure_worktree_usable()` (`sova/git/worktree.py`) now gates both reuse paths: it checks `worktree_path.is_dir()` (via `_is_dir_safe()`, which treats an `OSError` there as "needs recreate" rather than propagating) and, if present, probes `git rev-parse --git-dir` inside it; either failure means the directory or its git linkage is gone, so it recreates the worktree via `create_worktree()` (which needs a known branch, and fails closed with `None` when one isn't available). If the worktree is structurally fine, `missing_claude_artifacts()` diffs it against `project_dir`'s `.claude/{commands,rules,agent-memory,skills}` plus `CLAUDE.md`/`settings.json`/`settings.local.json` (an artifact only counts as required when the project actually has it; a required directory is compared by entry name rather than just non-emptiness, so a worktree missing one specific command added to the project after the worktree was created is still flagged, not just a fully-empty or fully-missing directory) and repopulates in place via `ensure_claude_artifacts()`. Every filesystem probe on the worktree side of that comparison treats `OSError` (permission denied, broken mount) as missing rather than raising, fail-safe toward a repopulate/recreate attempt; a probe on the project side that raises is treated as "not required" instead, since there is nothing to repopulate from. The directory-membership comparison (`_dir_entry_names()`) also drops broken-symlink entries from its listing before the subset check: `iterdir()` returns a dangling symlink's name regardless of whether its target exists, so without this a broken symlink inside e.g. `.claude/commands` would count as present and the worktree would be handed back as usable despite the command itself being unreadable. A repopulation that still leaves artifacts missing is unrepairable, but only blocks reuse (`ensure_worktree_usable()` returns `None`) when the still-missing set includes `.claude/commands` (`_CRITICAL_CLAUDE_ARTIFACTS`): an agent cannot run without slash commands, but can still function without `rules`/`skills`/`agent-memory`/`CLAUDE.md`/settings files, so those are logged and the worktree is returned as usable anyway. Callers (`_resolve_issue_worktree()`) treat a `None` result from the issue-number lookup like "no worktree found" and fall through to the branch-based lookup; both the `candidate.is_dir()` check and the `ensure_worktree_usable()` call in that issue-number branch are wrapped in `except OSError` so a probe failure there also degrades to the branch-based fallback (or ultimately `project_dir`) instead of propagating out of `start_agent()`/`start_command()`. When `find_worktree_by_branch()` resolves to the same path already probed by the issue-number branch, it is reused instead of probed a second time, avoiding a duplicate `git rev-parse` subprocess and repopulate attempt for a result already known. For the branch-based lookup specifically, an unusable result must not fall through further into "create a fresh worktree": that logic computes its own identity independently (`issue_id`/`pr-{N}`/a sanitized branch name), which generally does not match the existing worktree's own directory name, so `create_worktree()` would try to add a second worktree for a branch git already has checked out at the original path and fail ("already used by worktree" / "a branch named X already exists"), degrading to `project_dir` anyway but via a confusing git error instead of directly. `_resolve_issue_worktree()` returns `project_dir` immediately in that case instead. Files: `sova/git/worktree.py`, `sova/dashboard/services/agent_context.py`. Issue #976.
- **Pydantic request models must be at module scope in `app.py`**: `from __future__ import annotations` makes all type annotations lazy strings. FastAPI resolves annotation strings by searching the module's global namespace. Pydantic `BaseModel` subclasses defined inside nested functions (e.g., `_setup_multi_project()`) are not in module globals, so FastAPI falls back to treating the parameter as a query param, producing 422 errors. Always define request/response models at module level. File: `sova/dashboard/app.py`.
- **Thin re-export wrappers during module splits**: when splitting a large module into submodules (e.g., `control_service.py` -> `agent_lifecycle.py` + `agent_output.py` + `agent_recovery.py`), convert the original module to a thin re-export facade (`from agent_lifecycle import X`) rather than deleting it. This preserves backward compatibility for all existing imports in routers, tests, and commands. Delete the old module only after all imports are migrated.
- **Never non-visible overflow on containers with popout children**: the sidebar `<nav>` and its scrollable child div contain absolutely-positioned children (notification panel, CSS hover tooltips) that extend beyond their boundary at `left: 100%`. ANY non-visible overflow value (`hidden`, `auto`, `scroll`) on ANY ancestor creates a clipping boundary for positioned descendants. CSS spec: when one axis is non-visible, the other is computed to `auto` even if explicitly set to `visible`: so `overflow-y: auto; overflow-x: visible` does NOT work (x becomes `auto` too). Use `max-width` and opacity transitions on individual child elements instead of overflow on the container. This applies to any fixed-position sidebar or panel that contains popout menus or tooltips.
- **Operations persona for oversight**: user-maintained `~/.config/sova/operations_persona.md` defines operational priorities for the oversight agent. No caching: read fresh each cycle so live edits take effect immediately. Single global file (not per-project) ensures consistent oversight behavior fleet-wide. Loaded into `OversightAgent._persona` and exposed via `get_system_prompt()` for LLM context injection by observation/analysis hooks (#445, #446, #447). Dashboard settings page shows a read-only preview with an "Edit" button that launches the OS default editor. Falls back to `DEFAULT_PERSONA_TEMPLATE` on missing/empty/unreadable file. File: `sova/oversight/persona.py`, `sova/oversight/agent.py`.
- **Awareness is read-only and deliberately separate from `TaskAdapter`**: `AwarenessProvider` (`sova/awareness/base.py`) is a distinct ABC from `TaskAdapter`, not a variant of it: `TaskAdapter` owns issue lifecycle (create, transition, assign, comment), while `AwarenessProvider.fetch_items()` only reads from a source (Gmail, Calendar, GitHub PRs, Apple Reminders, SOVA's own run history) and returns `AwarenessItem`s classified into an `ItemCategory` (`needs_attention`, `informational`, `schedule`, `dismissed`); it never mutates the source.
