- **Subprocess agents must resolve project root from linked worktree CWD**: when the dashboard spawns an agent into `.claude/worktrees/<id>`, `Path.cwd()` inside the subprocess is the worktree, not the project root. `get_session()`, `load_config()`, and `_adopt_task_run()` all use this path, so they write to the worktree DB and fail to adopt the dashboard's TaskRun. `sova run` uses `get_primary_worktree_root()` (`git rev-parse --git-common-dir`: absolute path = linked worktree, relative `.git` = primary) to resolve the real project root before any DB/config calls. `write_handoff()` in the subprocess still needs `project_dir` passed explicitly (it calls `get_session()` without project_dir); the dashboard backstops this via `_persist_completing_agent_handoff()` in `_process_auto_handoff()`. Files: `sova/git/worktree.py:get_primary_worktree_root`, `sova/cli/commands/run.py`, `sova/dashboard/services/agent_handoff.py`. PR #373.

- **`start_agent()` recovers pr_number from DB history for developer role**: when `recover_stale_runs()` marks an in-progress developer run as "interrupted", the `--pr` context is lost. The next `start_agent(issue=N, role="developer")` without an explicit `pr_number` would fail at `AssessStep` (open PR detected). `_recover_last_pr_number()` queries the most recent `{"interrupted","failed","paused"}` developer `TaskRun` (NOT "done": done runs may have merged PRs) with non-null `pr_number` and forwards it. Called outside `pa._lock` (read-only). File: `sova/dashboard/services/agent_lifecycle.py:_recover_last_pr_number`. PR #373.

- **`PR_CHANGES_REQUESTED` split into `PR_SOVA_CHANGES` and `PR_EXTERNAL_CHANGES`**: the unified state conflated two different response paths. `PR_SOVA_CHANGES` is returned when `get_sova_review_verdict()` says revise/block: routes to the developer agent (address_review pipeline with structured handoff). `PR_EXTERNAL_CHANGES` is returned when GitHub `review_decision == "CHANGES_REQUESTED"` from CodeRabbit or a human reviewer with no SOVA override: routes to the `/address-pr` command (thread resolution). A standing SOVA revise/block verdict always wins over an external `CHANGES_REQUESTED`, because `resolve_next_action()` evaluates the SOVA verdict rule before the external-changes rule (see the next-action resolver entry below). `PR_CHANGES_REQUESTED` was removed once the resolver ladder no longer returned it (issue #1111). Config-aware: `external_reviews.enabled = false` (default) means CI-passed PRs with no SOVA review show `PR_AWAITING_REVIEW` ("Review" primary) instead of `PR_SOVA_PENDING` ("Address PR" primary), skipping the inline-comment step for bot-free projects. File: `sova/dashboard/services/work_state.py`. PR #378.

- **Review-completed gate: three-source check plus thread resolution and run-completion requirements**: `sova/supervisor/gates/review_completed.py` blocks `SPAWN_INTEGRATE` unless a review exists via one of three sources checked in order: a `sova:*` issue label, a completed reviewer `TaskRun` (via `get_sova_review_verdict()`), or a non-bot human GitHub approval. Bot detection for the third source keys off `author.login` (`[bot]` suffix or `DEFAULT_CODERABBIT_AUTHORS`), not a `type`/`__typename` field, because `gh pr list --json latestReviews` never returns one. The DB source requires `run_status == "done"` (exposed by `get_sova_review_verdict()`), not just the presence of a verdict: a failed or interrupted reviewer run can still carry `handoff_json` (crash after writing findings) but never finished, so it must not satisfy this gate. Independently of all three sources, the gate blocks whenever the PR has unresolved review threads (`get_unresolved_thread_count()` from the enriched PR data), closing the "SOVA approved but CodeRabbit/human threads still open" gap. `resolve_next_action()` (see the next-action resolver entry below) mirrors this for the dashboard via the same rule: an "approve" verdict on a PR with unresolved threads computes to `PR_EXTERNAL_CHANGES` rather than `PR_READY_TO_MERGE` (the `PR_APPROVED` state this sentence used to name was itself removed as unreachable in issue #1111), driving the "Address PR" button instead of "Integrate". Only gates supervisor autonomy; the dashboard's "Integrate" button stays available for human-initiated integration. `AgentHandoff.metadata["finding_summary"]` (severity-bucketed counts built by `_build_finding_summary()` in `reviewer.py`) is still recorded and surfaced to the LLM suggestion service (`_format_sova_context()`), but no longer drives a dashboard state: the originally-planned `WorkItemState.PR_APPROVED_WITH_FINDINGS` was removed as unreachable dead code in PR #892, since `ReviewerRole._write_handoff()` sets `next_action="address_review"` for *any* non-empty actionable finding regardless of severity, so the verdict is never `"approve"` when `finding_summary.actionable > 0`. Reaching a finding-aware approved state would require wiring a severity threshold into `_write_handoff()` first; not currently planned. Files: `sova/supervisor/gates/review_completed.py`, `sova/roles/reviewer.py`, `sova/dashboard/services/work_state.py`, `sova/dashboard/services/agent_recovery.py`. Issue #850, PR #892.

- **Integration gates default to on, and the label source of the review-completed gate excludes explicit rejections**: (superseded by the next entry; `IntegrationGatesConfig` and `check_integration_gates()` named below no longer exist). `IntegrationGatesConfig`'s `ci_passed`, `sova_reviewed`, and `threads_resolved` now default to `True` (previously `False`); `coderabbit_reviewed` stays `False` since CodeRabbit does not auto-review this repo (see "External Reviews" in `.claude/rules/workflow.md`). A project that sets nothing in `[integration_gates]` now blocks `/integrate-pr`-driven integration on CI, SOVA review, and thread resolution by default; an explicit `false` in `sova.toml` still overrides the new default. `sova/dashboard/settings_meta.py`'s descriptions carry an explicit `(on by default)`/`(off by default)` suffix per gate, cross-checked against `IntegrationGatesConfig()`'s actual field values by `test_integration_gates_description_default_annotation_matches_config` in `tests/test_settings_meta.py` so a future default change cannot silently leave the dashboard settings UI showing a stale annotation. Separately, `review_completed.py`'s label source (`_has_sova_label()`) now checks for `sova:approved` only: `sova:revise`/`sova:block` previously satisfied source 1 too (any `sova:*` label was accepted), letting a PR whose review explicitly rejected it still authorize `SPAWN_INTEGRATE`. This does not extend to source 2 (`_has_reviewer_run()`), which stays deliberately verdict-blind: see the "Known residual gap" note in the module docstring, deferred to issue #991's next-action resolver work rather than fixed here, since a genuine human GitHub approval (source 3) posted after a rejecting label or run must still authorize integration (the gate fails to satisfy on a rejection, it does not veto). `check_integration_gates()` (`sova/dashboard/services/pr_service.py`) gained an optional `project_dir` parameter (previously the SOVA-review sub-check queried `get_sova_review_verdict()` with no project scope, defaulting to whatever project the ambient contextvar resolved to rather than the project the gate check was actually being run for) and an optional `sova_verdict` parameter: `_attach_integration_gates()` (`work_item_service.py`) forwards the verdict it already resolved via `resolve_sova_verdict()` for the batch work-item listing, so the gate that enables the Integrate button reads the same verdict that decided the button exists, and avoids a second per-item DB lookup on every dashboard poll. The standalone single-PR endpoint (`GET /api/prs/{pr_number}/gates` in `sova/dashboard/routers/prs.py`) previously left `sova_verdict` unset, falling back to `check_integration_gates()`'s own DB-only `get_sova_review_verdict()` call, which does not consult the `sova:*` label or the cross-instance GitHub review marker scan the way `resolve_sova_verdict()` does, so the single-PR view and the list view could disagree about whether integration was authorized for the same PR. `_resolve_gate_verdict()` (`prs.py`) closes this gap by routing the standalone endpoint through `resolve_sova_verdict()` as well, best-effort fetching the linked issue's labels via the configured adapter's `get_task()` (non-fatal: an adapter failure falls back to an empty label list and no GitHub fallback, matching `resolve_sova_verdict()`'s own DB-only path) so both endpoints share one verdict-assembly path. Files: `sova/config/models.py`, `sova/dashboard/settings_meta.py`, `sova/supervisor/gates/review_completed.py`, `sova/dashboard/services/pr_service.py`, `sova/dashboard/services/work_item_service.py`, `sova/dashboard/routers/prs.py`. Issue #993.

- **`IntegrationGatesConfig` and its three independent evaluators: REMOVED.** `check_integration_gates()` (`sova/dashboard/services/pr_service.py`), the `GET /api/prs/{pr_number}/gates` endpoint, `_attach_integration_gates()` (`work_item_service.py`), the `integration_gates.*` settings, and the merge-conflict supervisor gate (`sova/supervisor/gates/merge_conflict.py`, plus `get_pr_mergeability_map()`, `TaskProgressionEngine._fetch_mergeability_map()` whose only caller it was, and the `all_conflict` branch in `_evaluate_single()` that turned a `gate="conflict"` blocker into `SPAWN_REBASE`: with the gate deleted no gate emits that name, so the branch was unreachable and auto-rebase now arrives only as a refined `SPAWN_REBASE` candidate from the resolver's `conflicting` rule) are all gone: three independently-computed answers to "can this PR integrate" is what rule 219 ("One shared next-action resolver replaces two independently-computed state machines") already argued against for the state itself, and the same argument applies to the action that follows from it. `resolve_next_action()` (`sova/dashboard/services/work_state.py`) is now the sole source of truth for whether Integrate is offered: every condition the deleted gates checked except one already exists as a resolver ladder rule (CI passed: `ci_failed`/`ci_running`; SOVA reviewed: `sova_standing_changes`/`no_sova_review`; threads resolved: `external_changes_or_unresolved_threads`; no merge conflict: `conflicting`). `coderabbit_reviewed` has no resolver equivalent and is intentionally dropped rather than ported: it defaulted to `False` (CodeRabbit does not auto-review this repo below 10 GitHub stars, see "External Reviews" in `.claude/rules/workflow.md`), so no project was relying on it, and reintroducing a dedicated per-reviewer toggle would reinstate exactly the multi-evaluator checklist this PR removes. A project that had explicitly opted in (`integration_gates.coderabbit_reviewed = true`) loses that guard silently: the row in its `sova.db` is simply ignored (see below). The disabled-button UX (`_renderGateBlockedBtn()` in `agents.html`, keyed off a `gate_result` the backend no longer populates) is replaced by an always-clickable secondary Integrate action: `Resolution.merge_blockers` (the unmet conditions from the existing `_unmet_merge_conditions()` helper, already computed for the `ready_to_merge` rule) is attached only to the secondary Integrate action (`_attach_merge_blockers()`) and never to the primary one, since the primary path (`PR_READY_TO_MERGE`, `PR_REVIEW_EXHAUSTED`) is only reached once those conditions are already satisfied or the address-review budget is exhausted. The frontend's `runCommandWithMergeBlockers()` shows a native `confirm()` listing each blocker before running `integrate-pr` when the list is non-empty, and runs immediately otherwise. `WorkItemState.PR_CHANGES_REQUESTED` and `PR_APPROVED` were removed alongside the gates: the resolver ladder had already superseded both (see the `PR_CHANGES_REQUESTED` split entry above and rule 219), so they were unreachable dead code. `integration_gates.*` rows already persisted in a project's `sova.db` are left in place and ignored (Pydantic `extra="ignore"`); no migration was written. Deleting `get_pr_mergeability_map()`/`_fetch_mergeability_map()` is not a pure no-op: the old map aggregated conflict state across *every* open PR linked to an issue (CONFLICTING wins if any linked PR conflicts), while the resolver's `conflicting` rule only ever sees the single PR `_find_pr_for_issue()` picks. For the ordinary case (one open PR per issue) nothing changes; for an issue with two open PRs, a stale conflicting one plus a clean one, the supervisor now evaluates only the PR it would actually integrate and can auto-integrate where it previously parked the issue on the aggregate conflict. This is a deliberate narrowing (evaluate the PR you're about to act on, not every PR that happens to reference the issue), not a preserved behavior. Files: `sova/config/models.py`, `sova/config/__init__.py`, `sova/config/loader.py`, `sova/dashboard/settings_meta.py`, `sova/dashboard/services/pr_service.py`, `sova/dashboard/services/work_item_service.py`, `sova/dashboard/services/work_state.py`, `sova/dashboard/routers/prs.py`, `sova/dashboard/templates/agents.html`, `sova/supervisor/progression.py`, `sova/supervisor/gates/merge_conflict.py` (deleted). Issue #1111.

- **Unresolved-thread state is three-valued, but only an explicit unknown fails closed**: `get_unresolved_thread_count()` (`pr_service.py`) returns `None` only when `pr_data` is a dict whose `thread_total`/`thread_resolved` keys are explicitly `None` (fetch succeeded but the count is genuinely unusable); it returns `0` both when those keys are simply absent (a synthetic test dict, an older cache shape) and when `pr_data` itself is `None` (no PR data fetched at all, e.g. a transient GraphQL failure in the supervisor's best-effort enrichment step). Treating "missing" and "not fetched" as `0` rather than `None` matters because the review-completed gate (`check_review_completed_gate()` in `sova/supervisor/gates/review_completed.py`) short-circuits to a hard block the moment thread state is `None`, before ever consulting its three review sources (`sova:*` label, completed reviewer run, human approval): reclassifying every missing-`pr_data` case as unknown would block `SPAWN_INTEGRATE` on transient fetch failures alone, with no fallback. Only a `pr_data` dict that was actually fetched but carries explicit `None` thread keys is truly unknown and fail-closed. This was originally fail-neutral for the dashboard (`_apply_sova_verdict()` held the pre-verdict state on `None`) versus fail-closed for the supervisor, a split #991's unified `resolve_next_action()` deliberately removed: its `thread_signal` field collapses `None`/`0`/`>0` into `"unknown"`/`"clear"`/`"pending"` once at the fact-building boundary (`_thread_signal()` in `work_state.py`), and rule 11 of the ladder treats `"unknown"` exactly like `"pending"` (routes to `PR_EXTERNAL_CHANGES`, never to an integrate action) for both the dashboard and the supervisor, since both now share the same resolver (see the next-action resolver entry below). Separately, a bot `CHANGES_REQUESTED` review with zero or fully-resolved threads only unblocks (`_should_unblock_bot_reviews()`/`compute_pr_state()` in `pr_service.py`) when `PRReviewData.bot_cr_superseded` is `True`, computed in `_fetch_pr_review_data()` (`sova/git/pr.py`) from the same batched aliased GraphQL query `get_pr_review_data()` already issues (`headRefOid` plus `reviews: latestOpinionatedReviews(last:10)`, requesting `state` on each node): reviews are grouped by bot login, keeping only each bot's most recent CR review, and `bot_cr_superseded` is `True` only when every distinct bot with an outstanding CR review has a known (non-empty) commit oid that differs from `headRefOid`. The connection was originally `reviews(last:10, states:[CHANGES_REQUESTED])`, but a GraphQL `states` filter matches each review's own immutable state, not "this author's current opinion": a bot that later submits an APPROVED review does not retract its earlier CHANGES_REQUESTED one, so the state-filtered connection kept surfacing that stale review as if it were still current, and a coincidental head-matching SHA on the newer (invisible) approval could hold `bot_cr_superseded` at `False` forever. `latestOpinionatedReviews` returns each author's single latest APPROVED-or-CHANGES_REQUESTED review regardless of state (no `states` argument exists on that connection), so the code filters client-side (`rev.get("state") != "CHANGES_REQUESTED": continue`) before grouping, keeping only bots whose latest opinion is still an outstanding change request. Issue #989, PR #1022 CodeRabbit. If any bot's latest review has an unknown/empty oid, the whole PR is treated as not-superseded regardless of the other bots (fail closed on the unknown one), since GitHub does not auto-dismiss bot reviews on push and an unresolvable bot's status relative to head cannot be determined. `bot_cr_commit_sha` (a single most-recent-known sha) is retained only for backward-compatible display; it is not sufficient on its own to decide supersession when multiple bots have outstanding CR reviews. Human `CHANGES_REQUESTED` reviews are never auto-superseded by this mechanism. Bot identification reuses the `[bot]`-suffix-or-`DEFAULT_CODERABBIT_AUTHORS` convention established above. Both GraphQL connections in this query are capped (`reviewThreads(first:100)`, `reviews(last:10, ...)`) and each carries a `pageInfo` field so truncation can be detected rather than silently miscounted: if `reviewThreads.pageInfo.hasNextPage` is `True`, `totalCount` covers threads beyond the 100 fetched nodes, so a `total - resolved` count could be wrong in either direction, and the whole PR's `PRReviewData` is set to `None` (unknown) rather than reporting a possibly-incorrect number. If `reviews.pageInfo.hasPreviousPage` is `True`, an older bot's still-outstanding CR review may have been pushed out of the last-10 window, so `bot_cr_superseded` is forced `False` regardless of what the visible bots' SHAs show (a truncated reviews window cannot rule out an unaccounted-for bot). Both cases fail closed: unknown thread state blocks `SPAWN_INTEGRATE` via `check_review_completed_gate()`, and a forced `bot_cr_superseded=False` keeps `_should_unblock_bot_reviews()` from unblocking. Files: `sova/git/pr.py`, `sova/dashboard/services/pr_service.py`, `sova/supervisor/gates/review_completed.py`. Issue #989.

- **One shared next-action resolver replaces two independently-computed state machines**: before #991, the dashboard (`_apply_sova_verdict()`) and the supervisor (`_refine_in_review_action()`'s own `if has_review and verdict in (...)` branching) each answered "what's next for this PR?" from a different subset of the same facts and could disagree. `resolve_next_action(facts: PRFacts) -> Resolution` in `sova/dashboard/services/work_state.py` is now the single source of truth: a pure, synchronous, first-match-wins ladder with no I/O and no imports of `pr_service`, `agent_recovery`, or any DB/session module inside its call path. `PRFacts` is a frozen dataclass snapshot (`running_agent`, `pr_state`, `is_draft`, `mergeable`, `merge_state`, `ci_status`, `head_sha`, `sova_verdict`, `sova_verdict_sha`, `sova_verdict_addressed`, `external_changes_requested`, `thread_signal`, `external_reviews_enabled`) built by the caller; `Resolution` carries the resolved `state`, an `action_id` (`"integrate"`, `"address_review"`, `"rebase"`, `"review_pr"`, `"address_pr"`, or `None`), and a `reason_chain` tuple naming every rule evaluated in order, truncated at the match (each branch appends its own name immediately before checking its condition, so a rule after the match is never evaluated or recorded). The 14-rule order is: agent running, merged, conflicting (mergeable == `"CONFLICTING"` or GitHub's own `merge_state == "DIRTY"` always wins over any verdict, per #990 and #1109, and routes to `"rebase"`, never integrate), draft, CI failed, CI pending/running, standing revise/block on the current head, a verdict anchored to a stale SHA falls through to "no current review" rather than acting on superseded findings, external changes requested or an unresolved/unknown thread signal (`"unknown"` is deliberately treated the same as `"pending"`, never as `"clear"`: fail closed, since a truncated or failed GraphQL fetch must never be read as "safe to integrate"; this rule sits ahead of the two review_pr routes below because open threads and a bot CHANGES_REQUESTED are concrete work a review would only re-discover, and on PR #1070 a CodeRabbit CHANGES_REQUESTED posted nine minutes after the address round was hidden behind "Review Addressed" while its two unresolved threads gated integration), an address cycle completed after the review routes to `PR_REVIEW_ADDRESSED` regardless of what the stale verdict said, no SOVA review at all, `merge_blocked_by_github` (GitHub's `mergeStateStatus` is `"BLOCKED"`, e.g. branch protection awaiting a required approval, routes to `PR_AWAITING_REVIEW` rather than a dedicated state, and deliberately carries **no** `action_id`: `ReviewerRole._post_review()` always posts with `event="COMMENT"`, so a re-review can never satisfy a required-approval rule nor turn a failing non-required check green, and emitting `"review_pr"` here would let the supervisor spawn a reviewer every poll cycle on a PR no review can unblock; a `None` action_id routes it to `CHECKPOINT_NEEDED` for a human while the dashboard's buttons are unaffected, since those come from `_get_actions(state)`), the single approve+clear+green+mergeable path to `PR_READY_TO_MERGE`, and a final fallback to `PR_AWAITING_REVIEW`. `_unmet_merge_conditions()`/`_github_will_merge()` (#1109) treat `ci_status == "none"` as neutral rather than blocking (a repository with no CI configured, e.g. Gwym where Actions is billing-blocked, must still be able to reach `PR_READY_TO_MERGE`), and resolve mergeability through `mergeStateStatus` first (`CLEAN`/`BEHIND`/`HAS_HOOKS`/`UNSTABLE` merge, since `UNSTABLE` per GitHub's documented semantics means only non-required checks are failing and the merge button still works; `BLOCKED`/`DIRTY`/`DRAFT` do not), falling back to the older `mergeable` field only when `merge_state` is `"UNKNOWN"` or unfetched (GitHub returns `UNKNOWN` while still recomputing after a push). `_check_ci_gate()` (`sova/dashboard/services/pr_service.py`) reads `"none"` the same way, because the `ci_passed` integration gate is what enables the Integrate button: without that alignment a no-CI repository reaches `PR_READY_TO_MERGE` and then renders its primary action permanently greyed out, which is worse than the `PR_AWAITING_REVIEW` it used to show. An empty or absent `ci_status` still fails the gate, since unknown is not the same as absent. `compute_work_item_state()` is now a thin adapter: it builds a `PRFacts` via `_build_pr_facts()` (translating `pr_data`/`sova_verdict` dicts, including collapsing the three-valued thread count from `_thread_signal()`) and delegates entirely to `resolve_next_action()`; it contains no verdict/threshold branching of its own. `_apply_sova_verdict()`, `_is_verdict_stale_by_sha()`, `_INTEGRATE_STATES`, and `_VERDICT_OVERRIDEABLE` were deleted rather than kept as unused wrappers. On the supervisor side, `_refine_in_review_action()` fetches the same enriched PR data via `_fetch_enriched_pr()` (returning `CHECKPOINT_NEEDED` if enrichment fails, since facts cannot be built safely without it), assembles the verdict through the same shared `resolve_sova_verdict()` the dashboard uses (passing the issue labels it already holds, so the label source is available to both callers; see the SOVA review verdict entry above, a shared resolver is not enough on its own if the two callers feed it differently-assembled verdicts), builds the identical `PRFacts` shape via the shared `_build_pr_facts()`, calls `resolve_next_action()`, and maps `Resolution.action_id` through `_ACTION_ID_TO_PROGRESSION`, a single table pairing each action_id with its `ProgressionAction` and the `SupervisorConfig` flag gating it (`"integrate"` -> `SPAWN_INTEGRATE`/`auto_integrate`, `"address_review"` -> `SPAWN_ADDRESS_REVIEW`/`auto_address_review`, `"address_pr"` -> `SPAWN_ADDRESS_PR`/`auto_address_review`, `"review_pr"` -> `SPAWN_REVIEWER`/`auto_review`, `"rebase"` -> `SPAWN_REBASE`/`auto_rebase`; only a `None` action_id, i.e. agent running, merged, draft or CI running, falls back to `CHECKPOINT_NEEDED`), so the resolver decides *what* the action would be while config still decides whether the supervisor may take it autonomously; this is also where the previously-missing `auto_rebase` gate was added directly to `_refine_in_review_action()` (it existed only in the separate gate-blocker fallback before). `PRFacts.running_agent` is always `False` on both call sites today (the dashboard checks its own running-agent map before ever building `PRFacts`; the supervisor relies on the existing downstream `check_already_running()` gate) since a wrong `False` here safely falls through to the real rules while a wrong `True` would mask everything. A golden-table test (`TestResolveNextAction` in `tests/test_work_item_service.py`) parameterizes every named scenario against `(state, action_id)`, plus a dedicated assertion that `thread_signal == "unknown"` never resolves like `"clear"` and that `reason_chain` truncates at the match. Rule 3 routes to the `WorkItemState.PR_CONFLICTED` state `#990` had already shipped by the time this resolver landed (see the `ComputedPRState` entry below): its UI action stayed the deterministic "Rebase" button wired to `POST /work/issue/{issue}/rebase` (`attempt_auto_rebase()`), not `/integrate-pr`, since only rule 3's `action_id="rebase"` needed to line up with `_ACTION_ID_TO_PROGRESSION`'s existing `SPAWN_REBASE`/`auto_rebase` mapping for the supervisor side to agree with the dashboard button. Every new `WorkItemState` needs registering client-side as well as in `_STATE_LABELS`/`_STATE_COLORS`/`_STATE_SORT_ORDER`/`_get_actions`: `agents.html` keeps its own `_STATE_FILTER_GROUPS.pr_stage` list and `_PR_STAGE_STATES` set, and a state missing from them is dropped from the PR-stage filter and sorted into the bottom tier by `_stateSortTier()`. `PRFacts` is `kw_only=True` precisely because it keeps growing by insertion (`merge_state` landed in the middle of the field list, not at the end): with keyword-only fields, two same-typed `str` fields in sequence can never silently swap values at a positional call site, a property that would otherwise have to be re-verified by hand every time a field is added. The `ci_status == "none"` reclassification has one downstream side effect outside this resolver: `sova/supervisor/pr_monitor.py`'s `_NOTIFY_STATES` mapping (unchanged) selects `notify_on_ready_to_merge` instead of `notify_on_approval` for an approved, mergeable, CI-less PR, since `compute_pr_state()` now computes that combination to `APPROVED_CI_GREEN` rather than `APPROVED`; pinned by `test_ci_less_approved_pr_fires_ready_to_merge_not_approval` in `tests/test_pr_monitor.py`. `compute_pr_state()` independently derives `ComputedPRState` from the same raw gh fields (it does not call `resolve_next_action()`), so it needed its own `merge_state` parameter: every `APPROVED_CI_GREEN` return is gated on `merge_state not in {"BLOCKED", "DIRTY"}`, so an approved, CI-less PR that GitHub itself reports `BLOCKED` (e.g. branch protection awaiting a required review) computes to `APPROVED` and never fires `notify_on_ready_to_merge`; pinned by `test_approved_no_ci_blocked_by_github_is_not_green` in `tests/test_pr_service.py` and `test_blocked_ci_less_approved_pr_does_not_fire_ready_to_merge` in `tests/test_pr_monitor.py` (CodeRabbit, PR #1114). The LLM shadow-evaluation suggestion request (`POST /prs/{pr}/suggestion`, `sova/dashboard/services/llm_suggestion_service.py`) also threads `merge_state` alongside `mergeable` in both its request schema (`PRSuggestionRequest` in `sova/dashboard/routers/prs.py`) and its prompt, and `agents.html`'s suggestion payload sends `prDetails.merge_state`, so the LLM's independent guess sees the same primary mergeability signal `_github_will_merge()` uses rather than just the superseded `mergeable` field. `llm_suggestion_service.py`'s read-through cache key (`_make_cache_key()`) includes both `merge_state` and `mergeable`, not `merge_state` alone: `_github_will_merge()`'s own fallback reads `mergeable` whenever `merge_state` is `"UNKNOWN"`, so a PR shifting `mergeable` from `UNKNOWN` to `MERGEABLE` while `merge_state` stayed `UNKNOWN` would otherwise serve a suggestion reasoned from the stale fallback value for up to the 5-minute TTL. `PRSuggestionRequest.merge_state` is validated via a `field_validator` against `work_state.VALID_MERGE_STATES` (the public union of `_MERGE_STATES_MERGEABLE`/`_MERGE_STATES_UNMERGEABLE` plus `"UNKNOWN"`/`""`), rejecting any value outside GitHub's documented `mergeStateStatus` vocabulary before it reaches the suggestion prompt. Files: `sova/dashboard/services/work_state.py`, `sova/dashboard/services/work_item_service.py`, `sova/supervisor/progression.py`, `sova/supervisor/pr_monitor.py`, `sova/dashboard/services/llm_suggestion_service.py`, `sova/dashboard/routers/prs.py`, `sova/dashboard/templates/agents.html`. Epic #986, issue #991, depends on #987-#990. CodeRabbit (PR #1114, issue #1109).

- **Dual-evaluation PR state experiment**: `ActionFeedback` DB table stores LLM suggestions alongside deterministic action choices plus user ground-truth clicks. Architecture: (1) `llm_suggestion_service.py` asks the configured provider via `sova.llm.client.invoke()` (the same choke point every other provider-routed caller uses, so cost is recorded and the call counts against the runaway-call guard) for the cheap (Haiku) tier, resolved per backend through `resolve_alias()` rather than pinned, and only when `is_anthropic_capable(cfg.llm)` holds, so an advisory widget can never reach a vendor the operator did not choose and a config-load failure fails closed (#924); the call is made `isolated=True` so the default `claude-code` provider runs it `--safe-mode --tools ""` (no CLAUDE.md/hook/MCP discovery, no tools), and a module-level `asyncio.Semaphore(3)` bounds how many calls can be in flight at once (the dashboard fans out one request per PR card); returns `{action_id, action_label, reasoning, disagrees}`, caches 5 min per `(pr_number|state|computed_state)`; (2) `POST /api/prs/{pr_number}/suggestion` serves suggestions lazily (204 when provider fails); (3) `POST /api/prs/feedback` writes `ActionFeedback` records; (4) work items include `sova_context: {has_sova_review, verdict}` for the JS to pass to the suggestion endpoint; (5) JS fires suggestions after card render, injects inline widget only when `disagrees=True`, clears stale suggestions on state change. The accumulated `ActionFeedback` rows are the ground truth for improving the deterministic model. Files: `sova/dashboard/services/llm_suggestion_service.py`, `sova/dashboard/routers/prs.py`, `sova/db/models.py:ActionFeedback`, `sova/dashboard/templates/agents.html`. Migration: 021. PR #399.

- **A documentation push must not re-run the expensive CI suite, and the whole-PR test cannot deliver that**: issue #857 gated the expensive jobs on "is every file in this pull request markdown", which only ever fires on a pure documentation PR. On a code-carrying PR the diff still holds the code pushed earlier, so the answer is `code=true` forever and every integrate-time documentation amend paid a full suite. Measured on PR #1003: the final push added only `.claude/agent-memory/cookbook.md` and `.claude/rules/architecture.md` on top of an identical code commit (same parent), and still burned CI (15 min) plus SonarCloud (15 min), delaying the merge by 16 minutes. PR #1002 did it twice in three minutes. At roughly 30 billable job-minutes per amend this was the dominant consumer of the 2000-minute monthly budget. The fix adds a second layer, **delta-since-last-green**, in the shared `.github/scripts/detect-code-changes.sh` (`detect` mode for the workflows, `classify` mode for tests and local debugging, `--help` per the `.github/scripts/` contract). It resolves the head SHA of the most recent successful run of *its own* workflow on this branch and skips the suite when nothing but markdown differs from that commit. **The comparison is between TREES, never commit topology or a commit range.** Documentation reaches a branch via `commit --amend` plus a force-push, which produces a *sibling* of the tested commit, not a descendant: `compare/A...B` reports `diverged` on precisely the case this exists to catch, and being three-dot it walks back to the merge base and reports the whole amended commit instead of what the amend changed (verified against `78e9e2be...03d26604`). Two `git/trees/{sha}?recursive=1` listings diffed on `(path, blob sha)` via `sort | uniq -u` answer the real question exactly, match `git diff --name-only A B`, and need no checkout, which also keeps the script safe to run from `pull_request_target`. Every failure path fails open (`no-files`, `no-baseline`, `baseline-unusable`, `bad-input`, `non-pr`); only `docs-only-pr`, `docs-only-delta` and `identical` may skip, and `tests/test_ci_docs_only.py` pins that set so a new skip path cannot be added silently. A truncated tree listing fails open because it cannot prove nothing else changed. JSON never passes through `echo` (backslash expansion in the tree payload turns valid JSON into a parse error); every hop uses `printf '%s'`. **Soundness does not depend on the ruleset**: a skip re-uses a green result produced against whatever the base branch held when it ran, and layer 2 only fires when the tree is byte-identical to the last green one apart from markdown, so it reuses a result a re-run of the test job would have reproduced (that job checks out the pull request head tree, not a merge with the base branch). `main-protection` was raised to `strict_required_status_checks_policy: true` in #1005 and deliberately turned back off on 2026-10-14 (#1188): forcing every open PR to update and re-run CI after each merge cost more than it protected, so a PR that is only behind main merges as it stands, skipped or not. The remaining risk is a semantic conflict between two individually green PRs. It is caught after merge, by the test job running on every push to main, and #1188 tracks the red-main alert. #1207's audit read the `false` as drift and an early revision of that work added a `changes` job step that failed whenever the policy was `false`; it was removed because it contradicted the decision above and because a failure there is not non-blocking: a failed `changes` job fails the whole `CI` run, `sonarcloud.yml` then reports the required `SonarCloud Analysis` check as `CI did not succeed`, and every PR is blocked. Any future check on the ruleset must not fail the `CI` workflow run. **Critical constraint, unchanged**: the required checks (Python Lint, Python Tests, Integration Test, SonarCloud Analysis, Static Checks) must always RUN and report success (a blanket `paths-ignore` skips the job entirely, leaving the required check pending forever and blocking the merge). Of those, `python-lint`, `python-test-matrix` and `integration` in `ci.yml` consult the detector and skip their expensive *steps*, gating on `needs.changes.outputs.code != 'false'` with `if: always()` at job level so a failed or skipped gate never strands the PR. `python-test-matrix` runs its suite once per Python version (3.12 and 3.14 on a pull request; 3.12 only on a push to main) via `make test-ci`; because a matrixed job's own check name gets a `(<matrix value>)` suffix from GitHub, a separate, unmatrixed `python-test` job reports that matrix job's aggregate `needs.python-test-matrix.result` and alone keeps the required `Python Tests` name. `Static Checks`, `Secrets Scan` and `Invariants` stay ungated regardless: markdown can still carry a leaked credential, a banned double dash, or broken command frontmatter. `ci.yml`'s `changes` job fetches the detector script itself from the base branch through the contents API into `RUNNER_TEMP`, rather than checking out the repository (the script only ever calls `gh api`, so a full checkout buys nothing), falling back to `reason=detector-unavailable` plus a full run if that fetch fails. This is only defense-in-depth against a narrower vector, a fork PR quietly editing just the script: GitHub resolves this entire workflow's YAML from the fork's own head ref under plain `pull_request`, so a fork PR can already edit this job's `if:` gates directly, a property shared by every `pull_request`-triggered job in this file (none of which touch secrets or gain write access). The job needs `actions: read` for the baseline lookup. `sonarcloud.yml` no longer runs this detector at all (#1187): see the dedicated entry below for how it now decides whether to scan. Files: `.github/scripts/detect-code-changes.sh`, `.github/workflows/ci.yml`, `Makefile`, `tests/test_ci_docs_only.py`. Issues #857, #1005, #1188, #1207.

- **SonarCloud consumes CI's coverage artifact via `workflow_run` instead of running its own copy of the test suite**: before #1187, `sonarcloud.yml` triggered on `pull_request_target`/`push` and ran its own `pip install` + `pytest --cov` just to produce `sonar.python.coverage.reportPaths` input, duplicating the ~15 minute suite `ci.yml`'s `python-test` job had already run for the same commit. The fix retriggers `sonarcloud.yml` on `workflow_run: { workflows: ["CI"], types: [completed] }` (matched by `ci.yml`'s declared `name: CI`, not its filename) and downloads the `coverage-xml` artifact `python-test` already uploaded, via `actions/download-artifact@v4` with `run-id: ${{ github.event.workflow_run.id }}` and `continue-on-error: true`, so the terminal reporter below (not an aborted job) decides what a failed download means. **`python-test` publishes that artifact on every run whose earlier steps succeeded, including the docs-only path where no tests ran** (a run that fails earlier reports job conclusion != success, caught by the reporter's own CI-conclusion check before it ever looks at the artifact), carrying a `no-coverage.marker` instead of a `coverage.xml` there, and with `if-no-files-found: error` so a run that tested and produced neither file fails loudly. The marker exists because the download's own outcome cannot distinguish "CI deliberately had nothing to measure" from "the artifact expired or could not be fetched": the first version treated every non-success download as the docs-only case, which passed the required `SonarCloud Analysis` check green on an expired artifact with SonarCloud never having run (CodeRabbit, PR #1218). The scan job therefore reads the presence of `coverage.xml` off the downloaded files in its own `Classify the downloaded artifact` step, and a failed download is now reported as a failure. `python-test` was also changed to run on `push` to `main` (previously skipped there), since a push-triggered scan needs an artifact to consume too, and to check out `github.event.pull_request.head.sha || github.sha` rather than the default `pull_request` merge commit: SonarCloud analyzes `workflow_run.head_sha`, which is the PR head, so coverage measured on the merge tree would report line numbers from a revision the scan never saw. The merge-result signal that gives up is recovered by `/integrate-pr`, which rebases onto `main` and re-runs CI before merging. `workflow_run` always executes in the base repository's context, with repository secrets available, even when the triggering CI run came from a fork PR; no fork code is EXECUTED in the SonarCloud job, whose only use of the checked-out `workflow_run.head_sha` is as read-only input to a static scan. That workspace is still fork CONTENT on a fork PR, and the scanner reads `sonar-project.properties` out of it, which is why one property is pinned on the command line instead (see `SONAR_HOST_URL` below). Because this job runs as a different workflow than the PR's own checks, GitHub's automatic job-status check would land on this workflow's own trigger commit (the default branch), not the PR head, so the required `"SonarCloud Analysis"` check is instead created `in_progress` as the job's **very first step** and completed explicitly via the Checks API (`checks: write`, `POST`/`PATCH repos/{repo}/check-runs`) against `github.event.workflow_run.head_sha` directly. "Early" is not good enough and "before the first conditional step" is not either: the terminal reporter is gated on that step's output, so any step placed ahead of it that fails (the PR-resolution step originally sat there, and it calls `gh api`) aborts the job with no check created at all, which blocks the PR on "Expected: waiting for status" exactly as surely as a check left pending. The job is also deliberately named `Scan`, not `SonarCloud Analysis`: GitHub auto-creates a check run named after the job, and on a push to `main` that automatic check lands on the same commit as the explicit one, so sharing the name would put two same-named checks on one commit, with the automatic one reporting success even on a failed quality gate (the scan step runs under `continue-on-error`, so the job itself still succeeds). One terminal `if: always()` step owns the completion for every outcome rather than a step per outcome, because a per-outcome step can only report a case it anticipated: an unguarded failure elsewhere (the checkout, say) would leave the required check `in_progress` forever and strand the PR. Its branches, in order: CI non-success (`conclusion != 'success'`, cancelled included, reports `conclusion=failure`, never a silent success), an unexpected `job.status == 'failure'` above it (reports `conclusion=failure`), `job.status == 'cancelled'` (reports `conclusion=cancelled`, since a superseded run never consulted the gate and falling through to the final `else` would blame it for a verdict that was never produced), CI success with an undownloadable artifact (reports `conclusion=failure`: the scan did not happen), CI success with an artifact carrying only the no-coverage marker (reports `conclusion=success` only when the baseline commit's own SonarCloud check was `success`, `none` (no earlier scanned commit), or `neutral`; `conclusion=failure` for an actual baseline gate failure, a literal `cancelled` baseline verdict, or an `unknown` baseline verdict; see the dedicated entry below), CI success with real coverage but no `SONAR_TOKEN` (reports `conclusion=neutral`), CI success with a `pull_request` run whose head commit has no resolvable open PR even after retries (reports `conclusion=failure`, not `neutral`, since a `neutral` check satisfies a required status check and would pass the gate with no scan having run), and CI success with everything resolved (the scan ran under `continue-on-error`, so its `outcome` decides). Each intermediate step is gated on its immediate predecessor's result (`steps.artifact.outcome == 'success'`, then `steps.coverage.outputs.present == 'true'`, then `steps.check-token.outputs.available == 'true'`) rather than re-stating the whole chain, since a skipped step's `outcome` is never `success`. The PR is resolved from `workflow_run.head_sha` via `gh api repos/{repo}/commits/{sha}/pulls`, not `workflow_run.pull_requests`, which GitHub leaves empty for fork PRs; a commit with no open PR (a direct push to `main`, or a `workflow_dispatch` of CI with no PR context) falls back to `-Dsonar.branch.name={head_branch}` instead of the PR-keyed `sonar.pullrequest.*` args, since `push` to `main` is a trigger path with no PR to decorate. **That lookup is gated on `workflow_run.event == 'pull_request'`**, because `commits/{sha}/pulls` is documented to return the MERGED pull request a commit arrived in once that commit is on the default branch: run unconditionally, every post-merge push to `main` resolved the PR that had just been merged and was analyzed with `sonar.pullrequest.*` parameters, decorating a closed PR instead of updating `main`'s own branch analysis (CodeRabbit, PR #1218). Among the pull requests the lookup does return, only an OPEN one is eligible, preferring the one whose own head is this exact commit and falling back to the first open one so a force-push landing between CI finishing and the scan starting does not silently demote a PR to branch analysis. A lookup FAILURE is a hard error rather than an empty result: the original `|| echo '[]'` turned a transient GitHub outage into a spurious `sonar.branch.name` analysis that registers a PR branch as a long-lived branch in SonarCloud, the masking failure mode `.claude/rules/bash-patterns.md` already documents. Two scanner properties are pinned on the command line rather than left to defaults, both because `workflow_run` changes what the defaults mean. `SONAR_HOST_URL` matters for secrets: the scanner reads `sonar-project.properties` out of the workspace, which on a fork PR is fork-authored content, so a `sonar.host.url` planted there would redirect the authenticated analysis upload and hand `SONAR_TOKEN` to that host (the same exposure existed under `pull_request_target`, which also checked out the PR head; it is closed here because this change rewrote the file anyway). The remaining fork-controlled properties in that file (`sonar.projectKey`, `sonar.organization`, the exclusion lists) are closed the same way: a `Fetch trusted sonar-project.properties for fork PRs` step, gated on `head_repository.full_name != github.repository`, overwrites the checked-out copy with the base branch's own version via the contents API before the scan step runs, the way `ci.yml` fetches the detector from base rather than trusting a fork's copy. `sonar.scm.revision` matters for correctness: the scanner's GitHub Actions auto-detection derives it from `GITHUB_SHA`, which under `workflow_run` is the DEFAULT BRANCH tip rather than the commit being analyzed. Concurrency keeps the pre-`workflow_run` push exemption, re-expressed as `github.event.workflow_run.event != 'push'` since `github.event_name` is now always `workflow_run`: two merges landing on `main` in quick succession can have their CI runs finish out of order, and cancelling the newer commit's scan because the older one's `workflow_run` arrived late would leave `main` analyzed at the older tree and report a non-success check for a commit that was never scanned. The group key is `workflow_run.head_repository.full_name` plus `head_branch`, not the branch alone: `head_branch` is a bare branch name with no owner in it, so a fork PR from `someone/sova:main` shared a group with this repository's own `main` push, and since every non-push run sets `cancel-in-progress`, that fork's scan would cancel the push's, reporting the required check `cancelled` (or leaving it uncreated when the cancellation lands before the first step) and blocking the PR (CodeRabbit, PR #1218). Scope boundary: no install/build/test step belongs in `sonarcloud.yml` after this change; it only ever consumes CI's artifact and reports a check. The checkout is itself gated on `steps.coverage.outputs.present == 'true'` rather than running unconditionally on CI success, and passes `clean: false` since the coverage artifact was already downloaded into the same workspace directory beforehand (checkout's default `git clean -ffdx` would otherwise delete it); this also means the documentation-only path, which never reads the checked-out tree, skips the full-history clone entirely. The terminal reporter also no longer gates on `steps.check.outputs.id != ''`: it runs unconditionally (`if: always()`) and, when the pending check's own 3 retries were exhausted and left no check ID, POSTs a brand-new already-completed check run instead of leaving the required check absent. One operational consequence of `workflow_run`: GitHub always resolves the workflow file from the default branch, so none of this wiring executes on the pull request that introduces it (the old `pull_request_target` version on `main` keeps providing the required check until merge), and its first real exercise is the first CI run after merge. The verdict is also posted as a `SonarCloud Analysis` commit status (pending in the first step, final in the reporter, before the check run is completed), because a check run created with `GITHUB_TOKEN` cannot choose its check suite: GitHub files it under the oldest GitHub Actions suite on the commit, which is PR Quality Gate's or Fork PR Safety Gate's depending on which won the race, and a later run of that same workflow on the same commit (a PR body edit re-fires PR Quality Gate, a reopen re-fires both) supersedes the suite. A superseded suite's checks drop out of the PR page and out of ruleset evaluation, so PR #1248 sat on "Expected: waiting for status" with a green check hidden underneath. A commit status has no suite to be superseded and is attributed to the GitHub Actions app that posted it, the source the ruleset pins. Statuses have no neutral or cancelled state, so neutral (no `SONAR_TOKEN`) maps to success, preserving its old non-blocking meaning, and cancelled maps to error. Files: `.github/workflows/sonarcloud.yml`, `.github/workflows/ci.yml`, `tests/test_ci_docs_only.py`, `tests/test_observability_config.py`. Issue #1187.

- **A docs-only skip must be re-justified against SonarCloud's own verdict, not CI's**: the first cut of the `workflow_run` rewiring above let the `COVERAGE_PRESENT != 'true'` branch report `conclusion=success` unconditionally, reusing `ci.yml`'s own docs-only baseline (the last successful run of `ci.yml` itself, independent of whether its SonarCloud scan passed). Concretely: push a code commit whose SonarCloud quality gate fails, then amend with a documentation-only commit and force-push, exactly what `/address-pr`'s cycle and a stale-branch rebase both do. The second CI run's detector finds the first run's tree as baseline, sees only markdown differs, emits `code=false`, and `python-test` writes the `no-coverage.marker`; this job then hit the marker branch and reported the required check green with the scanner never consulted about the still-failing gate. A `Resolve baseline verdict for docs-only skip` step (gated on `steps.coverage.outputs.present == 'false'`) looks up the most recent earlier successful `ci.yml` run on this branch, similarly to how `detect-code-changes.sh` does (`gh api --method GET repos/{repo}/actions/workflows/ci.yml/runs` filtered on `branch`/`status=success`/`event`, excluding the current head sha; not byte-identical to the detector's own lookup, which uses `per_page=1` with no sha exclusion since the detector never needs to exclude its own still-running commit), and reads that commit's own `"SonarCloud Analysis"` check via `commits/{sha}/check-runs`. `gh api` switches from GET to POST as soon as any `-f`/`-F` parameter is supplied, so `--method GET` has to be explicit; an earlier version omitted it and also passed `--jq --arg` (not valid `gh api` syntax, the flag takes exactly one expression), so the lookup silently 404'd on every run, the step always wrote `verdict=none`, and the whole guard was inert until review caught it (PR #1218 review, 6 findings). The verdict is three-valued, not two: `none` (no earlier scanned commit on this same head repository: the lookup also filters out a candidate from a different fork sharing this branch's bare name) and a resolved baseline commit's actual `conclusion` when its `status` is `completed`; `unknown` when a `gh api` call fails, the resolved baseline check is still `in_progress`, or every retained candidate was `cancelled`/superseded, since none of those prove anything about the gate and must fail closed rather than read as "no baseline". A cancelled or superseded candidate does not stop the lookup: it walks to the next-oldest successful ci.yml run on this branch instead of reading "never scanned" as a pass (PR #1187 review), checking `commits/{sha}/check-runs` filtered to `check_name=SonarCloud Analysis` and selecting the newest by `started_at` rather than assuming page-1 return order. The terminal reporter's marker branch mirrors that: `none`, `success`, and `neutral` (the baseline run's own `SONAR_TOKEN` was missing, not a real gate failure) report `conclusion=success`; `unknown`, a literal `cancelled` verdict (every candidate the lookup tried was cancelled), and an actual gate failure (`failure`/`timed_out`/`action_required`) all report `conclusion=failure`. `tests/test_ci_docs_only.py::TestSonarCloudWorkflow::test_baseline_verdict_resolution` executes the step itself against a stubbed `gh` for each of these cases (not just the terminal reporter's branches), and `test_docs_only_skip_is_justified_by_a_green_sonarcloud_run` and `test_reporter_outcome_matrix` pin the reporter's mapping.
- **The branch-analysis fallback is gated on the event, not on an empty PR number, and an unresolved PR fails closed**: `steps.pr.outputs.number` is empty both when the step never ran (a `push`, matching the comment's intent) and when a `pull_request` run's `commits/{sha}/pulls` lookup legitimately found no open PR (closed/merged between CI finishing and the scan starting, or the endpoint not yet indexing a just-opened PR). The original code treated both the same, so the second case ran `-Dsonar.branch.name={head_branch}`, registering a PR's own feature branch as a long-lived branch in SonarCloud, evaluated against the main-branch gate with no PR decoration, exactly the pollution the lookup's own hard-error-on-failure exists to prevent, reached through a different door. The PR-resolution step itself retries up to 3 times (short fixed backoff) when the lookup resolves to no PR, since a just-opened PR can lag the `commits/{sha}/pulls` index. A `Guard against unresolvable PR context` step (`id: pr-guard`) checks `github.event.workflow_run.event == 'pull_request' && steps.pr.outputs.number == ''` explicitly and sets `resolvable=false`; the `SonarCloud Scan` step's `if:` now requires `steps.pr-guard.outputs.resolvable == 'true'` too. The terminal reporter reports `conclusion=failure` ("PR could not be resolved"), not `neutral`: GitHub treats a `neutral` check run as satisfying a required status check, so reporting `neutral` here would pass the gate with the scan never having run at all (PR #1218 review); a closed/merged PR, the other case this guard catches, is unaffected by a failing check on an old head.
- **Both Checks API calls are retried**: `Create pending check run`'s `POST` and `Report check result`'s `PATCH` are the only two calls that actually deliver the "required check is never left pending" guarantee the rest of the job's step ordering is built around, and neither had error recovery: a transient GitHub API failure on either one stranded the PR (an empty `steps.check.outputs.id` skips the `always()` reporter too; a failed `PATCH` leaves the check `in_progress` forever). Both now retry up to 3 times with a linear backoff (`sleep $((attempt * 5))`) before failing the job outright.
- **The pending check run carries `details_url` and `external_id`**: without a `details_url`, the required check's "Details" link had nowhere to go (GitHub's automatic job-status check, which this explicit one replaces, normally supplies it). Both are set on the initial `POST` (`details_url` to this run's own Actions page, `external_id` to the triggering `workflow_run.id`) so every conclusion the reporter writes, including the failure paths this round of fixes added, is one click from the logs.
- **`Resolve PR for this run's head commit` is also gated on `workflow_run.conclusion == 'success'`**: previously it ran on every `pull_request`-event run regardless of whether the triggering CI run actually succeeded, matching neither the checkout nor the artifact-download steps that follow it, both of which are gated that way already. On a failed or cancelled CI run, the lookup was pure waste (its result is discarded by every downstream step's own `conclusion == 'success'` guard) and, because the step hard-errors on a `gh api` failure, a transient outage there could set `job.status=failure` and make the terminal reporter blame "a step in this workflow failed" for a run whose real story was "the triggering CI run was cancelled". The reporter's own `CI_CONCLUSION != 'success'` branch fires first regardless, so this was a diagnosability issue, not a wrong verdict, but the condition now matches its neighbors.

- **`/integrate-pr` must not push on its own account; knowledge capture belongs to `/address-pr`**: the second half of the same CI-minute leak was structural, not a CI-config problem. `/address-pr` wrote its cookbook entry in its LAST step, long after the squash-rebase-push in steps 8 through 10, so those edits were never committed on the branch; they sat dirty in the worktree until `/integrate-pr` Phase 3 swept them up with `git add -A` and force-pushed an amend onto an otherwise-ready PR. Ordering was the root cause, and the fix is ordering: the capture step now runs BEFORE the squash (step 8 of 17, renumbering the rest), so knowledge and doc-count corrections ride the push that branch makes anyway and cost nothing. `/integrate-pr` Phase 2's Update subroutine (rebase for a real conflict or a `BEHIND` branch) runs the same documentation check inline, immediately before its own push, so a fix found there also rides for free; the first version of this fix put the check in Phase 3 instead, which runs strictly after Phase 2's push has already gone out, so amending there only rewrote the local commit and silently never reached the remote PR head Phase 5 merges (caught in review before merge, not in production). Phase 3 now only runs at all when Phase 2 did not push, and is verify-only: when it finds something missing, it queues the content to `.claude/agent-control/pending-docs.md` in the **primary checkout** instead of amending, resolving that path explicitly via `git rev-parse --git-common-dir` rather than assuming the current directory is it (worktree isolation means it usually isn't), and the next `/address-pr` run drains the queue the same way before its own squash. The autonomous path mirrors this only when it runs at all: `AddressReviewStep` checks the pending-docs queue even when the reviewer found zero findings, so a queue left by `/integrate-pr` does not sit stranded on a PR whose only remaining review action is a clean pass; `_format_findings_prompt()` renders a docs-only prompt in that case. This does not fully close the gap, though: `AddressReviewStep` only runs at all inside the address-review pipeline, which the reviewer's revise/block verdict triggers, so a queue deferred on a PR whose reviewer approves cleanly first still has no autonomous drain point and needs a human or a subsequent `/address-pr` run to clear it. `ExtractMemoryStep` needs no reordering despite sitting after `PushStep`: it writes to the `Memory` table, never to the worktree. Phase 2 no longer rebases unconditionally either. `strict_required_status_checks_policy: true` means a `BEHIND` branch cannot merge no matter how green its existing checks are, so Phase 2 checks `mergeStateStatus` as well as `mergeable`: a real conflict (`CONFLICTING`) or a clean-but-stale branch (`BEHIND`) both justify running the update subroutine, which rebases, resolves only bounded and unambiguous conflicts (5 commits, 3 attempts each), and aborts to a `/address-pr` handoff on anything semantic. A `MERGEABLE`/not-`BEHIND` branch still gets no push at all. Mirrored across `.claude/commands/` and `sova/commands/data/commands/` for both commands, byte-identical, since CodeRabbit reviews only `sova/commands/data/commands/`. Files: `.claude/commands/{address-pr,integrate-pr}.md`, `sova/commands/data/commands/{address-pr,integrate-pr}.md`, `sova/core/steps/address_review.py`. Issue #1005.

- **Chat-style activity cockpit**: the activity feed renders as a chat timeline in a right-side panel opened by a floating owl button (`.feed-fab`, bottom-right) instead of the old sidebar bell. Backend: `FeedService` (global singleton, `feed_service.py`) keeps its in-memory ring buffer + SSE fan-out for live events and additionally persists each event to the `feed_events` table (`FeedEventRecord`, migration 032) so history survives reloads and restarts. `emit()` stays synchronous; `_schedule_persist()` writes the row as a non-blocking background task (no-op when no event loop, e.g. sync CLI), capturing `project_dir` from the contextvar at emit time. **The in-memory `FeedEvent.id` and the persisted `FeedEventRecord.id` share one id space**: `_persist()` writes the event's id as the primary key, and `init_counter()` (called at dashboard startup, per project in multi-project mode) advances the counter past the DB max so a process restart does not restart ids at 1 and collide with persisted rows (which would corrupt client-side dedup, ordering, and the pagination cursor). `history_page(before_id, limit)` paginates backward (oldest-first, exclusive cursor, fetch limit+1 to detect `has_more`); `prune_old_events(retention_days)` runs at startup. Router (`routers/feed.py`): `/feed/stream` (SSE), `/feed/history` (dual mode: `before_id` = DB backward pagination for infinite scroll, `since_id` = buffer gap-fill with DB backfill), `/feed/briefing` (aggregates awareness providers via `BriefingService`, always returns an empty briefing rather than 500 when awareness is disabled/unconfigured/failing). Frontend (`app.js`): SSE appends live events to the tail; first panel-open bootstraps history + briefing; scrolling near the top auto-loads older pages; a disabled "Ask SOVA..." input bar teases future conversational interaction. Config: `FeedConfig` (`[feed]` in sova.toml: `retention_days` default 30, `page_size` default 50), triple-registered (models, loader `_NESTED_SECTIONS`, settings_meta). Owl mascot asset at `static/owl-avatar.png` (cropped to a circle in CSS). Files: `sova/dashboard/services/feed_service.py`, `sova/dashboard/routers/feed.py`, `sova/db/models.py:FeedEventRecord`, `sova/dashboard/static/app.js`, `sova/dashboard/static/style.css`, `sova/dashboard/templates/base.html`. Issue #852.

- **Confidence scoring is advisory unless gated**: `ConfidenceScoreStep` runs after `monitor_ci` in the developer pipeline (opt-in via `confidence.enabled`, default false) and invokes an LLM with a truncated, priority-ordered PR diff (high-risk source files first, tests/docs last, under a fixed character budget) to produce a 0-100 deployment risk score with reasoning. The step degrades gracefully on any failure (LLM error, budget exhaustion, unparseable or non-integer score, failed `git diff`) by reporting "scoring skipped" and never blocking the pipeline; a failed diff command fails closed rather than defaulting to a full-confidence score. The score is stored on `ExecutionContext.confidence_score`, persisted to `TaskRun.assessment_json` for dashboard display, and written into the PR body as a `## Confidence Score` section via `upsert_section()` (`sova/utils/markdown.py`), which sanitizes LLM prose before insertion (escapes `## ` headings, backtick-wraps `#123`-style references so the score's text can never accidentally close an issue on merge). The score only affects routing when `confidence.gate_enabled=true` is also set: `HandoffToReviewerStep` drops the reviewer to manual handoff when the score is below `critical_threshold` or `review_threshold`, and also forces manual handoff (adding an "Integrate PR" fast-path action instead of auto-executing) when the score is at or above `auto_merge_threshold`, since auto-spawning a reviewer on an auto-merge-tier score would race the dashboard action. Thresholds are validated to satisfy `critical <= review <= auto_merge`. Files: `sova/core/steps/confidence_score.py`, `sova/core/steps/handoff_to_reviewer.py`, `sova/utils/markdown.py`, `sova/git/pr.py`, `sova/config/models.py`. Issue #321.
- **Step pipelines are config-overridable, with two read-only consumers still assuming built-ins**: `PipelineDefinitions` (`[pipelines]` in `sova.toml`: `developer`, `address_review`, `researcher` ordered step-name lists) lets a project customize its pipelines without code changes. `STEP_REGISTRY` in `sova/core/steps/__init__.py` maps every step name to its class; `build_pipeline(names)` instantiates a pipeline from a name list, raising `ValueError` (listing available steps) for an unknown name and a second, independent `ValueError` (listing the offenders) for a duplicated name, since "every name is known" does not imply "every name appears once" and the spec calls out duplicate rejection as its own edge case. `validate_pipeline(names)` checks ordering constraints (`create_worktree` before `develop`, `commit` before `push`, `push` before `create_pr`) and returns non-fatal warnings, never raises. `build_configured_pipeline(default_names, configured_names)` falls back to the built-in default when the config list is empty, otherwise validates and builds the configured list. `DeveloperRole`/`ResearcherRole` call it inside `execute()` (not `get_steps()`, which still returns the hardcoded default for callers without an `ExecutionContext`, e.g. tests) before transitioning the issue to `IN_PROGRESS`, so a config typo surfaces as a failed `RoleResult` rather than a crash with the issue already moved. `PlannerRole` is intentionally excluded: it does not run a step pipeline. Neither `build_pipeline()` nor `validate_pipeline()` models implicit step dependencies (e.g. dropping `create_worktree` while keeping `develop`, which needs a worktree); such a config fails at runtime inside the first downstream step that needs the missing state, not at config-load time. On the dashboard read side, `agent_progress.py:_resolve_pipelines()` and `work_service.py:_resolve_step_positions()` both resolve overrides via `asyncio.to_thread(load_config, project_dir)` (a 60s TTL cache per project, since `load_config()` is a blocking TOML + DB read too expensive for a per-agent polling path) so the step-progress bar and the Kanban board honour `[pipelines]`; `work_service.py`'s module-level `_STEP_POSITIONS` table (built-in only) is now purely the fallback used when no `project_dir` is known. Two consumers remain built-in-only by design (documented trade-off, not a bug): `work_service.py`'s other call sites that lack project context at their call sites, and the client-side `PIPELINE_STEPS` arrays in `app.js`. A custom pipeline that reorders or removes steps will show a mismatched step bar in those two paths; acceptable because pipeline customization is expected to be rare. `test_pipeline_definitions_defaults_match_builtin_pipelines` (`tests/test_config.py`) enforces that `PipelineDefinitions`'s defaults stay in sync with `get_developer_steps()`/etc., so a new built-in step added without a matching config default fails CI instead of silently diverging. Files: `sova/config/models.py`, `sova/core/steps/__init__.py`, `sova/roles/developer.py`, `sova/roles/researcher.py`, `sova/roles/base.py`, `sova/dashboard/services/agent_progress.py`, `sova/dashboard/services/work_service.py`, `sova/dashboard/settings_meta.py`. Issue #322, PR #1003.
- **A new `ComputedPRState` fans out across four independent maps; missing one fails open to the wrong label**: `compute_pr_state()` in `pr_service.py` short-circuits to `ComputedPRState.CONFLICTED` whenever `mergeable == "CONFLICTING"`, ahead of the CI/review checks, so this state can now appear anywhere those checks used to win (a conflicting-and-approved or conflicting-and-CI-failed PR both compute to `CONFLICTED`). Surfacing a `ComputedPRState` value correctly requires updating every translation table that mirrors it: `work_state.py`'s `WorkItemState.PR_CONFLICTED` (dashboard label/color/sort-order/primary-action, wired to a `Rebase` button that calls `POST /work/issue/{issue}/rebase` in `routers/work.py`, itself a thin wrapper around the pre-existing `attempt_auto_rebase()` supervisor helper that `SPAWN_REBASE` already used autonomously), `dependencies.py`'s `_PR_STATE_MAP`/`_PR_STATE_ACTIONS` (dependency-graph node color and action, also wired to the rebase endpoint via a `{"type": "api", "url": "/work/issue/{issue}/rebase"}` action whose `{issue}` placeholder `DependencyGraph.to_dict()` substitutes per node, not to `address-pr`: that command addresses review findings and CI failures via `rearrange-commits`/reply/resolve-thread flows, it never resolves merge conflicts), and the two independent JS color tables (`app.js`'s `PR_STATE_COLORS`, `supervisor.html`'s `prStateColor()`). The `dependencies.py` map is easy to miss because it fails open rather than erroring: `_PR_STATE_MAP.get(computed, "awaiting_review")` silently relabels an unmapped state as `awaiting_review`, which also flips its graph action to `[]` (the `_PR_STATE_ACTIONS["awaiting_review"]` value), erasing whatever action a CI-failed-and-conflicting PR would otherwise have shown. `PR_CONFLICTED` is never verdict-overrideable (absent from `_VERDICT_OVERRIDEABLE` in `work_state.py`): a conflicted PR is never integrate-eligible regardless of SOVA's review verdict. The agents-page Rebase button (`triggerRebase()` in `agents.html`) guards re-entry with a `_rebaseInFlight` Set plus disabling the clicked button for the duration of the request, since `attempt_auto_rebase()` is long-running and manages its own worktree; a double-click must not start two worktree creations. When adding a new `ComputedPRState`, grep for every existing entry's key string across all four files before considering the change complete. `POST /work/issue/{issue_number}/rebase` in `routers/work.py` does not use the `require_same_origin_csrf` dependency documented under "Dashboard access control" below. When that note was written the guard had zero callers (`issue_csrf_cookie()` was never invoked), so wiring it onto only this one endpoint would have made every rebase request fail closed with 403. Issue #1120 changed that: the Connections page now issues the cookie and guards its own state-changing routes plus `POST /settings/config` and `POST /settings/config/migrate-secret`. The rebase endpoint is still unguarded, as is every other state-changing route whose page does not issue the cookie (including the pre-existing `mark_failed` in the same router); extending the guard to one of them means also calling `issue_csrf_cookie()` on the page route that drives it and sending `csrfHeaders()` from that page's JS, in the same change. Files: `sova/dashboard/services/pr_service.py`, `sova/dashboard/services/work_state.py`, `sova/dashboard/routers/work.py`, `sova/dashboard/routers/dependencies.py`, `sova/supervisor/dependency_graph.py`, `sova/dashboard/static/app.js`, `sova/dashboard/templates/agents.html`, `sova/dashboard/templates/supervisor.html`. Issue #990.

- **Dashboard access control: single-user loopback model, no accounts**: the dashboard has no user accounts, sessions, or login page, and none are planned. Access to the machine (or to whatever host it is bound to) implies access to the dashboard; this is the deployment model, not a gap. The threat this closes is narrower: any web page the operator's browser visits can issue cross-origin `fetch()` calls at the local dashboard port, and DNS rebinding makes same-origin policy alone incomplete (a rebound page's `Origin` header still reflects the address it was loaded from). `sova/dashboard/security.py` provides `require_same_origin_csrf`, a FastAPI dependency for state-changing routes only (never `GET`/status endpoints), composing two independent checks: (1) `validate_origin()` matches the request's `Origin`, falling back to `Referer`, against `build_allowed_origins(host, port)` (all loopback hostname spellings for a loopback bind, else the configured host only): both headers absent is a rejection, since the dashboard's own JS always sends one; (2) a double-submit CSRF cookie/header pair (`issue_csrf_cookie(response, request)` sets a `sova_csrf` cookie the dashboard's own JS echoes back as `X-CSRF-Token`, compared via `hmac.compare_digest`; `HttpOnly` stays off because that readability *is* the double-submit pattern, the defense resting on a cross-origin page being unable to read a cookie scoped to another origin rather than on hiding it from same-origin script, and `Secure` is derived from `request.url.scheme` rather than forced on, since the default bind is loopback HTTP and a browser never returns a `Secure` cookie over plain HTTP, so forcing it would leave the cookie permanently absent and every guarded request rejected; `request.url.scheme` already reflects `X-Forwarded-Proto` under uvicorn `--proxy-headers`, so an HTTPS deployment behind a proxy gets the flag without this function trusting a client-supplied header itself. Both flags carry a `NOSONAR` marker for the SonarCloud hotspots they raise). `create_app()` in `sova/dashboard/app.py` takes `host`/`port` kwargs (falling back to `SOVA_DASHBOARD_HOST`/`SOVA_DASHBOARD_PORT` env vars, then `dashboard.port` config, mirroring the existing `SOVA_DASHBOARD_PROJECT` pattern for the `--reload` uvicorn factory-string path that cannot receive direct kwargs) and computes `app.state.is_loopback_bind`, `app.state.allowed_origins`, and `app.state.csrf_fail_closed` once at app-creation time rather than per-request. When bound to a non-loopback host with no `dashboard.csrf_secret` configured, `csrf_fail_closed=True` and `require_same_origin_csrf` rejects every dependent request unconditionally (fail closed) rather than physically omitting routes from the router, logging `dashboard.nonloopback_no_secret` at startup. A wildcard bind (`0.0.0.0`, `::`, detected via `is_wildcard_host()`'s `ipaddress.is_unspecified` check) fails closed unconditionally, even with a secret configured: the browser's real Origin carries the server's actual LAN IP or hostname, never the literal wildcard, so `build_allowed_origins()` can never construct an entry a real remote request would match, and letting a secret "unlock" it would silently reject every legitimate caller while looking configured. Both host-binding entry points (`sova dashboard --host` in `sova/cli/app.py`, `sova server start --host` in `sova/cli/commands/server.py` via `SOVAServer.create_app()` in `sova/scheduler/server.py`) thread `host`/`port` through. `csrf_secret` is an opt-in switch, not a credential: only its presence is checked, never its value, and neither check authenticates a caller (both stop a *browser* driven cross-origin request; a direct client sets Origin and both halves of the double-submit pair itself), so a non-loopback bind stays reachable by anyone who can reach the port. Giving the secret real force means signing the token with it via the HMAC generate/validate pair in `sova/dashboard/services/mcp_service.py` (the precedent issue #931 names) and giving the operator a way to supply it. `is_loopback_host()` classifies via `ipaddress.is_loopback`, not a literal name set, so all of 127.0.0.0/8 and a bracketed `[::1]` are recognized; `build_allowed_origins()` adds the configured host to the canonical loopback spellings so a `--host 127.0.0.2` bind can still match its own Origin. No auth router consumed the guard at the time this was written. Issue #1120 wired the first consumers: `issue_csrf_cookie()` is now called on the `/settings`, `/connections` and `/supervisor` page routes (both the single-project and `/p/{slug}/` variants), and `require_same_origin_csrf` guards the Connections page's state-changing and probe routes in `routers/auth.py` plus the pre-existing `POST /settings/config` and `POST /settings/config/migrate-secret`. The read-only `GET /api/auth/status` stays uncovered (state-changing routes only). The coupling this creates is the thing to remember: a guarded route is only reachable from a page whose own GET route issues the cookie *and* whose JS echoes it back via `csrfHeaders()` in `static/app.js`. Guarding a shared endpoint therefore breaks every *other* page that posts to it until both halves are added there too, which is exactly how the supervisor page's enable/disable toggle started returning 403 when `POST /settings/config` was guarded. Grep for an endpoint's path across `templates/` before adding the dependency to it. The two probe routes (`POST /connections/llm/validate`, `POST /connections/runtime/validate`) are guarded even though they persist nothing: they issue an outbound `GET {api_base}/api/tags` against a caller-supplied host and spawn provider CLI subprocesses, so an unguarded version would be a reachability oracle rather than a read. Files: `sova/dashboard/security.py`, `sova/dashboard/app.py`, `sova/cli/app.py`, `sova/cli/commands/server.py`, `sova/scheduler/server.py`, `sova/config/models.py`. Issue #931.
- **A dependency applied once at router level, self-exempting safe methods, covers a mutating route by construction; a per-route opt-in is exactly how most of them went unguarded**: issue #1204 superseded the per-route opt-in above. A per-route `Depends()` is exactly how most of the dashboard's mutating routes went unguarded (71 routes were missed), so `require_same_origin_csrf` now self-exempts safe methods (`GET`/`HEAD`/`OPTIONS`, returning immediately before the fail-closed/origin/token checks) and is registered once, at router level, in `_register_api_routers()` (`csrf_dep = [Depends(require_same_origin_csrf)]` passed to every `include_router()` call), so a router is covered by construction regardless of how many routes it carries or gains later. `mcp.router` is the sole exemption from that call (its own bearer-token scheme already authenticates every call, and FastAPI cannot satisfy an `HTTP Request`-typed dependency for a `WebSocket` route in any case, which is why `agents.py`'s `/ws/agents/status` route was split onto its own un-guarded `ws_router`); `/api/telemetry/ingest` stays structurally exempt, registered by `_register_telemetry_router()` outside `_register_api_routers()` entirely. `fleet_manager.router` and the ad-hoc `/api/projects/{register,unregister,uninstall}` routes in `_setup_multi_project()` (not themselves inside `_register_api_routers()`) were individually given the same dependency in the same change, closing the same gap. `issue_csrf_cookie()` now runs on every page route (`_with_csrf_cookie()` wraps every `templates.TemplateResponse()`/`_project_page()` return in both single- and multi-project mode), not the three pages #1120 wired up, since the router-level guard means any page's JS can now reach a guarded endpoint. The dashboard's own `fetch()` calls needed a matching change: rather than hand-adding `csrfHeaders()` to the handful of call sites that predated router-level enforcement, `static/app.js` patches `window.fetch` once to attach `X-CSRF-Token` to every same-origin `POST`/`PUT`/`PATCH`/`DELETE` call whose caller did not already set it, so neither existing call sites nor ones added later need to opt in by hand.
